- 2026-10-18 **Discord (Angel): boot stops after "switching root -> /system/bin/init selinux_setup" with `traps: init[1] trap invalid opcode ip:7fb7bc1d6b46 ... in linker64[1d6b46,7fb7bc068000+17c000]`; no crash text, then nothing.**
  The offset 0x1d6b46 in `lapis/probe/system_a/system/bin/bootstrap/linker64` (same exec segment size 0x17c000, file offset 0x68000) is the **first VEX instruction of `__dl___linker_init`: `vxorps %xmm0,%xmm0,%xmm0` followed by `vmovups %ymm0`**.
  Baseline AVX never traps on a CPU that has it, and the kernel emulator only covers the newer extensions (GFNI, AVX-VNNI, SHA-NI, VAES-256, VPCLMUL, IFMA), so this CPU has **no AVX** (typical of Celeron/Pentium/Atom-class chips); init (PID 1) dies in its first
  instructions. Inferred from the trap site, **the reporter's CPU model is not known yet** (their `hw.txt` `== cpu` line has it). Not fixable with drivers: emulating all of AVX/AVX2 (every memcpy in Android uses it, plus ymm state in every context switch) is a different project.
  What was done: `hook.sh` now checks `/proc/cpuinfo` for `avx` before anything else and calls `fail` ("this CPU has no AVX...", drops to the tty1 shell; `exit` continues) unless `REQUIRE_AVX=0` in `gbos.cfg`; `hwdetect.txt` gets `== cpu` (model + avx/avx2/fma/bmi2/sha_ni/gfni/avx_vnni/avx512f yes/NO);
  README/website say AVX is required. The check is a stick/installed-system script (no kernel change). Tested only as a shell unit test with fake cpuinfo files. Not checked: CPUs with AVX but no AVX2 (Sandy/Ivy Bridge are pre-Skylake and outside the stated support anyway).
- 2026-10-18 **AMD GPU laptops (reporter: Ryzen 7 8840U / Radeon 780M, PCI 1002:1900, MT7922 Wi-Fi): software rendering ("GPU_SOFTWARE"), written from static analysis + QEMU, NEVER run on an AMD machine.**
  Problem: the reporter's `hw.txt` has no GPU driver (normal: the inventory is taken before hwdetect loads modules); the image itself has nothing that can draw on AMD. Evidence from Google's vendor image: `ro.hardware.egl=angle`, `ro.hardware.vulkan=intel`,
  `ro.hwui.use_vulkan=true`, `debug.renderengine.vulkan=true`; the only Vulkan HAL is `/vendor/lib64/hw/vulkan.intel.so` (ANV, sha256 5c687450...); buffers come from minigbm. Kernel side was already fine (`amdgpu.ko`, `mt7921e`, amdgpu firmware ship).
  What `hook.sh` does now (all after hwdetect, before the vendor build.prop rewrite): `gbos_gpu_decide` waits up to `GPU_WAIT` (25 s) for a real DRM driver to bind on a `/sys/class/drm/cardN` (simpledrm/vkms don't count),
  and turns **software GPU mode** on only if no Intel GPU is present (no `i915`/`xe` card bound **and** no Intel PCI display controller, so a failed i915 never triggers it) and some other driver is bound. `GPU_SOFTWARE=auto|1|0` in `gbos.cfg` (1 forces it, 0 never). In that mode, each hash-pinned bind-mount, skipped quietly when the file is missing (older sticks) or the original differs:
  1. `usb/patched/vulkan.swiftshader.so` over `/vendor/lib64/hw/vulkan.intel.so` (so `ro.hardware.vulkan=intel` is untouched). SwiftShader (commit cddb5c91) built for Android with the NDK by `tools/swiftshader-android/build.sh` (docker; patch `swiftshader-android.patch` makes its CMake take the Linux paths plus
     the Android glue of src/Android.bp: `HMI` hwvulkan module, `VkDeviceMemoryExternalAndroid.cpp` = AHardwareBuffer import, Android export map; same flags as AOSP's `vulkan.pastel`, which Cuttlefish uses with ANGLE). 24 MB, **not committed** (gitignored), shipped as release asset `gbos86-swr-r1.tar.xz` (tag v38, **to be created**), `build.sh` fetches it (non-fatal if missing).
     Scripted build from scratch reproduces the manual one bit for bit: sha256 `f44c067d0bc2a6746153a06a9bb9874deabd9beca279834d9f61a33a29d83ae7` (x86-64 baseline, -O3, c++_static, LLVM 10 JIT, no AVX needed). Traps met: OrbStack needs `docker build --platform` for an amd64 image to be runnable with `--platform` (fixed in the script);
     `-Wno-error` in CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS is overridden by SwiftShader's own `-Werror`, so `-DSWIFTSHADER_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS=OFF`; a clean build is ~40 min (`KEEP_BUILD=1` reuses the tree).
  2. `usb/patched/libminigbm_gralloc.amdgpu.so` over `/vendor/lib64/libminigbm_gralloc.so` (sha256 d4853695...; `tools/patch_gralloc_amdgpu.py`). **This, not `libgbm.so`, is Google's gralloc**: `gralloc.minigbm.so`, `mapper.minigbm.so` and the allocator service link `libminigbm_gralloc.so`, which has minigbm statically linked (`libgbm.so` is only used by the camera HALs and
     `android.hardware.media.c2-cros-codecs-service`). Its backend choice is a `strcmp` chain on the DRM driver name (`.text 0xc4c0`). Backends komeda, radeon, nouveau, vkms... are the identical generic linear dumb-buffer backend (same `init` 0xef30, `bo_create_with_modifiers` 0xf040), so the 7-byte literal "komeda" (ARM-only) becomes "amdgpu".
     Dumb buffers are allowed on render nodes (`DRM_RENDER_ALLOW` on `DRM_IOCTL_MODE_CREATE_DUMB` in our 6.18 `drm_ioctl.c`), and cros_gralloc scans `renderD128..` first, so it works without a card node (and without taking DRM master from drm_hwcomposer).
     Second edit in the same file: `cros_gralloc_driver::init` sets a "software renderer" flag when `ro.hardware.vulkan` contains `lvp` or `pastel` (Google's own hook for lavapipe/SwiftShader), and `lock()` refuses CPU access to buffers allocated without CPU usage unless that flag is set. A CPU Vulkan driver locks *every* buffer (`AHardwareBuffer_lockPlanes`),
     so the literal "pastel" is rewritten to "intel": the flag is set for `ro.hardware.vulkan=intel` and no `vulkan.pastel.so` overlay of the read-only /vendor/lib64/hw is needed.
  3. `usb/patched/libgbm.amdgpu.so` over `/vendor/lib64/libgbm.so` (sha256 64a0f253...; `tools/patch_libgbm_amdgpu.py`, the vkms backend answers to "amdgpu"): for the camera/codec HALs only, not essential.
  4. vendor build.prop gets `ro.surface_flinger.supports_background_blur=0` (blur on the CPU) and `ro.vendor.hwc.use_overlay_planes=false`: drm_hwcomposer picks its backend by DRM driver name (`i915`, `xe` -> "client", everything composited by SurfaceFlinger; any other name -> "generic", which also tries overlay planes). amdgpu gets "generic"; with overlays off it behaves like "client".
  A failed gralloc or Vulkan bind cancels the prop changes. Every step logs `hook: software rendering: ...`.
  Verified: `sh -n` on all scripts, busybox-ash and dash; `tools/test_gpu_select.sh` (19 checks: fake /sys and fake /new; Intel i915/xe/unbound-i915/hybrid never switch, AMD/nouveau do, forced/disabled, missing files, wrong originals, failing mounts); the HAL under QEMU TCG inside Google's own bionic userspace
  (`tools/swiftshader-android/qemu/run.py HAL.so`, bind-mounted over vulkan.intel.so like hook.sh does, Skylake-Client and EPYC-Genoa CPU models): HMI tag/id "vulkan", `open("vk0")`, Vulkan 1.3 CPU device "SwiftShader Device (LLVM 10.0.0)", `VK_ANDROID_native_buffer`, `VK_ANDROID_external_memory_android_hardware_buffer`, `VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd`, `VK_EXT_queue_family_foreign`,
  ycbcr conversion present (`VK_KHR_external_memory_fd` absent, as in AOSP's Android build), a queue submit (`vkCmdFillBuffer`) read back, and a SPIR-V compute pipeline (exercises the JIT) read back. **Not verified**: anything involving a real amdgpu or the whole Android graphics stack (ANGLE/SurfaceFlinger/HWC on SwiftShader), the patched gralloc (no DRM device in the emulator kernel), speed, the AMD CPU (Ryzen) side.
  Risks for the first boot on the Radeon laptop, in the order I'd expect trouble: (1) amdgpu may not bind (firmware/ASIC support/BIOS): look at `hwdetect.txt` `== gpu` (card: driver, then amdgpu/[drm] dmesg lines, `*ERROR*`) and `== missing firmware`; (2) drm_hwcomposer on amdgpu: the "generic" backend and its atomic/plane assumptions (COLOR_PIPELINE, LOG_GAMMA_LUT -> GAMMA_LUT fallback, modifiers) are untested: `drmhwc` lines in `logs/boot-N/logcat.txt`, and `dumpsys SurfaceFlinger` in `display.txt` (70 s after boot);
  (3) CPU rendering speed and first-boot time (SwiftShader JITs every shader at every boot, no cache): a 2.8K panel is a lot of pixels, set `RESOLUTION=` to something small; (4) brightness: the lights HAL knows `intel_backlight`/`backlight`/`backlight-lcd0`/`dp_aux_backlight`, not `amdgpu_blN`; (5) hardware video decode (cros-codecs via Intel VA-API `iHD_drv_video.so`) and the Intel NPU services cannot work on AMD;
  (6) the Ryzen CPU itself (AMD-specific MSRs/CPUID paths in the ISA-emulation kernel, `kvm_amd` for the Trusty VM) was never tried; (7) `LOAD_I915=1` makes stage0 `modprobe i915` on an AMD laptop (harmless: no device). Logs to ask for: `hw.txt`, `hwdetect.txt`, `stage0-dmesg.txt` (look for `hook: software rendering`), `kmsg.log` (kernel `#N`), `logcat.txt` (grep `gralloc|minigbm|drmhwc|vulkan|SwiftShader|surfaceflinger|angle`), `display.txt`.
- 2026-10-18 **HP ENVY 13-aq0xxx speakers: codec quirk `103c:85e2`, kernel #36 (NOT verified on hardware; QEMU can only prove it builds and boots).**
  Evidence (reporter's `hwdetect.txt` of kernel #33 + modules r3, card `sof-hda-dsp` now present): ALC285, vendor 0x10ec0285, subsystem 0x103c85e2; dmesg `ALC285: picked fixup  for PCI SSID 103c:0000` (only the HP *vendor wildcard*
  `ALC269_FIXUP_HP_MUTE_LED`, which acts only on a DMI OEM string, so effectively no fixup), then `autoconfig for ALC285: line_outs=1 (0x17) type:speaker, speaker_outs=0, hp_outs=1 (0x21)`, Mic=0x19.
  Pin defaults: 0x17 = 0x90170180 (fixed internal speaker), 0x14/0x16/0x12/0x18/0x1a/0x1b/0x1e = 0x411111f0 (not connected), 0x21 = HP jack, 0x19 = mic jack. The BIOS declares **one** speaker pin, so one pair
  of the four speakers gets signal ("thin, like cheeks"; the reporter says stock mainline sounds the same). `alc269.c` (6.18.21) has no quirk for 0x85e2.
  Chosen fix: reuse upstream's `alc285_fixup_hp_spectre_x360` (HP Spectre x360 15-df0xxx, 103c:8519, Elia Devito 2021, bugzilla 189331, "enable all four speakers"): pin 0x14 := 0x90170110 (internal fixed speaker, so the
  parser sees two speaker pins) and 0x17's connection list forced to DAC 0x02 (`SPEAKER2_TO_DAC1` effect: one stream, one volume for both pairs). That is the same pin situation (ALC285, only 0x17 declared, 0x14 not connected).
  `kernel/isa_emu_patch/alc269_hp_envy13_aq.sh` adds a one-line wrapper `ALC285_FIXUP_HP_ENVY13_AQ` (same function, chained to `ALC269_FIXUP_HP_MUTE_LED` so the vendor wildcard's behaviour is kept, because an exact-SSID quirk replaces the wildcard),
  `SND_PCI_QUIRK(0x103c, 0x85e2, "HP ENVY Laptop 13-aq0xxx", ...)` in SSID order after 0x85de, and the model name `alc285-hp-envy13-aq`. Only this SSID is affected.
  Not used: `ALC285_FIXUP_HP_ENVY_X360` (85de, Ryzen 13-ar): it overwrites pins 0x12/0x14/0x17/0x19 with B&O soundbar values and writes amp coefficients that are for a different board; the 13-ar thread still reports a too-weak amp. The Spectre x360 df1/eb1 variants also toggle a GPIO for a back-speaker amp
  (we have no evidence this board needs that), so they were not picked either.
  **Confidence about 55-60%.** Known unknowns: (1) whether pin 0x14 is the correct second pin on this board (the Spectre x360 precedent says 0x14 on the same codec/BIOS family; the 13-aq has no schematic available to us);
  (2) whether the second pair needs an amp enable (GPIO1, as in the Spectre x360 15-df1/eb1 fixups) or Realtek coefficients; if `speaker-test` drives a pair but it is silent, try that next;
  (3) the 2ch SOF topology is used (`sof-hda-generic-2ch.tplg`), so both pairs get the same stereo stream, no 4-channel routing; (4) overall volume may stay low (the 13-ar patch author blames a missing `_DSD` amp parameter).
  If still wrong, ask the reporter for: `hwdetect.txt` of a boot with kernel #36 (check `== sound codecs`: `autoconfig` should now say `line_outs=2 (0x14/0x17...)` or `speaker_outs`, and the log line should name the 85e2 fixup instead of `103c:0000`),
  `speaker-test -c 2 -t sine` and `-c 4` (does each pair play, are they swapped?), and from a working Linux `cat /proc/asound/card0/codec#0` (full, with the Node 0x14/0x17 `Connection:` and `Amp-Out vals`) plus `alsa-info`.
  Runtime model override via cmdline/hints is not available: `SND_HDA_RECONFIG`/`HWDEP` are off, so every variant needs a kernel rebuild (`model=alc285-hp-envy13-aq` / `alc285-hp-spectre-x360` exist as names but nothing passes them).
  Build: the codec driver is built in (`CONFIG_SND_HDA_CODEC_ALC269=y`), so this is a relink of kernel #35 -> **#36**, `usb/out/BOOTX64.EFI` is now #36 (the previous tracked file, #33, is kept untracked as `BOOTX64.EFI.33`). Only built-in code changed: `usb/modules` (6.18.21-gbos-whl+) stays valid.
  Test: `kernel/isa_emu_test/run_qemu.py` prints `ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS`.
- 2026-10-18 **ENVY 13-aq0xxx audio, second pass (PR #7 branch): quirk key corrected, kernel #37; audio HAL config now generated from the real card layout; NOT verified on hardware.**
  1. *Quirk key (verified in the 6.18.21 source, `sound/hda/common/auto_parser.c` `snd_hda_pick_fixup`).* `SND_PCI_QUIRK` entries are compared with `codec->bus->pci->subsystem_{vendor,device}`, i.e. the **PCI SSID of the audio controller (00:1f.3)**, whenever `bus->pci` is set; SOF sets it
     (`sound/soc/sof/intel/hda.c: hbus->pci = pci`), and the reporter's log line says `picked fixup  for PCI SSID 103c:0000` (type "PCI SSID", so `bus->pci` is non-NULL on SOF). The codec SSID (`103c:85e2`, from `== sound codecs`) is only consulted by `HDA_CODEC_QUIRK` entries
     (`match_codec_ssid`) or when `bus->pci` is NULL. Nothing in the logs shows the controller's own SSID, and the HP vendor wildcard (`SND_PCI_QUIRK_VENDOR(0x103c)`) matches any HP PCI SSID first, so the #36 entry would only have matched if the PCH audio SSID also happened to be 103c:85e2.
     Fix: `HDA_CODEC_QUIRK(0x103c, 0x85e2, ...)` (same fixup, chained to `ALC269_FIXUP_HP_MUTE_LED`), so it matches on the known codec SSID regardless of the bus. `kernel/isa_emu_patch/alc269_hp_envy13_aq.sh` also upgrades an already-patched tree in place.
     Kernel relinked as **#37** (`usb/out/BOOTX64.EFI`; the #36 file is kept untracked as `BOOTX64.EFI.36`); `run_qemu.py` prints `ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS`. Check on hardware: dmesg must say `ALC285: picked fixup  for codec SSID 103c:85e2` (not `PCI SSID 103c:0000`).
     Fixup choice re-checked, unchanged: with 0x14 := 0x90170110 the parser has two speaker pins (sorted by assoc<<4|seq: 0x14 = "Speaker", 0x17 = "Bass Speaker", `get_line_out_pfx`), both forced onto DAC 0x02, so one stream and one `Master Playback Volume`, and a `Bass Speaker Playback Switch` appears. The 2ch SOF topology is not a limit: SOF only moves a stereo stream to the HDA link; routing to the speaker pins is done inside the codec by the HDA driver.
     Still a guess: that 0x14 is the second pair on this board and that no amp enable (GPIO1 as in Spectre x360 15-df1/eb1, or `alc285-hp-amp-init` coefficients) is needed. ~55-60 %.
  2. *Audio HAL (`dras`).* Its config was written for legacy HDA and the generated copy only substituted card id/name, which is wrong for a SOF-HDA card. Facts from `sof-hda-generic-2ch.tplg` (parsed: PCM ids) and the reporter's log: playback+capture **pcm0 "HDA Analog"**, pcm31 deep buffer, **HDMI pcm3/4/5**, capture **pcm6 "DMIC"** (48 kHz, 2ch, S16/S24/S32) and **pcm7 "DMIC16kHz"**; input devices `sof-hda-dsp Headphone`, `sof-hda-dsp Mic`, `sof-hda-dsp HDMI/DP,pcm=3|4|5` (separate jacks, not the legacy combo `Headphone Mic`).
     What the generated config had wrong: HDMI2/3 on pcm 7/8 (7 is a capture-only DMIC PCM), JackDev `sof-hda-dsp Headphone Mic` (does not exist), and **InternalMic on pcm0, which on this laptop is the headset-jack ADC (pin 0x19); the internal mics are the two digital mics on pcm6** (this alone explains "microphone doesn't work").
     New `usb/audio/gen_config.sh` (called from `hook.sh` for any card id other than `PCH`; `PCH` still uses the shipped files untouched; falls back to the old substitution if the script is missing or fails) reads `/proc/asound/cards`, `/proc/asound/pcm` and `/proc/bus/input/devices`: Speaker/Headphone = first non-HDMI playback PCM, Mic = analog capture PCM, InternalMic = DMIC PCM if any else analog capture, HDMI1..3 = the HDMI playback PCMs (missing ones dropped, sections are optional), JackDev = the real input device names. It also adds `Bass Speaker Playback Switch` and `Mic`/`Headset Mic Capture Switch` unmute lines to `mixer_paths.xml` (audio_route skips absent controls).
     The generated file is copied to `logs/boot-N/audio_config.generated.xml`. Test: `sh tools/test_audio_config.sh` (fake /proc/asound for `PCH`, `sofhdadsp`, a legacy non-PCH card, a SOF combo-jack card, an unreadable one; checks well-formed XML and numbers; also passes under the stage0 busybox). Not verified on hardware: that dras accepts the generated file and opens pcm6 (its parser source is not available, only the sof-soundwire config as reference; JackSwitch for Mic is left at the default as in Google's file).
  3. *Microphone.* Kernel side looks complete on the log: `snd_soc_dmic` loaded, NHLT reports 2 DMICs, `sof-cnl.ri` + `sof-hda-generic-2ch.tplg` loaded. The only unknown is whether `pcm6` exists in `/proc/asound/pcm` (hwdetect did not list PCMs before); `hwdetect.txt` now has `== sound pcm`, `== sound inputs` and `== sound speaker nodes` (codec Node 0x02/0x03/0x14/0x17/0x21 incl. Connection and Amp-Out vals). The headset mic (0x19) is on pcm0. If DMIC capture is silent although the HAL records from pcm6, suspect the SOF DMIC gain/`Capture` controls or `IntrinsicSensitivity` (-2600 is Google's value, untuned here).
  Next from the reporter (boot with this stick, choose 1): `hwdetect.txt` (the new `== sound pcm/inputs/speaker nodes`, and the `picked fixup` dmesg line), `audio.txt` and `audio_config.generated.xml` from the same boot-N; on a working shell/Linux, `speaker-test -c 2 -t sine` and `arecord -D hw:0,6 -f S32_LE -c 2 -r 48000 -d 5 x.wav`.

- 2026-11-05 **Surface Laptop 7 full hardware support completed: IPU7 camera bridge, precision touchpad IDC, audio DSP/internal mic, and sleep/lid guard.**
  1. *Camera (OV02C10 on Intel IPU7):* Userspace bridge daemon (`kernel/surface-webcam.c`, `tools/build_surface_tools.sh`) routes the raw CSI-2 MIPI sensor to `/dev/video42` via `v4l2loopback`. Implemented on-demand power gating (hardware sensor only streams and privacy LED only illuminates while an app is capturing), dynamic resolution negotiation (`VIDIOC_G_FMT`), and 4:3 optical center cropping (resolves squished CameraX aspect ratio in Telegram and enables Camera 1 / Camera 2 compatibility).
  2. *Precision Touchpad IDC:* `Vendor_045e_Product_0c9f.idc` configured and installed for the virtual uinput multitouch device so Android registers it as an internal precision touchpad with smooth gestures and palm rejection.
  3. *Audio & Microphone:* Internal microphone mixer routes enabled in `mixer_paths_surface_laptop_7.xml` and `audio_config_surface_laptop_7.xml`. Added speaker DSP equalization presets in `usb/audio/dsp/`.
  4. *Sleep & Lid Guard:* `sleep-guard.sh` prevents unhandled s2idle sleep freezes when closing the lid, restores display backlight on reopen, and dynamically optimizes CPU frequencies and Intel P-state / EPP power profiles.
  5. *Kernel panic prevention:* Speakup synth probing without serial hardware caused kernel panics on boot; disabled via `sys/accessibility/speakup/synth none`.
- 2026-10-18 **Surface Laptop 7 (Intel, Core Ultra 7 266V / Lunar Lake; Surface Laptop for Business 7th Edition): boots and works; kernel config + two module patches + usb changes (PR from the reporter).**
  Verified on the hardware: Wi-Fi, internal keyboard, battery/AC, speakers, touchpad with gestures and physical click, stable display, Bluetooth adapter present; touchscreen and suspend/resume were tested by hand afterwards and work with the kernel and files of this entry (nothing extra was needed). Not working / not tried: internal microphone, HDMI audio, camera. What was wrong, in the order it was found:
  1. *Wi-Fi.* BE201 (PCI 8086:a840) logs `No config found for PCI dev a840` (-22) without `CONFIG_IWLMLD`: this chip only has an entry in the MLD op mode's device table. Firmware `iwlwifi-bz-b0-fm-c0-*.ucode` was already shipped.
  2. *Keyboard, battery, fan (Surface Aggregator Module, SAM).* Three separate blockers: (a) no ACPI match: `ssam_node_group_sl7` exists in mainline but is only referenced from the device-tree table, so `MSHW0551` is added (`surface_sl7_sam.sh`); (b) `SERIAL_8250_RUNTIME_UARTS=0` in Google's config makes `serial8250_init()` return -ENODEV, `dw-apb-uart` fails with `probe ... failed with error -22` and the SAM UART never exists (now 4); (c) the SAM wakeup IRQ is a GPIO of the Lunar Lake pin controller: `surface_serial_hub: error -EINVAL: failed to setup IRQ` until `CONFIG_PINCTRL_INTEL_PLATFORM` (ACPI `INTC105F`) is on. All symbols are in `configure_whl.py --generic`.
  3. *README step 5.* A kernel built from the listed patches stops after `init: Loading SELinux policy`: `debug_selinux.sh` (`gbos_permissive`) was missing from the list. Fixed.
  4. *Sound (RT721 jack codec, RT1320 amp, SoundWire, SDCA function topologies).* (a) the codecs were not autoloaded by the coldplug (cause not found, the modalias is there): hwdetect loads them by name; (b) the BIOS declares two RT1320, the machine has one: the ghost peripheral broke `sof-sdw` (`refcount_t: saturated` / `underflow; use-after-free` warnings, because `asoc_sdw_rt_amp_exit()` did not clear `amp_dev1/2` after `put_device()`, fixed on upstream master) - `sdw_sl7.py` clears them and skips BIOS-declared peripherals that never attached; (c) the DAI type suffix was only appended when a link has more than one DAI, but `sof_sdw_get_tplg_files()` finds the `sof-sdca-*` topologies by that suffix, so no analog PCM existed; (d) the generated HAL config was bind-mounted from the initramfs /tmp, empty after switch_root (`dras: Failed to read /vendor/etc/audio/gbos-gen/audio_config.xml`) - now copied; (e) Speaker and Headphone were both mapped to PCM 0; SoundWire names its PCMs `Jack Out`, `Jack In`, `Speaker`, `Deepbuffer ...`; (f) the amp's output switches `rt1320-N OT23 L/R` are Off after boot (`mixer_paths_surface_laptop_7.xml`, installed only when DMI says Surface Laptop ... 7th Edition); (g) the amp's MCU patch code `realtek/rt1320/rt1320-patch-code-vc.bin` has no MODULE_FIRMWARE entry (`fetch_firmware.sh` ships it now).
  5. *Touchpad.* HID 045E:0C9F (IPTS, behind quickspi) gives a plain pointer through hid-multitouch; gestures, palm rejection and the physical click need iptsd. `usb/surface/` + service `gbos_iptsd` (oneshot, exits by itself on other laptops) + `tools/build_surface_tools.sh` (alex-lentz/iptsd fork + nierdal's LiftGraceMs patch, static).
  6. *zram.* Android's `mmd_setup` aborted every boot (`zram setup failed: failed to write zram disk size: No such file or directory`) because zram was not in the initramfs; hook.sh retries `modprobe zram` once hwdetect mounted the module tree. Crash logs of boots without it also had Google Play services, `com.google.android.as` and Bluetooth stack aborts; after it, four boots in a row had empty crash.txt (not proven to be cause and effect).
  7. *Display.* Ghost images ("3D glasses"), freezes. The kernel log had `Selective fetch area calculation failed in pipe A` (PSR2), then `CPU pipe A FIFO underrun`, in most boots (6 of 8 logs). `XE_PARAMS="enable_psr=0 enable_fbc=0"` (new gbos.cfg option, applied before the coldplug) fixed it; if it comes back, the next candidate is `enable_dc=0`.
  8. *Model / Device name* showed Google's. `DEVICE_MODEL` in gbos.cfg (build.prop model/marketname + `Settings.Global.device_name`).
  Not done, leads: internal microphone (RT1320 DMIC DAI + cfg-mics: nierdal's `0002-ASoC-sdw_utils-SL7-speakers-and-internal-mic.patch` in linux-surface issue #1710 covers it; not applied here); camera (OV02C10 wired to 12 MHz, the driver expects 19.2 MHz: nierdal's 0003; plus an IPU7 stack); HDMI audio. Tests added: `tools/test_audio_config.sh` (SoundWire case), `tools/test_iptsd_run.sh`, `tools/test_device_model.sh`. `sdw_sl7.py` is the consolidation of two scripts that were run on the android17-6.18 tree (kernel/common 3938c8d97142) and produced the modules running on the hardware; on a pristine 6.18 copy of the two files it yields the same code as those two scripts (diffed; only a comment differs). The final `tools/build_surface_tools.sh` was not re-run end to end.
- 2026-10-19 **RADV (Mesa 25.0.7 AMD Vulkan) built as an Android Vulkan HAL; NOT run on any AMD hardware.** `tools/radv-android/build.sh` (docker, NDK r27c from the gbos-swsr volume, libdrm 2.4.124 static with amdgpu, `-Dandroid-stub=true -Dvulkan-drivers=amd -Dllvm=disabled`) took six runs to get through: meson machine files want single quotes; Mesa 25.0 has no `spirv-tools` option; `glslang-tools` needed on the host; RADV's compile lines miss libdrm's include dirs (`-I/w/sys/include -I/w/sys/include/libdrm` in c_args/cpp_args). Result `build/radv/vulkan.radv.so` (13419576 bytes, sha256 50e10ea2...): ELF64 x86-64, exports `HMI`, NEEDED libcutils liblog libnativewindow libsync libm libz libdl libc. `hook.sh` `gbos_gpu_vulkan`: RADV when `patched/vulkan.radv.so` exists and an amdgpu card is bound, else SwiftShader; `GPU_VULKAN=radv|swiftshader|auto` in gbos.cfg; falls back to SwiftShader when the RADV bind fails; `tools/test_gpu_select.sh` 25 checks. Unknowns for the first Radeon boot: gralloc (our patched minigbm hands RADV linear dumb buffers), drm_hwcomposer on amdgpu, permissions on renderD128, and whether a Ryzen 3 7320U/5425U class iGPU needs firmware not in the stick (check `== missing firmware`). The Discord report (Acer Aspire A5-16, Ryzen 3, black screen) has no logs yet.

No RADV stack traces remain. Mesa `radv_image_from_gralloc` can return this at dma-buf memory import or image-layout creation; logs don't distinguish. - 2026-10-19 **Ryzen 3 3350U (Picasso/Raven, Vega, Acer board 1025:1552; DMI is spoofed "Acidanthera MacBookPro16,2" = booted through OpenCore) hwdetect, kernel #37, build from ~2-4 h before.** `amdgpu` binds fine (RAVEN, DCN 1.0, 512M VRAM, eDP-1 1920x1080 connected, fbcon on amdgpudrmfb, backlight amdgpu_bl1); Wi-Fi AX200, Bluetooth, camera, ALC255 all detected; the user still gets a black screen once Android starts. **No stage0-dmesg/kmsg/logcat from that boot, so the cause is unknown**: the `hook: software rendering: ...` lines and surfaceflinger/hwc errors in logcat.txt are what is needed (SwiftShader release v38 does exist, so a missing download is not it). - 2026-10-19 **Ryzen 3 3350U black screen, boot-6 logs (stage0-dmesg, kmsg 0-90 s, logcat.txt = only the last second).** The AMD path ran as designed: `software rendering: on (auto; no Intel GPU, DRM: card1:amdgpu)`, gralloc/libgbm/SwiftShader all `replaced`, PATCH-STATUS 5/0. Android booted: zygote 57.4 s, `vendor.hwcomposer-3` 58.1 s (opens /dev/dri/card0 first, probes eDP-1 + HDMI-A-1 on amdgpu, does atomic commits at 59.9 s and 61.9 s), surfaceflinger 58.6 s, bootanim 60.2 -> exited 74.8 s (14.5 s; 5.6 s on the Intel ENVY), no service deaths. So the failure is after the composer's first commits (SwiftShader render / buffer path / what the panel shows), not a missing driver or a bind. **Not visible: logcat.txt is the newest 1 MB file; the HWC/SurfaceFlinger/SwiftShader errors are in `logcat.txt.1`, `.2`, ... (`-r 1024 -n 99`).** Next evidence needed: the other `logcat.txt.N` files, `display.txt` (debugfs DRM state at 70 s: planes/FB, shows whether a buffer is scanned out), `crash.txt`, `audio.txt`. Also confirmed in this log: the audio HAL config was generated for card id `Generic` (the Radeon HDMI codec), fixed in acc1e41.
- 2026-10-19 **Ryzen 3 3350U black screen: CAUSE FOUND in logcat.txt.01/.02 (boot-6) and fixed (not yet confirmed on the laptop).** drm_hwcomposer (hwc3-drm, pid 1308) logs for every plane `Plane[N] does not support COLOR_PIPELINE. Rejecting.` + `Failed to init plane N`, then `Found Backend 'generic' for 'eDP-1' and driver 'amdgpu'`, `Primary plane for CRTC 74 not found`, `Could not find a suitable encoder/crtc`, `Failed to create pipeline for connector eDP-1`, `No pipelines available. Creating null-display for headless mode`. SurfaceFlinger therefore got a fake display (`Invalid present fence` on every frame) and nothing reached the panel, although amdgpu, SwiftShader (ANGLE renders OK: `Vulkan 1.3.0 (SwiftShader Device)`), gralloc and bootanim all ran. Cause: Google's `/vendor/build.prop` has `vendor.hwc.drm.enable_color_pipeline=true` (binary strings: it sets the DRM COLOR_PIPELINE client cap and requires the plane property); amdgpu on 6.18 has none. Fix: hook.sh drops that line and writes `=false` when `GPU_SW=1` (tests: `tools/test_gpu_select.sh` 27 checks; `tools/port_build.py` now also anchors the key). Other lines in the same log, harmless: `SurfaceFlinger init: Vulkan device does not support sufficient external semaphore sync fd features, bailing` (SwiftShader has no sync-fd semaphores; SurfaceFlinger uses the GL path), `getOverlaySupport failed`. Still to check after the reporter rebuilds: `drmhwc: Attaching connector eDP-1`, no `Rejecting`, and whether a picture appears; if it does, SwiftShader speed (bootanim took 14.5 s vs 5.6 s) and RADV (`vulkan.radv.so`) are next.

Cause: apps render through HWUI's Vulkan backend (`ro.hwui.use_vulkan=true` in Google's vendor build.prop) and HWUI wants more than one queue on the graphics family; SwiftShader reports `queueCount = 1` (`src/Vulkan/VkPhysicalDevice.cpp:2700`; its Device does support several queues) and RADV's general family has 1 too. SurfaceFlinger is unaffected: it bailed out of its Vulkan RenderEngine (`external semaphore sync fd`) and uses SkiaGL over ANGLE. Fix: hook.sh writes `ro.hwui.use_vulkan=false` in software-GPU mode (apps then use GL via ANGLE like SurfaceFlinger); `tools/test_gpu_select.sh` 29 checks; port_build anchors the key. **Not confirmed on the laptop.** If GL turns out unusable for apps: patch SwiftShader to report queueCount >= 2 (rebuild with `KEEP_BUILD=1 tools/swiftshader-android/build.sh`, new release asset) and patch RADV's `radv_get_physical_device_queue_family_properties` the same way. - 2026-10-19 **KernelSU v3.3.1 built into the kernel (root for apps), not yet run on the hardware.** What was done, and what had to be found out:
  - *The kernel side.* KernelSU (tiann/KernelSU, GPL-2.0) is now part of our kernel: `kernel/kernelsu/apply.sh` links its sources into `drivers/kernelsu` (the way upstream's `kernel/setup.sh` does) and `kernel/configure_whl.py` sets `CONFIG_KSU=y` and `CONFIG_KSU_X86_PATCH_SYSCALL_DISPATCHER=y`. Upstream's own x86_64 build list (`kernel/build-all-x64.sh`) has `android17-6.18` with `clang-r584948c`, i.e. exactly our branch, so no porting was needed.
  - *Why the dispatcher option is not optional.* Since 6.9 x86 hardens the syscall path, and in 6.18 `arch/x86/entry/syscall_64.c` dispatches with a **switch statement** (`case nr: return __x64_##sym(regs);`), not through `sys_call_table[]` - the table is still there (for `trace_syscalls.c`) but is not used for dispatch. KernelSU's hooks patch that table, so on 6.18 they would silently do nothing. Upstream's answer (3.3.0+) is `CONFIG_KSU_X86_PATCH_SYSCALL_DISPATCHER`: it patches the `x64_sys_call` dispatcher itself at runtime (absolute jump to `my_x64_sys_call`, which then goes through the table), through the fixmap, so read-only `.rodata` is no problem. The alternative (the android-generic/kernel-zenith patches that add `X86_FEATURE_INDIRECT_SAFE` and a `syscall_hardening=off` cmdline) was **not** used: it needs two extra commits in the tree and a kernel source change for the same effect.
  - *The syscall tracepoint is enough.* The hooks are driven by the `sys_enter` tracepoint, which exists whenever `CONFIG_HAVE_SYSCALL_TRACEPOINTS` is set (x86 selects it unconditionally, `arch/x86/Kconfig:297`) - `CONFIG_FTRACE_SYSCALLS` is **not** needed (it only adds the ftrace syscall tracer). The tracepoint's regfunc (`syscall_regfunc`, `kernel/tracepoint.c`) sets `SYSCALL_WORK_SYSCALL_TRACEPOINT` in every task, and `syscall_tracepoint_update()` (`include/trace/syscall.h`) hands it to new tasks, so the hooks work for everything started after the kernel boots.
  - *The sources must stay in their git checkout.* KernelSU derives its version from the commit count of its own repository (`Kbuild`: `KSU_VERSION = 30000 + rev-list --count HEAD`), and the manager app refuses a kernel older than 32513 (`Natives.kt: MINIMAL_SUPPORTED_KERNEL`). With the sources copied out of the checkout the count is unknown and the kernel reports **version 16**, which the manager rejects. So `drivers/kernelsu` is a **symlink** into the checkout (`../../inputs/KernelSU/kernel`), as upstream does, and the checkout has to be readable by the build (root owns it in the volume, otherwise git refuses it: "detected dubious ownership").
  - *The manager app has to be the official one.* The kernel checks the APK's v2 signature: the certificate must be exactly 827 bytes with sha256 `c31061b19d8c7d7d6133c6a9bafe198fa944e50c1b31c9d8daa8d7f1fc2d2d6` (`kernel/manager/apk_sign.c`). A Play Store copy is signed by Google and is **not** accepted, so the APK comes from the KernelSU release (`build.sh` puts it in `usb/ksu/manager.apk`, 13.6 MB; the daemon is `ksud-x86_64-linux-android`, 5.8 MB, and also ships inside the APK as `libksud.so`). `tools/test_ksu.sh` checks the certificate with the same rules the kernel uses.
  - *Userspace wiring.* `usb/ksu/install.sh` copies the daemon to `/data/adb/ksud` (the kernel's own injected init.rc runs it) and installs the manager app with `pm install`; it runs from the new `gbos_ksu` service in `hook.sh` (at post-fs-data for the daemon, after boot_completed for the app, because `pm` needs the framework). Log: `logs/boot-N/ksu.txt`.
  - *SELinux stays permissive.* KernelSU only switches SELinux to enforcing in its "late loaded" (module) path; built in, it does not. It creates its own `u:r:ksu:s0` domain when Android's init runs its second stage (`apply_kernelsu_rules()`), so the injected `exec u:r:ksu:s0 ...` works even though Google's policy has no such domain.
  - *The build environment was gone* (the `gbos-ksrc` docker volume with the kernel tree and the toolchain). `kernel/fetch_kernel_sources.sh` puts it back: kernel/common at the pinned commit `3938c8d9` (android17-6.18), the AOSP clang and the KernelSU checkout. The AOSP prebuilts repo publishes **clang-r584948b** (LLVM 22.0.1) but not the `c` build the shipped kernel used; the same compiler, a different build number. `kernel/build_kernel.sh` now does the whole kernel build (patches, config, build, initramfs, results) instead of the manual checklist in the README; `tools/extract_ikconfig.py` gets the .config back out of a kernel image (it unpacks the LZ4 payload the way the kernel's own `unlz4` does).
  - *Verified in QEMU (kernel #38, 6.18.21-gbos-whl).* `kernel/isa_emu_test/run_ksu_qemu.py` boots the kernel and finds:
    "welcome to KernelSU version 32680, package name me.weishu.kernelsu", "sys_call_table=0x...", "x64_sys_call=0x...",
    "patching x64_sys_call", "ni_syscall 1: 134", "dispatcher installed at slot 134", the syscall hooks for nr=117
    (setresuid), 59 (execve), 322 (execveat), 262 (newfstatat), 269 (faccessat), "sys_enter tracepoint registered",
    the read/fstat patches for the init.rc injection, "reboot kprobe registered successfully" and the LSM hooks.
    The ISA emulator still passes 16/16 in the same kernel (`run_isa_qemu.py`, ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS).
    The two QEMU tests are new: the old `run_qemu.py` needs the gbrunch helper module, which is not in this repo.
  - *Two things about the build that cost time.* (1) KernelSU tests its options with `#ifdef`, so the in-tree build
    must pass `-DCONFIG_KSU_X86_PATCH_SYSCALL_DISPATCHER=1` only when the option is **on**: defining it as 0 switches
    the "manager disabled" code path on and the build fails in `throne_tracker.c` (the header's stub and the real
    function collide). (2) The sources must stay in their git checkout (a symlink, as upstream does), otherwise
    KernelSU's version comes out as 16 and the manager app refuses the kernel.
  - *The kernel release string changed*: "6.18.21-gbos-whl+" (the old tree was a git checkout) is now
    "6.18.21-gbos-whl" (the tree is a tarball, so `scripts/setlocalversion` adds nothing). The module directory and
    the vermagic follow it, so **the module tree has to be replaced with the new one** (it is signed with the key
    of the build tree, so the old modules are rejected by the new kernel anyway). The build number continues at
    #38 (the `.version` file in the build directory).
  - *One step left for a public release:* the module tree has to be published again (the new one is in
    `usb/modules/`, built from this tree, but `usb/modules/` is not committed). The old release asset
    (v37, "gbos86-modules-6.18.21-gbos-whl-r3.tar.xz") is signed with the old build's key, so the new kernel
    rejects it: `build.sh` has to point at the new asset. `build.sh` does not fetch it when `usb/modules/`
    already holds a tree, so a local build works as it is.
  - *Not verified on the laptop.* What to look for in `kmsg.log` / `stage0-dmesg.txt`: "welcome to KernelSU version 32680",
    "dispatcher installed at slot N", the app showing up in the manager, and `logs/boot-N/ksu.txt` (the install log).
    Whether `su` really works for a granted app is the real test.

- 2026-10-20 **Photos/Play Store video SurfaceFlinger abort fixed (boot-3 after the fixes, kernel #37).** SurfaceFlinger aborted with `Failed to create a valid texture ... format:842094169` (HAL YV12), then `system_server` logged `DeadSystemException`, which looked like Play Store restarting the VM. Two causes. (1) RADV's own `vkGetAndroidHardwareBufferPropertiesANDROID` answered UNDEFINED for YV12 (skia `Failed to create valid YCbCr conversion information`); `tools/radv-android/patch_mesa.py` (called from `build.sh`) renames it so Mesa's common `vk_common_` one, which reports G8_B8_R8_3PLANE_420, is used. (2) The import then failed with `GBOS AHB layout: stride override failed plane=1 offset=1152000 stride=640`: minigbm's `drv_dumb_bo_create_ex` aligns a YVU420_ANDROID width to 32, amdgpu rounds the pitch to 256 and `drv_bo_from_format_and_padding` sets chroma stride = pitch/2 (1280 -> 640), but RADV GFX9+ needs linear pitches that are multiples of 256. `tools/patch_gralloc_amdgpu.py` now aligns that width to 512 (call into a 14-byte int3 cave at 0xe422), so pitch/2 is a multiple of 256; `usb/patched/libminigbm_gralloc.amdgpu.so` regenerated, original sha unchanged. Result: crash.txt has only the Widevine aborts; Play Store and Photos previews play. Rejected: SkiaGL (`debug.renderengine.backend`) still goes through ANGLE/RADV and hits the same import; the property is ignored from vendor `build.prop`, and a vendor init directory bind overlay hung boot before the desktop.

UNKNOWN_ERROR` and `all codecs failed to extract frame`; AVC/VP9 decode fine (YouTube, Play Store) and `isa_emu_prof` has no libhevc entries. The AMD binds (`libminigbm_gralloc`, `libgbm`, `vulkan.intel.so` replaced by RADV/SwiftShader) are pinned in `hook.sh` and not in the MANIFEST (port_build prints NOTE lines about them): their three originals are also unchanged. `sh tools/test_port_scripts.sh`: PORT_SCRIPTS_PASS. Trap: `PORT_KEEP=1` leaves the unpacked build in docker volume `gbos-port-work`, and the first dry run silently re-checked the OLD build from it ("build in WORK" still showed 16452207): run `docker volume rm gbos-port-work` after changing `lapis/BUILD`, and look at the "build in WORK" line. Still to check on a laptop (see porting.md "What no tool can check"): SELinux types in gbos.rc, first-stage init/dm layout, new Trusty/GSC-backed services (a hang on "Waited one second for ..."), `PATCH-STATUS 5 applied, 0 not applied` in `stage0-dmesg.txt`. Going back to .271.R1 needs a data wipe; data carries forward.
- 2026-10-25 **Reviewed and merged PRs #25 (dual-boot installer, vthonte) and #26 (SKIP_SETUP, vthonte); QEMU-tested, NOT tested on hardware.** Bugs found and fixed in #25: (1) it overwrote the machine's own fallback loader `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` on the shared ESP whenever that file existed (that is Windows' fallback path): it now writes only `EFI/gbos86/BOOTX64.EFI`; only a clean install owns the fallback path; an update refuses an ESP that is neither `GBOS_EFI` nor has `EFI/gbos86`; (2) the new partitions were looked up with `findfs LABEL=` before they had a filesystem, so every dual-boot install failed ("GBOSSYS partition did not appear"): they are now found by GPT name in `sfdisk -d`; (3) the update path took `LABEL=EFI`, which is also the stick's own EFI partition: `internal_efi` now takes the EFI-type partition of the disk that holds GBOSSYS; (4) `tools/install_dualboot.sh` had no serial confirmation and could target an internal disk by accident (added both); (5) `/metadata` without a `GBOS_META` partition: `stage0_init.sh` mounts `gbos/metadata.img` on loop12 (this block is not reachable in the QEMU test, which has a dummy super.img: check `metadata:` lines in `kmsg.log` on hardware). New `GBOS_INSTALL_MODE=dualboot|clean` in gbos.cfg presets the menu (the serial is still required). New test `kernel/isa_emu_test/run_installer_dualboot.py` (cancel, install, update, second install refused, no space, boot from the NVMe alone: `ISA_EMU_INSTALLER_DUALBOOT_PASS`; checks the Windows ESP files and data partition are byte-identical). The eMMC/SATA installer tests had a stale stick builder (no `surface/`, `ksu/`). #26: `SKIP_SETUP=1` is the default, so fresh installs skip the setup wizard (SetupWizard masked, user provisioned by `gbos_provision`, `--user 10` assumed): unverified on hardware.
- 2026-10-25 (MSI Modern 15 B7M, no sound): kmsg showed dras.core.audio denied open of `/vendor/etc/data/hal_configs/audio_default.xml (deleted)`: hook.sh bind-mounted audio_default.xml from the initramfs /tmp, which switch_root deletes (same bug as gbos-gen/audio_config.xml). Fix: copy it into the tmpfs over /vendor/etc/audio and bind from there. Untested on hardware; boot, pick 1 (Update) and check for sound / dras lines.
- 2026-10-25 (MSI Modern 15 B7M): dras read the generated config but looked for card `Generic1` while ALSA named the ALC256 `Generic_1`, then fell back to the stub after 100 retries. Attempts to rename the ALSA id through `/proc` and sysfs failed. The working fix is to leave AMD HDMI audio unbound, re-probe the ALC256, and wait for it to register as `Generic` before generating the config. Hardware testing confirmed dras finds `Generic` and laptop audio works.
- 2026-10-25 **MSI Modern 15 B7M audio fix confirmed.** Stage0 reports the internal ALC256 as card `Generic`; generated config is `gbos-gen` for `Generic`; dras startup uses `primary_soundcard_name: Some("Generic")` and no longer reports the internal-card-not-found/stub failure. HDMI audio is left unbound so laptop speakers retain the tested working configuration. The hook removes the unsuccessful id-write attempt and temporary diagnostics while retaining the verified unbind/re-probe/wait behavior.
- 2026-10-25 **MSI Modern 15 B7M HDMI audio test:** re-binding the GPU HDMI audio creates ALSA card `Generic_1`, and HDMI PCMs 3 and 7 are present. However, the generated dras config reports `hdmi=-`, dras never opens either HDMI PCM, and Android audio policy logs `could not find HW module for AUDIO_DEVICE_OUT_HDMI` / `This kernel does not have HDMI audio support`. HDMI audio is not working; supporting the separate ALSA card likely requires audio-policy/HAL routing changes beyond this hook fix. The re-bind experiment was reverted; HDMI display remains separate and unaffected.
- 2026-10-25 **Acer Aspire A515-46 and HP OmniBook 5: black screen with mouse after AMD HDA re-probe.** Both users reported the desktop booted before the hook and hung after it; logs showed `dras` finds `Generic` but `audio_route` cannot open the mixer, so the audio HAL does not register. The Acer boot confirmed the hook selects the ALC255 as card 0 and generates `gbos-gen`. Earlier HP stage0 logs showed stale `/dev/snd` entries for the previous card numbering after re-probe (old HDMI PCM nodes remained, and the analog PCM was still named card 1). Rebuilding `/dev/snd` nodes from `/sys/class/sound/*/dev` after successful re-probe fixed audio on the affected test laptop (reporter-confirmed). The temporary early-boot diagnostic service was removed after confirmation; the standard post-boot audio diagnostics remain.
- 2026-10-25 **Acer Aspire A515-46 and HP OmniBook 5 audio confirmed working.** Both reporters confirmed audio after the `/dev/snd` node recreation fix. Speaker volume is reported low; defer investigation of mixer levels/routes as a separate follow-up.
- 2026-10-26 **Low speaker volume on legacy-HDA laptops (Dell Latitude 5290 2-in-1, i5; user report): `Master Playback Volume` in `usb/audio/gbos-hda-pch/mixer_paths.xml` raised 64 -> 87 (the Realtek maximum; do not go above 87), NOT tested on hardware.** Only the shipped `PCH` profile changes; `gen_config.sh` cards already set 87. `tools/test_audio_config.sh` passes. Built `dist/gbOS86-20261026.img` (sha256 bb9efd9d...) on Google build 16658739; the raw image holds only `Master Playback Volume" value="87"`.
- 2026-11-01 **Night Light verified on Lenovo ThinkPad X13 Gen 1 (Intel).** Hardware: Core i5-10210U, Intel UHD graphics, eDP 1920x1200; Google build 16658739 (`CL3B.260622.276.R4`), kernel `6.18.21-gbos-whl`. Android Night Light works. On i915, drm_hwcomposer selects the client-composition backend; SurfaceFlinger/RenderEngine GPU-composes the frame and applies the ColorDisplayService transform before scanout. Kernel #29 neutralizes the KMS CRTC `DEGAMMA_LUT`, `GAMMA_LUT` and `CTM` properties, but this does not affect the transform already applied during client composition. The earlier statement that Night Light was broken was a prediction based on those KMS properties, not a hardware test. The old log-gamma fallback veil masked the tint and made the behaviour harder to distinguish; its removal/neutralization does not disable client-composed colour transforms. This finding is verified for this i915 client-composition configuration, not a blanket claim for xe or other backends. **Follow-up still required:** retest the Dell Latitude 5300 with the current stick; capture `gbos_displaydiag` and `dumpsys color_display` while toggling Night Light to distinguish Android's active colour transform from KMS properties.
- 2026-11-17 **Play Store updates stuck pending on dynabook GZ/HWL:** upstream commit `e3b52f4` reports that disabling `com.android.vending` in headless user 0 prevented user 10's Play Store from binding its MultiUserCoordinatorService. `usb/optimize.sh` now keeps it out of the disable list and re-enables it for user 0, including previously affected installs. The upstream reporter confirmed downloads and installs on boot-7; that evidence does not establish a hardware result for the Latitude 7320.
- 2026-11-04 **HP Pavilion 15-ab032tx (Intel Broadwell Core i5-5200U HD 5500 GT2 / NVIDIA 940M): black screen with frozen cursor fixed.** Logcat and kmsg (boot-4 and boot-5) showed SurfaceFlinger successfully built shaders and brought up NexusLauncher, and the cursor plane displayed, but presentation stalled with `drmhwc: could not create drm fb -22` -> `drmhwc: Failed to create AtomicCommitArgs for frame composition` -> `SurfaceFlinger: present failed for display: BAD_DISPLAY (2)`. Kernel kmsg diagnosed the exact failure: `i915 [drm:intel_framebuffer_init]: unsupported pixel format AB24 little-endian (0x34324241) / modifier 0x100000000000001`. SurfaceFlinger defaulted its composition target to `HAL_PIXEL_FORMAT_RGBA_8888` (`DRM_FORMAT_ABGR8888` / `AB24`), which Intel Gen <= 8 display controllers (Broadwell, Haswell, Ivy Bridge) physically do not support on primary scanout planes (universal planes supporting AB24 were only introduced in Gen 9 Skylake). Setting `ro.surface_flinger.default_composition_pixel_format=2` in `build.prop` was insufficient because Google's binary build of SurfaceFlinger hardcodes format 1 for FramebufferSurface. Root cause and definitive fix: in `libminigbm_gralloc.so`, `cros_gralloc_convert_format` (at file offset `0x1a303`) returned FourCC `0x34324241` (`AB24`) for `HAL_PIXEL_FORMAT_RGBA_8888` (1). In `tools/patch_gralloc_amdgpu.py`, we patch this 4-byte FourCC at offset `0x1a304` to `0x34324258` (`XB24` = `DRM_FORMAT_XBGR8888`, identical to the FourCC already returned for `RGBX_8888` at `0x1a35d`). Because `DRM_FORMAT_XBGR8888` has identical 32bpp memory layout and channel ordering, RenderEngine/SwiftShader rendering remains intact while KMS `drmModeAddFB2` succeeds natively on older Intel and AMD hardware.
- 2026-11-04 **Software GPU performance optimization on legacy / CPU-rendered hardware:** On hardware running SwiftShader (`GPU_SW=1`), CPU was saturated at 100% due to Kawase background blurs, unpinned CPU scaling governor, missing `optimize.sh` on the system partition, and drmhwc 60 FPS gamma logcat spam. Fix: (1) disable window blurs system-wide (`persist.sys.sf.disable_blurs=1`, `cmd window disable-blur 1`), (2) lock CPU frequency governor to `performance` in stage0, hook.sh and gbos.rc to prevent low-power throttling, (3) package and start `usb/optimize.sh` to stop headless User 0 duplicates and free 2.5 GB RAM, and (4) suppress drmhwc gamma logging with `log.tag.drmhwc=WARN`.
- 2026-11-04 **HP Pavilion 15-ab032tx (Intel Broadwell Core i5-5200U HD 5500 GT2 / NVIDIA 940M): mouse lag, 360% CPU SurfaceFlinger load, and ANR freezes resolved.** Analysis of boot-7 logs (`logcat.txt.01`, `hw.txt`) showed the machine has 8 GB RAM (7.8 GB free at boot), completely ruling out low-RAM / OOM issues. CPU usage diagnostics during ANR dumping revealed SurfaceFlinger alone was consuming 363% CPU (out of 400% capacity on a 4-thread CPU), starving `system_server` (which only got 6%) and `InputDispatcher`, causing 5-second focus event timeouts and ANRs (`SettingsHomepageActivity`, `system_server`). Root causes and fixes:
  (1) **Hardware cursor plane disabled:** In `hook.sh`, `ro.vendor.hwc.use_overlay_planes=false` was applied indiscriminately in software GPU mode. `drm_hwcomposer` responded by discarding all non-primary planes including `plane[44]` (the Intel hardware cursor plane, format AR24). Consequently, every mouse movement forced SurfaceFlinger to composite the cursor into the 1366x768 framebuffer in software via SwiftShader across 4 threads, spiking CPU to 363% and delaying input delivery by 3-4 seconds. Fix: on Intel (`i915`/`xe`), preserve `ro.vendor.hwc.use_overlay_planes=true` so `drm_hwcomposer` assigns the mouse cursor to `plane[44]`, enabling zero-CPU hardware cursor scanout at 60 FPS.
  (2) **Graphite Vulkan compiler on CPU:** Stock vendor `build.prop` enables Google's Graphite Desktop opt-in (`debug.renderengine.graphite_desktop_optin=true`), which compiles Vulkan pipelines asynchronously at runtime and thrashes CPU SwiftShader. Fix: set `debug.renderengine.backend=skiaglthreaded`, `debug.renderengine.graphite=false`, `debug.renderengine.graphite_desktop_optin=false`, and `debug.renderengine.vulkan=false` to use lightweight Ganesh SkiaGL over ANGLE.
  (3) **Frame queue latency reduction:** Set `ro.hwui.render_ahead=0` and `ro.surface_flinger.max_frame_buffer_acquired_buffers=2` to eliminate multi-frame buffering lag on CPU rendering. Set animation scales to 0 (`window_animation_scale=0`, `transition_animation_scale=0`, `animator_duration_scale=0`) for instant window responsiveness.
  (4) **CPUSet & PowerHAL (libperfmgr) fix:** On systems with < 8 cores (e.g. 2 cores, 4 threads), Google's `powerhint.json` hardcodes values `"4-7"` for background and restricted cpusets, causing continuous `-EINVAL` kernel write errors and starving background tasks. In `optimize.sh`, configure all cpusets dynamically to match `0-$(($(nproc) - 1))` and set `vendor.sys_tuner.disable_cpuset_config=true`.
  (5) **Touchpad inhibitor busy loop:** In `optimize.sh`, stop OEM proprietary `touchpad-inhibitor` to eliminate its 1-second busy loop polling for non-existent ASUS touchpad hardware.
  (6) **Hardware assessment on NVIDIA GeForce 940M:** Clarified that the hybrid NVIDIA GeForce 940M (GM108M) cannot be used for UI scanout or rendering: its display lines are physically unrouted (routed exclusively via Intel HD 5500 over Optimus/PRIME), and Mesa NVK Vulkan explicitly requires Maxwell 2+ (GM20x), making GM108 unsupported by modern Android Vulkan graphics stacks.
- 2026-11-04 **Dell Inspiron 14 5440 (i3-1305U, RTL8852BE) user report: /data fell back to tmpfs after the partitions were re-made; screen-reader request.** Display/i915, Wi-Fi and BT fine; speaker volume ~50% quiet, fixed by the 64 -> 87 `Master Playback Volume` change already shipped (2026-10-26). `stage0_init.sh` looked for the data partition only by ext4 label (`findfs LABEL=GBOS_DATA`), so a partition that kept its GPT name `GBOS_DATA` but lost its ext4 label gave `data/metadata: tmpfs (not persistent)`. New `find_part` in `stage0_init.sh`: label first, else the partition whose sysfs `uevent` has `PARTNAME=GBOS_DATA` (or `GBOS_META`) **on the same disk as GBOSSYS**; it is still only used if it mounts as ext4 (nothing is formatted). Logs `data partition: ...` in `kmsg.log`. **Inside the kernel image, so it needs a kernel rebuild (`make_stage0_initramfs.sh`, `kernel/isa_emu_patch/build.sh`); NOT built, NOT tested on hardware, `sh -n` only.** Workaround without a new kernel: from Linux, `sudo e2label /dev/nvme0n1p6 GBOS_DATA` (the filesystem must be ext4). `install.sh` still finds an existing install only by label (`NDATA`). Screen reader (speakup/espeakup in stage0 + installer) is NOT done: needs `CONFIG_SPEAKUP*` in the kernel, espeakup + espeak-ng data (downloads) and a working sound path in stage0.

Stick: `usb/a11y/` (`tools/fetch_a11y_tools.sh`: espeakup + espeak-ng English data + amixer from Alpine edge/testing, musl, run through the bundled loader like `usb/installer/`, 10 MB) with `start.sh`/`stop.sh`. `stage0_init.sh` copies the folder to RAM while it reads `gbos.cfg` from the stick, unmounts, sources `start.sh` before the boot menu, and `stop.sh` before switch_root. `start.sh`: finds Speakup, `/dev/softsynth`, the first non-HDMI ALSA card, writes `/etc/asound.conf` (plain `hw:N,0` via plug, no dmix), unmutes every playback control to 85%, turns `ignore_loglevel` off + `dmesg -n 1` (else every printk is read aloud; the stick log reads `/dev/kmsg` and is unaffected), starts espeakup. **This espeakup rejects `-P`** (prints usage); it daemonizes and writes `/var/run/espeakup.pid`. Menu waits 60 s with speech (`A11Y_MENU_TIMEOUT`), `A11Y=0` disables. `install.sh`: with `/a11y/active` it prints progress every 10 percent as plain lines, drops `=====` rulers and spells the serial out ("A, B, C"); the typed serial confirmation is unchanged. Android: `ACCESSIBILITY=talkback` in gbos.cfg -> service `gbos_talkback` enables `com.google.android.marvin.talkback/.TalkBackService` for users 0 and 10 once per /data (TalkBack and GoogleTTS are in the product partition). Also in this kernel: the stage0 `find_part` fallback (GPT name `GBOS_DATA`/`GBOS_META` on the GBOSSYS disk when the ext4 label is missing). **Tested in QEMU only**: `kernel/isa_emu_test/run_a11y_qemu.py` (intel-hda + hda-duplex, audio recorded to WAV: 744k loud samples with speech on, 0 with `A11Y=0`) = `ISA_EMU_A11Y_PASS`; `run_qemu.py` = `ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS`. **NOT run:** the installer QEMU tests (`run_installer_*.py`: too little free disk for the 15 GB guard) after the `install.sh` output change, `find_part` (only reached when booting from the NVMe), the speech during an install, TalkBack enabling, and anything on real hardware. SOF-only laptops have no sound card at stage0 (`a11y: no sound card`), so no speech there; nothing is spoken before the stick's kernel starts. The module tree in `usb/modules` was rebuilt for #46 (not tracked): the stick needs `tools/deploy_stick.sh` (replaces modules when the build id differs).
- 2026-11-05 **Dell Inspiron 14 5440 (Raptor Lake-U) confirmed on kernel #46: TalkBack worked on first boot; the spoken boot menu did not (`a11y: no sound card, no speech`); dual-boot `/data` is now persistent.** Cause of the silent menu, from the reporter's `kmsg.log`: `pci 0000:00:1f.3: deferred probe pending: snd_hda_intel: couldn't bind with audio component`. On 12th/13th-gen Intel the HDA controller shares the GPU's audio component, so it cannot bind until i915 is loaded; stage0 loads i915 only after the boot menu, and `start.sh` gave up after 8 s (i915 bound at ~34 s, menu was over at ~30 s). Same report, second message: Chrome/YouTube crash right after launching, Firefox and the KernelSU manager are very slow to come to the foreground. The `kmsg.log` shows no kernel-level cause (no faults, 8 GB RAM, i915 healthy, only SELinux denials in permissive mode), so it is **undiagnosed**: needs `logcat.txt*` and `crash.txt` from `gbos/logs/boot-N/` (installer menu: 3, Only save logs), taken after reproducing the crash. Quick isolation test: `GPU_SOFTWARE=1` in gbos.cfg (CPU rendering).

The bus clash/clock-stop part is not explained. (2) `usb/stage0_init.sh` + `kernel/stage0_modules.txt`: stage0 loads `rtsx_pci_sdmmc` (and `rtsx_pci` as its dependency). The HP OmniBook 7 225U's Wi-Fi is PCI 8086:7740 (`iwlwifi` loaded, no `iwlmld`/`iwlmvm`, no firmware error, no wlan0); the 10-16 hwdetect has no driver-message section, so the cause is still unknown. Stick-side fixes only, NOT tested on hardware, `sh -n` + `tools/test_gpu_select.sh` pass.** (1) *Animations/blur off on every boot (confirmed regression from 37a3442, 2026-11-04):* `hook.sh` (`gbos_skip_setup`) and `optimize.sh` wrote `window_animation_scale`/`transition_animation_scale`/`animator_duration_scale` = 0 and `disable_window_blurs` = 1 into /data on every boot, on every GPU. Now `hook.sh` exports `ro.gbos.software_rendering=1` only in software-rendering mode and `optimize.sh` applies the old behaviour only then; with a hardware GPU it restores the defaults **once** (`persist.gbos.anim_restored`) for installs that already got the 0 values, then leaves the user's settings alone. (2) `usb/a11y/stop.sh` now also releases the Speakup synth (`echo none > /sys/accessibility/speakup/synth`) so nothing queues console text for a reader that is not running. Precautionary: **not shown to be the cause of anything.** **Open, NOT fixed:** (a) the zygote loop: `zygote_next` `SpawnSubspecies` child exits with `Errno EBADF` ("Resetting FileDescriptorRegistry ... Reinitialization"), every isolated process (Chrome GPU) fails to start, 12k-50k `ZygoteProcess: Got error connecting to zygote` lines per boot on the Vostro 3400 (TGL, kernel #46, software rendering off, `xe` backs off, i915 fine, scanout fine).
