- 2026-11-14 **Discord reports after the Surface Laptop 7 merge (57cdc6b, 2026-11-09): apps freeze after a while and Google Play cannot install/update on non-Surface laptops; Nov 8 builds were fine. Found by reading the merge, NOT confirmed on hardware, `sh -n` + `test_port_scripts.sh` pass.** The merge added `start gbos_camera` and `start gbos_sleep_guard` for every machine. `gbos_sleep_guard` has no `oneshot` and its script exits at once on a non-Surface, so init restarted it every 5 s for as long as the system ran (the neighbouring `gbos_iptsd` has `oneshot` for this reason). `surface-camera.sh` guarded with `non-Surface && no /dev/media0`, so any laptop with a webcam media node ran it: it loaded v4l2loopback, chmod/chown/chcon every `/dev/video*` and `/dev/media*`, and `kill -9`ed cameraserver and the camera provider HAL. Fix: `hook.sh` starts both services only when DMI `product_name` contains Surface; the camera guard is now `non-Surface || no /dev/media0`. Open: whether this explains the freezes (the zygote `EBADF` loop in the 2026-11-12 entry is still unexplained and may be the real cause or an extra one); needs a boot with logs from an affected laptop (look for `gbos_sleep_guard` restarts in logcat/kmsg and `Got error connecting to zygote`).
- 2026-11-15 **zygote_next app freezes (blank app windows, Play-updated Chrome): mitigation + evidence capture added; root cause still unknown; NOT tested on hardware (`tools/test_native_zygote.sh` passes against stubs).** Evidence (Dell Vostro 3400, R5 16693588, kernel 6.18.21-gbos-whl, diagnostics of 2026-11-10, 5 boots): 10k-50k `ZygoteProcess: Got error connecting to zygote` per boot; each `zygote_next` `SpawnSubspecies` child logs `Resetting FileDescriptorRegistry for subspecies`, then `Error encountered while responding to client socket N: Reinitialization`, closes its own socket and dies with `Failed to poll server file descriptors: EBADF` (status 1) about 45 ms after the fork. The processes ActivityManager kills are `com.android.chrome:sandboxed_process0:org.chromium.content.app.NativeOnlySandboxedProcessService0..N`, i.e. the image's own Chrome already uses the native-only path (not just a Play-updated one). **R4 ships zygote_next too** (checked: `zygote_next64.rc` and `libzygote_species_android_native` are in both system images), so R5 did not introduce it. The framework side is `NativeZygoteProcess` gated by aconfig flag `android.os.native_framework_prototype` (DeviceConfig `android_os/native_framework_prototype`). zygote_next's fd audit has `AuditFailure`/`FileDescriptorUnexpected`/`Unregistered{Fd,BoundSocket,ConnectedSocket,FifoFd,SignalFd}`/`ProvidedFdNotOpen`, so an fd it does not expect is the first suspect, but nothing yet names it (stage0 hands init only fds 0-2; its subshell loggers are separate processes). AVC lines (permissive) show zygote_next opening `/dev/null` and reading the patched `system.libz.so` from the FAT stick: noise, not the fault. **Added:** `usb/native_zygote.sh` (service `gbos_nativezygote`, started from `on boot`, oneshot, runs all boot): sets `persist.zygote.zygote_next.start_on_boot=false`, forces the DeviceConfig flag to false and re-applies it every minute (Play services can sync it back), and writes `logs/boot-N/native-zygote.txt` with `/proc/PID/fd`, fdinfo, SELinux context and init's own fd table for each zygote_next pid (max 8 a boot). Shipped by `deploy_stick.sh`, `make_gbos86_image.sh`, `install_dualboot.sh`, `install.sh`. **Known gap:** a reporter measured that the flag alone did not stop Chrome 157 (it still asked for `NativeOnlySandboxedProcessService` with the flag read back false), so this may not fix Chrome; a per-component disable was tried and dropped by the reporter, and is not shipped. **Next:** boot a stick with this, open Chrome, then Update (1) and read `native-zygote.txt`: any fd in the zygote_next table that is not 0-2, a logd/statsd socket, `/dev/null` or its own server socket is the one the registry rejects; then find where it comes from (init, stage0, a `gbos.rc` service) and close it.
- 2026-11-16 **zygote_next / app freezes: root cause = `/dev/null` is a regular file, created by stage0 (found by itakosu55 on fork branch `fix-zygote-next-dev-null`; re-derived here from our own logs and code; the fork author reports it fixed on their dynabook, we have NOT booted it).** `usb/stage0_init.sh` ran `echo "1 1 1 1" > /proc/sys/kernel/printk 2>/dev/null` on the empty `/dev` tmpfs before `mknod /dev/null c 1 3`: the redirect creates `/dev/null` as a regular file, the mknod fails ("File exists"), and Android inherits the file. zygote_next saves each fd's state at start and audits every SpawnSubspecies child against it, offset of fds 0-2 included; on a file the offset moves (everything "written to /dev/null" piles up in it), the audit fails, and the child reports only `Reinitialization` and dies with EBADF: Chrome sandboxed processes and WebView renderers never start, system_server's single process-start thread stalls, and every app freezes while Play keeps working. **Evidence in our own Vostro 3400 bundle (2026-11-10, 5 boots):** all 1104 AVC lines on `path="/dev/null"` say `tclass=file` (a character device is `chr_file`), and the first one is `init` writing to it with `tcontext=u:object_r:tmpfs:s0` (not `null_device`). **Why only gbOS86 and only zygote_next:** Google's first-stage init creates the node itself, we skip it (`exec switch_root ... init selinux_setup`), and nothing else audits fds this strictly. **Fix:** `usb/hook.sh` now starts with a repair (`[ ! -c /dev/null ]`: mknod a new node, rename it over the file, keep the old one if mknod fails; logs `hook: /dev/null was a regular file (stage0 bug), recreated as the character device` to `stage0-dmesg.txt`), so it works on every kernel, including #46 and older, once the stick/NVMe is updated (choose **1 (Update)**). `usb/stage0_init.sh` now does the mknods first, but stage0 lives in the kernel initramfs, so that only takes effect after the next `kernel/build_kernel.sh` (BOOTX64.EFI NOT rebuilt). `usb/native_zygote.sh` logs `ls -l /dev/null` every boot (`logs/boot-N/native-zygote.txt`: must be `crw-rw-rw- ... 1, 3`) and stays on as a second line of defence (flag off + fd table of zygote_next). `tools/test_native_zygote.sh` checks the mknod-before-use order in stage0 (fails on the old script), that the repair is the first statement of hook.sh, and its failure path. **To confirm on hardware:** `stage0-dmesg.txt` has the "recreated as the character device" line, `native-zygote.txt` shows `crw-rw-rw-` and zygote_next fds 0-2 at `pos: 0`, and logcat has no `Reinitialization`. Possible follow-up once confirmed: drop the DeviceConfig flag override (it is not needed if this is the whole story).
- 2026-11-17 **dynabook GZ/HWL (R5 16693588, kernel #46, NVMe install with the `/dev/null` repair): Play Store updates, and the "System services" updates in Google settings, stay "Pending" forever, while APKs from Aurora Store install. Cause: `optimize.sh` disables `com.android.vending` in user 0 (since 37a3442, 2026-11-04). Read from the `boot-3`..`boot-6` logs; fixed, confirmed on hardware in `boot-7`.** Play Store runs in user 10, but before each download its install queue binds `com.android.vending/com.google.android.finsky.multiusercoordinator.MultiUserCoordinatorService` in user 0. Every `IQ: N scheduled install statuses found to proceed` is followed within 1 ms by `ActivityManager: Unable to resolve service Intent { ... MultiUserCoordinatorService } U=0: not found`, then `Installer::MCS: Couldn't start service` and `IQ: Failed to run current jobs after cancellation` (`java.lang.Exception: Failed to bind to MultiUserCoordinatorService`): 26 times in boot-3. No install in the four boots got past `SCHEDULED`; in boot-3, 37 updates waited 16 min (`tsc=PT16M7s`) and were then cancelled by the user. Aurora Store does not go through Play Store's install queue, so it does not need the coordinator. The disable takes effect: in `ai-1.txt`, packages from the same list (aicore, bard) show `User 0: ... enabled=3` (DISABLED_USER). Other vending services fail in user 0 the same way (`GlobalSharedPrefService`, `ProfileStateService`). Fix: vending is out of `USER0_BLOAT`, and `optimize.sh` runs `pm enable --user 0 com.android.vending` on every boot, because the disable is stored in `/data` and outlives the script change. This is probably the "Google Play cannot install/update" part of the 2026-11-14 Discord report. Not changed: a request for aicore's `AiCoreUser0Service` in user 0 fails the same way (`U=0: not found`, aicore is in the same list); whether that blocks AICore features was not checked. Result (`boot-7`, kernel #46, the new `optimize.sh` on GBOSSYS, `gbos_optimize` exited 0): updates start and finish. The logcat covers only 00:36:19-00:37:08 (the install logging rotated the rest out); in it there are 0 `MultiUserCoordinatorService` errors and no `U=0: not found` for vending, 138 `DOWNLOADING` lines and 15 `INSTALLED` packages: `com.google.android.gms` and `com.google.android.aicore` (`single_install`, the System services updates), 11 bulk updates and 2 WebAPKs.
- 2026-11-24 **Haswell (no ADX): ADCX/ADOX emulation in the #UD handler; RTL8852BE-VT, AMD PMC, r8169 PHY. Verified in QEMU, NOT yet on hardware.** Issues 57 (i5-4570T) and 61 (i7-4790): zygote64 dies in `bn_mulx4x_mont` (`libcrypto_httpengine.so`, tethering APEX) and keystore2 in `bn_sqrx8x_internal` with SIGILL ILL_ILLOPN. Google's BoringSSL is built for an ADX CPU, so its MULX/ADX big-number paths have no runtime CPU check; `lapis/scan` lists ADX in ~20 libraries (every libcrypto copy, `libjavacore.so`, `libcros_ml_core_internal.so`, ...), so binary patches are not practical. `isa_emu.c` `try_adx()` emulates `66|F3 [REX] 0F 38 F6` on pt_regs (32/64-bit, reg/mem source, only CF resp. OF written) before the XSAVE/vector path; all 358 sites in `system.libcrypto.so` are `66|F3 REX.W 0F 38 F6` (objdump). Tests: `isa-emu/test_core.c` 413385 checks (200k random vs `__int128` and, with `-madx`, vs the real instructions); kernel built from this tree, `run_qemu.py` **Skylake-Client: ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS, Haswell: ISA_EMU_TEST_PASS** (19 checks incl. interleaved CF/OF chains, memory operands, 32-bit zero-extension); control: the shipped `usb/out/BOOTX64.EFI` under `-cpu Haswell` dies at the first `adcx` (`traps: init[1] trap invalid opcode`, panic exitcode 4). Expect slow RSA private-key operations on Haswell (every ADCX/ADOX traps); verification/ECDH should be fine. Other Haswell gaps in the scans: RDSEED/CLWB/CLFLUSHOPT only in the Trusty VM images (not run here) and 5 CLFLUSHOPT in vendor. Same branch: `RTW89_8852BTE=m` (issue 59, 10ec:b520), `AMD_PMC=m` (issue 51, s2idle S0i3), `hwdetect.sh` `gbos_preload_phy` loads `realtek` before coldplug (issue 61: r8169's `MODULE_SOFTDEP("pre: realtek")` is ignored by busybox modprobe, so it probed without a PHY driver, error -49); all three modules build. Found while building: `configure_whl.py --check` raised `KeyError: 'VMD'` (VMD is in REQUIRED but only in GENERIC), which aborts `build_generic.sh`/`build_thc.sh` under `set -e`; fixed. Two more for whoever rebuilds from scratch: the `+archive` tarball sha256 pinned in `fetch_kernel_sources.sh` no longer matches what googlesource serves (a `git fetch --depth 1` of commit `3938c8d9` + `git archive` gives the same tree, verified by object hashes), and `kernel/test_kernel_qemu.py` imports `test_amd_kernel_qemu` from a `gbrunch/tools` directory that is not in the repo (`run_qemu.py` only needs its `cpio()`).
- 2026-11-25 **Surface Pro 5 (model 1807, LTE) on hardware with gbOS86-20261124.img (kernel #66 of feat/surface-pro-5): sound and Bluetooth work, touch and Wi-Fi did not; both fixed in stick files, NOT yet re-tested.** (1) Touch: the ipts driver comes up (`IPTS EDS Version: 1`, event mode, `IPTS 045E:001F Touchscreen`, hidraw1) and iptsd-run.sh found the node, but `iptsd-check-device --quiet --type touchscreen` refused it on every try for 120 s, then "giving up". Cause (iptsd 3663e96e source): check-device builds the same `core::Application` as iptsd, which throws `InvalidScreenSize` when width/height are 0; EDS v1 devices have no metadata report, so the size comes only from a config, and iptsd-run.sh exported `IPTSD_CONFIG_FILE` only for iptsd, not for the check (the preset dirs don't exist on Android). Now the check gets `IPTSD_CONFIG_FILE=$conf`, runs without `--quiet`, and its output is logged once per node; giving up names the refused node. (2) Wi-Fi switched itself off again: the 88W8897 firmware loads now (`FW download over, size 723540 bytes`, `WLAN FW is active`), but mwifiex names its interface `mlan0` and Android looks for `wlan0`. `hwdetect.sh` `gbos_rename_wifi` renames `mlan*` to `wlan0` (busybox `ip link set dev mlan0 name wlan0`, while down) in `gbos_wait_devices`, logged as `hwdetect: Wi-Fi interface mlan0 renamed to wlan0`. It also lets the wait loop see the interface (it counts `^wl`, so it waited the full 15 s for `mlan0`). `nxp/rgpower_WW.bin` -2 is optional (power table). Tests: `tools/test_iptsd_run.sh` 21/21 (new: check runs with the config, reason logged once, giving-up line), new `tools/test_wifi_rename.sh` 10/10; `sh -n` with dash and the stick's busybox. The image also carries a second module dir `6.18.21-gbos-whl+` (the kernel uses `6.18.21-gbos-whl`): dead weight.
- 2026-11-25 **Output volumes at the codec's own maximum instead of a fixed 87 (issues 31 and 32: Samsung Galaxy Book2 Pro / Book2 Pro 360, ALC298, "can't hear a thing unless the volume is on max"). QEMU-checked, NOT tested on hardware.** The template's `Master Playback Volume` 87 (and `gen_config.sh`'s Speaker/Headphone `87 87`) is the Realtek 0x57-step maximum; the ALC298 has 0x7f steps (the #32 reporter measured max 127, and public ALC298 codec dumps show `nsteps=0x7f`), so it stayed 40 steps below its top. New `usb/audio/volmax.sh CARD_DIR MIXER_XML`: reads `/proc/asound/cardN/codec#*`, where every control is listed under its node (`Control: name="Speaker Playback Volume"` + `ControlAmp: dir=Out ... ofs=N` + `Amp-Out caps: ... nsteps=0x7f`), takes max = nsteps - ofs (`get_amp_max_value`), gives the virtual `Master` the smallest maximum of the output volumes (inputs mixed into the output, such as `Beep Playback Volume` with 0x1f steps, are left out), and replaces only values that are still 87. Called by `gen_config.sh` (non-PCH cards) and by `hook.sh` for card id PCH on a copy in `/tmp/gbos-audio` (the shipped files stay as they are; with 0x57 steps nothing changes, logged `nothing to change`). No `amixer` needed, so it also works on NVMe installs (the a11y amixer is only on the stick). QEMU (`intel-hda` + `hda-duplex`, kernel #65): the real codec file has exactly that layout, volmax gives `Master=74` (0x4a) and the kernel's own range (a11y amixer) is `min=0,max=74`, start value `0,0`. Caveat found there: amixer clamps 87 to 74 in alsa-lib, so whether dras refuses or clamps an out-of-range value was not shown. `tools/test_audio_config.sh`: 94/94 (17 new). #32's other changes (Night Light colour pipeline, Egis fingerprint HAL) are not part of this.

- 2026-11-10 **Dell Latitude 7320 (kernel #46) user-supplied boot logs: no ALSA card; untested fix.** `hwdetect.txt`/`audio.txt` show no card; `kmsg.log` shows SOF failing to connect `ALH770.IN` / `SDW3-Capture` and the machine driver failing card registration. The topology reports ABI 3:22:1 while the kernel reports 3:23:1, but the logs do not prove this is causal. SoundWire codecs (`rt711-sdca`, `rt1316`, `rt715-sdca`) were requested at the time SOF parsed the topology; `usb/hwdetect.sh` now requests the codecs before coldplug to avoid that possible registration race. Hardware confirmation is needed. Separately, later `/vendor/lib/modules` loads fail with struct-module-size mismatches and `Exec format error`; those are Google-kernel modules, not the GBOS tree, and `usb/hook.sh` now masks that incompatible directory after GBOS modules load. The no-card audio path now leaves Google's vendor audio configuration untouched instead of activating the static PCH config. The SoundWire link-2 bus-clash and clock-stop warnings remain unexplained; no BIOS/ACPI or firmware defect is inferred.

- 2026-11-10 **Silent SoundWire speaker diagnostics (user-supplied logs):** Android routes unmuted audio with nonzero signal to speaker PCM 2 at 48 kHz, but no sound is audible. The image's reported UCM directory (`sof-soundwire_cs42l43_cs35l56`) does not match the detected RT711/RT1316/RT714 codecs; this suggests a profile mismatch but does not identify a safe replacement or prove that it causes silence. `InternalMic` is also absent from the reported UCM path, but the available log summary does not establish which PCM/profile should replace it. Playback timing errors (`get_htimestamp`, presentation position) are not controlled by the repo's HAL XML and there is no corresponding SoundWire timing-driver patch here; no fake position or diagnostic suppression was added. `usb/audio/mixdiag.sh` now falls back to the already bundled `a11y` ALSA `amixer` when optional `tinymix` is absent, so the next capture can include actual card controls. Speaker playback and capture-profile issues still need the exact active UCM files/control dump; timing needs a kernel/PCM trace.

- 2026-11-10 **Dell Latitude 7320 Detachable (DMI board 0TNW0H) follow-up logs:** ALSA exposes jack playback PCM 0, Speaker PCM 2, and HDMI PCMs 5-7; `gen_config.sh` correctly maps Speaker to PCM 2 and Headphone to PCM 0. The earlier `analog-playback=0` log field meant the first/default non-HDMI playback PCM and did not mean the Speaker route used PCM 0. The generated XML comment incorrectly identified the Latitude 5300 because it inherited a PCH-specific template comment; the comment is now generic, and the PCM diagnostic names default, speaker, and headphone playback separately. The runtime hook generates `gbos-gen` HAL XML but does not install a 7320-specific vendor UCM profile; the image's `sof-soundwire_cs42l43_cs35l56` directory still mismatches the detected Realtek codecs. No replacement profile or mixer routes are safe to select yet: the mixer dump failed before reporting controls, and amp-channel configuration is not established by driver/module names alone. `mixdiag.sh` now sets both ALSA config path and config directory and addresses card 0 as `hw:0` directly instead of relying on `amixer -c 0`'s sysdefault alias. Host-side invocation loads bundled ALSA config without missing-file errors, but this host has no ALSA card, so controls remain unverified. `get_htimestamp` still fails after PCM open and remains a separate unresolved ALSA/kernel timing issue. The user reports this laptop's audio works in Windows.

- 2026-11-10 **Dell Latitude 7320 Detachable RT1316 mute-path evidence:** the new mixer capture shows `Speaker Switch` on and PGA playback volumes at 32/32, but `rt1316-1 DAC Switch` remains off/off while Speaker PCM 2 is active. The bundled SOF SoundWire `rt1316.conf` is explicit: for `cfg-amp:1`, Speaker enable selects RT1316 RX channels `L,R` and enables the DAC switch (or separate L/R DAC switches); Speaker disable turns those switches off. The active image profile directory is named `sof-soundwire_cs42l43_cs35l56`, which does not match the detected RT711/RT1316/RT715 codecs; independently of its name, the captured off/off DAC state proves the active Speaker path did not perform the required RT1316 enable. Added `mixer_paths_latitude_7320.xml` for the Speaker path only, mirroring the bundled one-amp UCM route and enabling the observed stereo DAC switch; `hook.sh` installs it only when ALSA card id is `sofsoundwire` and both DMI product and board match. Regression tests check the mixer route and hook guard. Not yet retested on hardware; `get_htimestamp` remains unverified as a secondary/separate failure.

- 2026-11-10 **Dell Latitude 7320 Detachable microphone retest:** sound now works after the RT1316 speaker route change. Capture controls in `mixer.txt` were off before the later stream started, so they do not prove a live-route failure. Rotated logcat shows DRAS enabling `InternalMic`, applying that path, and opening PCM 1 successfully; the earlier missing-InternalMic-path error did not recur. But the bundled SOF UCM identifies RT711 `Headset` capture as PCM 1 (`rt711 FU0F Capture Switch`) and RT714 `Mic` array capture as PCM 4 (`rt714 FU02 Capture Switch`). The generic generated Android profile mapped both `Mic` and `InternalMic` to first capture PCM 1, so Android's selected InternalMic route was opening the headset ADC. Added a 7320-only `audio_config_latitude_7320.xml` mapping InternalMic to PCM 4 and Mic to PCM 1, with the RT711 mixer element and headset jack; added corresponding route-scoped RT711/RT714 capture switches to `mixer_paths_latitude_7320.xml`. `hook.sh` installs both overrides only for the matching SoundWire card and DMI model/board. Tests cover both PCM assignments and per-route controls. Hardware confirmation still needed for microphone capture; no switch is globally forced while idle.

- 2026-11-10 **Latitude 7320 Detachable camera and auto-rotate review (user-supplied boot diagnostics):** PCI `0000:00:05.0` (`8086:9a19`, class `0x048000`) is unbound and the boot has no video nodes. The generic payload has no IPU6 modules or matching camera HAL/bridge; the existing Surface Laptop 7 bridge targets IPU7/OV02C10 and is not interchangeable. The attempted `/vendor/lib/modules/ov8856.ko` is rejected for a module-structure size mismatch, so it must not be used with this kernel. The current `hook.sh` masks Google's incompatible vendor-module directory after loading GBOS modules; this should stop that `ov8856.ko` attempt after the updated hook is deployed, but does not add camera support. On sensors, HID-SENSOR accel, gyro and orientation IIO devices are bound, raw accel/gyro values are live, and Android's sensors multihal registers `ISensors/default`, but Android reports no accelerometer and `OrientationEventListener` cannot find sensors; Windows does operate the hardware. This rules out missing kernel IIO drivers. SELinux is permissive, and its audit lines are not proof of access denial.

  **Follow-up inspection of the local vendor image, HAL source and later capture:** `/vendor/etc/sensors/hals.conf` contains only `/vendor/lib64/android.hardware.sensors@2.1-Desktop-IIO-Subhal.so`; no other vendor sensor/IIO config files were found. Read-only inspection of the binary and a public mirror of Google's Trogdor IIO sub-HAL ([`SensorsSubHal.cpp`](https://github.com/YAJATapps/android_device_google_trogdor/blob/a8a85fe07e1489a48eb5ac519fd8f400e21d2307/hal/sensors/SensorsSubHal.cpp), [`iio_utils.cpp`](https://github.com/YAJATapps/android_device_google_trogdor/blob/a8a85fe07e1489a48eb5ac519fd8f400e21d2307/hal/sensors/iio_utils.cpp)) confirms the discovery flow: `load_iio_devices()` reads each IIO `name`, then `isSensorSupported()` applies a compiled-in whitelist (`scmi.iio.accel`, `scmi.iio.gyro`, `cros-ec-accel`, `cros-ec-gyro` in that source revision; the shipped binary also contains other desktop IIO names such as `cros-ec-mag`, `cros-ec-light`, `sx9324`). Neither includes the Latitude's `accel_3d`/`gyro_3d`. Only accepted devices proceed to `scan_elements()`, which enables scan channels; the constructor then calls `enable_sensor(..., false)` to leave the IIO buffer disabled until a client activates the sensor. Thus the zero scan-enable flags show the HAL rejected these names before channel setup, while buffer enable=0 by itself is expected during idle. This explains the empty framework sensor list; it is not a missing kernel driver, absent IIO character device, or permission issue. Permissive-mode AVC records on another IIO name are not the cause. The whitelist is code, not an XML mapping; the vendor image contains no matching source/config overlay, and this repo has no Android HAL build tree. No supported in-repo change can make the proprietary HAL enumerate these HID devices; do not rename a HID sensor to impersonate Chrome EC hardware or invent calibration/orientation data. A supported HAL source/build must map the Latitude's actual HID IIO channels, scale, axes and calibration, then be tested for event delivery. `usb/sensordiag.sh` records the runtime details; `tools/test_sensordiag.sh` covers its synthetic event-path report.

  Camera remains blocked on a compatible IPU6 driver/firmware/HAL path. `CameraService_proxy` reports an UID mismatch near teardown, but with no registered video device this does not establish a separate cause.

- 2026-11-11 **Latitude 7320 auto-rotation and camera follow-up:** the latest sensor capture confirms `/dev/iio:device0..8` nodes are present with `system:system` ownership and `iio_device` labels; `accel_3d` and `gyro_3d` expose live raw values and scan/trigger metadata. The vendor `Desktop-IIO-Subhal` binary has a fixed embedded name table for devices including `cros-ec-accel` and `cros-ec-gyro`; it does not include these HID names. This accounts for the framework's empty sensor list and why no scan channels or buffers become enabled. No editable mapping/config or HAL source is in the image/repository, so adding a fake name or guessed calibration is not a justified fix. A HAL source/build that explicitly supports this HID IIO layout is required.

  Camera logs now confirm Android `ueventd` attempted incompatible vendor `intel-ipu6.ko` and `ov8856.ko`. Inspection of the local vendor EROFS image found `/vendor/lib/modules` is an absolute symlink to `/vendor_dlkm/lib/modules`. The previous mask checked `/new/vendor/lib/modules`, causing the absolute symlink to resolve outside `/new` in stage0 and skip masking; this accounts for the failures after building the preceding image. `usb/mask_vendor_modules.sh` now detects that exact symlink and mounts tmpfs over `/new/vendor_dlkm/lib/modules`, the real mounted partition path. Its regression test uses the same absolute-symlink layout. This prevents Android's `/vendor/lib/modules` alias from exposing incompatible files, but does not add a driver. The GBOS payload has no IPU6/IPU6EP driver, OV8856 sensor driver, or matching firmware/config in this checkout; device `8086:9a19` remains unbound with no video or media nodes. Windows camera functionality does not supply the Linux driver/HAL path. Compatible IPU6 source/build, firmware and Android camera HAL support are still required.

- 2026-11-11 **Latitude 7320 18:12 retest:** sensors remain unchanged: `accel_3d`/`gyro_3d` provide live raw data but the framework still enumerates zero sensors and scan-channel enable bits remain zero. Source inspection established that the vendor HAL filters IIO devices by a compiled-in name whitelist before it configures scan channels; these HID names are not in the whitelist. Buffer disabled at idle is expected once a sensor is exposed; it is not evidence that the IIO driver is broken. The `hal_sensors_default` AVC for reading `iio:device7/name` is permissive and did not block this boot. An enforcing build would need a targeted access rule, but relaxing SELinux cannot correct the current whitelist mismatch.

  Camera `hwdetect` still reports no media/video devices. The new PCI `DependencyGraph unable to find module for modalias pci:v00008086d00009A19...` is an alias lookup miss: the GBOS modules database has no driver matching IPU6 `8086:9a19`. Earlier `intel-ipu6.ko`/`ov8856.ko` errors were rejected Google vendor modules with an incompatible module ABI. There is no matching IPU6/OV8856 source, config, firmware or Android camera HAL in this checkout, so no additional camera support change is justified.

  **Build-to-build difference:** the current helper resolves `/vendor/lib/modules -> /vendor_dlkm/lib/modules` and masks the real mounted target at `/new/vendor_dlkm/lib/modules`; preceding helper versions followed the absolute symlink outside `/new` and skipped masking. The latest missing-alias warning is consistent with the IPU6 module no longer being discoverable, but alone does not prove that the mask succeeded. Confirm that boot's hook log says `incompatible Google vendor modules masked at /new/vendor_dlkm/lib/modules`; the previous vendor `.ko` ABI errors should then be absent. The regression test models this exact symlink target.

- 2026-11-11 **Public driver/HAL source search for Latitude 7320 camera and rotation:** found source-level candidates, but no validated drop-in files; nothing was downloaded or added to the image.
  - **Camera:** Intel's [`ipu6-drivers`](https://github.com/intel/ipu6-drivers) targets Tiger Lake among other platforms, and its [`ipu.h`](https://github.com/intel/ipu6-drivers/blob/e52f5e8f1da6aac7063358ec85514d603e51e44e/drivers/media/pci/intel/ipu.h) explicitly defines IPU6 PCI ID `0x9a19`, matching this laptop's `8086:9a19`. That source tree also contains an [`ov8856.c` sensor driver](https://github.com/intel/ipu6-drivers/blob/e52f5e8f1da6aac7063358ec85514d603e51e44e/drivers/media/i2c/ov8856.c). Intel's [`ipu6-camera-hal`](https://github.com/intel/ipu6-camera-hal) includes OV8856 profile examples under `config/linux/ipu6ep`, but those are not proof that this board's sensor/module wiring matches; the detected PCI ID identifies IPU6, not IPU6EP. The HAL is a Linux camera stack, not an Android Camera3 provider, and its README requires Intel's separate [`ipu6-camera-bins`](https://github.com/intel/ipu6-camera-bins) firmware and proprietary processing libraries. The driver README says it was tested with Linux 6.10 and documents kernel patches/configuration; this does not establish a successful build against GBOS's custom 6.18.21 kernel. Thus these are concrete upstream starting points, not binaries that can safely be copied into `usb/`; Android camera provider integration, board-specific sensor metadata, firmware, kernel config/build compatibility, and on-device testing remain prerequisites.
  - **Auto-rotation:** [`diegonmarcos/cloudy-u-desktop`'s Waydroid sensor implementation](https://github.com/diegonmarcos/cloudy-u-desktop/tree/0b53d2fdc3e164689165ddde68e88b0da21c7307/z_archive/da_waydroid-sensors) is a source reference that discovers an IIO device by its `name` (configured as `accel_3d`), reads `in_accel_*_raw` and `in_accel_scale`, and publishes an Android accelerometer. It is explicitly for Waydroid and a Surface Pro 8: the service registers through Waydroid's host-side `libgbinder`/HIDL 1.0 path, unlike GBOS's vendor Sensors 2.1 multihal; its mount matrix also requires per-device calibration. It demonstrates a possible data-source approach, but cannot be installed unchanged and does not establish the Latitude's axis mapping. A GBOS implementation would still need integration with the existing Sensors HAL/service and verified calibration/event-delivery tests.
  - **Download constraint:** Intel's firmware/binary repository is public but contains the required runtime blobs. No firmware or images were fetched; obtain approval before downloading them, and confirm the exact sensor/profile and Android HAL integration before attempting a camera port.

- 2026-11-11 **Latitude 7320 sensor HAL hypothesis verified against the shipped vendor image:** extracted `/etc/sensors/hals.conf` from `lapis/parts/vendor_a.img`; it contains only `/vendor/lib64/android.hardware.sensors@2.1-Desktop-IIO-Subhal.so`. The publicly available Google IIO SensorsSubHal sources in the Trogdor and Kukui device trees confirm the behavior pattern: `load_iio_devices` applies a compiled-in exact-name filter before scanning channels; XML supplies metadata only and cannot add a supported device name. This source is not the exact Desktop binary source, but it explains the reported sequence and is consistent with the actual binary strings and IIO scan-enable state. Zero `buffer/enable` is expected while idle; zero scan-element enables plus an empty Android sensor list indicate the devices were not accepted/constructed.   **No safe runtime-only fix is available in this repository.** The proprietary HAL source and an Android Soong/HIDL build tree are absent, `hals.conf` only selects a library, and the optional sensor XML is not present and cannot override the compiled device filter. Renaming kernel IIO devices to impersonate Chrome EC hardware or patching the vendor binary would be unsafe. A proper fix requires source/buildable Sensors 2.1 sub-HAL support for this HID-IIO layout; before claiming auto-rotation, it must also apply a Latitude-validated axis/mount transform. No code change was made to the HAL, SELinux policy, or sensor axes. `tools/test_sensordiag.sh` still passes and covers the diagnostic path, not HAL enumeration.

- 2026-11-12 **HID sub-HAL event-path work:** the kernel's HID accelerometer and gyro drivers expose signed 32-bit IIO storage with per-report real bits and a separate 64-bit timestamp; both expose shared offset and scale. The experimental sub-HAL now validates the expected HID layout, decodes IIO real bits/shift/endian, applies `(raw + offset) * scale`, propagates buffer and frequency write errors, and avoids starting the event thread before its sensor is initialized. Host synthetic scan, mapping, scale/offset and error tests pass with address/undefined-behavior sanitizers. `sensordiag.sh` now captures `current_timestamp_clock`; a hardware HID timestamp can bypass that clock, so its relation to Android boottime remains unverified. No axis mapping, built Android HAL, or hardware event verification is claimed. With user approval, an Android 17 platform checkout was started under ignored `build/aosp-android17`; the local machine has 7.6 GiB RAM, below Google's documented 64 GiB full-build recommendation.

This does not depend on when `i2c-INT3472:07` appears. On preload failure it logs the error and stops coldplug rather than starting the PMIC out of order. - 2026-11-14 **Latitude 7320 latest sensor trace follow-up:** ran `tools/analyze_iio_trace.py` on `D:\gbos\logs\boot-1\sensors.txt`. The 587 scans span 180.277 seconds, but the median inter-scan interval is 100 ms (10 Hz); one gap of 121.770 seconds lies between scans 0 and 1. The first IIO timestamp is 184 ms before the diagnostic's trace-start marker and the last is 180.093 seconds after it. Thus the 3.251 Hz whole-trace average is caused by a single long gap, not steady 3.3 Hz delivery. A scan can therefore be queued before the marker, matching the first timestamp. The source does not explain the subsequent 121.770-second gap; `dd` blocks on the IIO character device, so the logs alone do not localize the gap to HID, kernel trigger, or read path. The analyzer now reports first/largest gaps and median rate separately, and compares sysfs rate against median rather than whole-trace average. The HAL remains disabled; no range/matrix inference. - 2026-11-16 **Latitude 7320 Goodix HAL trial:** the attached Live boot enumerated `27c6:6384`. Android reported interface 0 with bulk OUT `0x01` and IN `0x83`, both max packet size 64, matching the experimental backend endpoints. `gbos_fp` received repeated 64-byte transfers but logged each as an invalid packet, never registered the binder service, and `BiometricServiceRegistry` reported no sensors. The wire dump is not retained, so the exact declared frame length/CRC cannot be checked. libfprint v1.94.9 reads the response CRC at the header-declared frame boundary and tolerates bytes beyond that frame; our parser had required transfer length to equal the declared frame. It now validates CRC at the declared boundary and permits trailing transfer padding, with padded-64-byte and truncated-frame regression tests.

  Init also logged that CrosFP's AIDL interface definition was already defined by `gbos_fp`; that is an init lazy-service mapping collision, not proof of two successful binder registrations. `gbos_fp` was class-started before sensor probing completed, exited status 1 without registering, then init/servicemanager repeatedly started it while the framework retried service lookup. The generated GBOS service is now `disabled` and `oneshot`, so it starts on lazy interface requests rather than at `class_start hal` and does not auto-respawn after a failed probe. Camera-test image config must keep `FINGERPRINT=1`; the prior boot-1 image explicitly used 0. This source fix has host protocol/service tests and an image-matched Android link, but trailing-padding acceptance is not yet confirmed against a subsequent device response. AIDL v4 versus the CrosFP APEX's VINTF v5, firmware handshake, registration with Android, USB access under enforcing SELinux, and actual Settings detection remain unverified. Do not enroll or claim support until a later no-enrollment test reports a registered sensor; no live image/device storage was written by this change.

- 2026-11-16 **Goodix 19:03 boot matched to staged binary; protocol/reset follow-up:** the `gbos_fp` extracted from `build/camera-test-rgbir-awb-goodix-20261116/gbOS86-20261116.img` is byte-identical to the then-staged binary (SHA-256 `b26218e5791fae4ec7ce1b6aee2d28035da144c0faed87425eff3d3fe820f486`), so the later no-sensor result did exercise that build. Review against [libfprint v1.94.9 `goodix_proto.c`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/libfprint/libfprint/-/blob/v1.94.9/libfprint/drivers/goodixmoc/goodix_proto.c) found its header parser reads the CRC8/complement bytes but does not validate them; this HAL's stricter CRC8 check diverged. The parser now ignores those header checksum bytes while still validating the CRC32 at the declared frame boundary, with a regression for valid CRC32 plus nonmatching header CRC8. The HAL also issued `USBDEVFS_RESET` on its first open and again after packet/transport errors; that matches the observed reset loop and is absent from libfprint's interface-claim flow. The backend no longer resets the device, and a mock-open regression asserts that behavior. Failure logging now captures header metadata only (no packet payload), and the probe-exhaustion log includes the final error. The rebuilt Android x86-64 PIE at `usb/gbos_fp` has SHA-256 `95ea4e8c96c963082eafcab146a0729c5fd33ddbafdbd9f34ef7c9b0300eb901`; focused protocol/service tests, ASan/UBSan, shell syntax and whitespace checks pass. This is a source-backed correction, not a confirmed hardware fix: the retained boot has no raw header/CRC, so it cannot establish that the CRC8 check caused the rejection. Sensor firmware/protocol compatibility, AIDL v4 versus the image's VINTF v5, successful registration, enforcing-mode USB access, and Settings detection still require a later no-enrollment boot. Only the HAL binary was rebuilt; no image was assembled or deployed.

- 2026-11-16 **Latest Latitude 7320 boot explains the dead camera feed:** logs collected at 19:03–19:08 are from the preceding RGB-IR/Goodix diagnostic image. - 2026-11-17 **Latitude 7320 22:22–22:26 boot and V4L2 size correction:** the user still reports green preview. The USB HAL's `apply_capture_config` selected 1920x1080 YUYV while the bridge reported 640x480 output. The previous image contained the 640x480-only fake UVC descriptor, but `v4l2loopback` independently advertised a hard-coded six-mode frame-size list and initialized its format to 1920x1080. The Latitude has many IPU6 capture nodes and its front camera is reported to use a 4x4 RGB-IR mosaic, not the Surface Bayer pattern ([Latitude camera project](https://github.com/githomeserver/latitude-7320-camera)). The image lists an Android `usb/0` provider and the repo contains a loopback module, but provider discovery, supported formats, module compatibility with the actual #61 build, frame content and repeated stream shutdown are not established. A bridge needs a collision-free verified loopback node, appropriate RGB-IR processing, provider integration and a clean, repeatable capture lifecycle; none is demonstrated yet. No loopback was enabled, no image built, and no physical media or UEFI changed.

In libfprint `goodixmoc` (recalled; the sources are not in this checkout and were not downloaded), any nonzero `a3` status means duplicate, and the reply carries the matching template's finger id. **Likely cause:** yesterday's template for the same finger is still on the sensor as `GBOS-<yesterday's instance>-u?-<id>`. The tmpfs `/data` lost both the HAL's state and its random instance, so Android and the HAL know 0 enrolments, while the sensor's check-duplicate matches against its whole database. These are deleted: this `/data`'s templates for the session user that the HAL does not report to Android, but only when the user's state file was read completely (new `end` line) or is absent; `GBOS-L` templates of another instance; and old-style templates of another instance, only on a live boot with no `LABEL=GBOSSYS` install. - 2026-11-18 **Goodix enrolment fix finished and built (not yet on the stick or tested on hardware):** completes the WIP entry above. `gbos_fp.c`: `createSession` queues an internal `OP_RECONCILE`, so the sensor's unreachable templates are deleted before the session's first enrol (logged: `deleted sensor template <name>: <reason>` and `sensor templates for user N: S stored, D deleted, K kept, E enrollments known`). `op_enroll` runs the cleanup again if it could not run, and reopens the sensor if the cleanup hit a transport error. On `GOODIX_DUPLICATE`, `fp_on_duplicate()` (`gbos_fp_policy.h`) deletes the matching template when the policy allows. Otherwise it logs who owns it: another OS (e.g. Windows Hello), this user (same finger enrolled twice), another user, or another gbOS86 system. The enrol then ends with `ERR_UNABLE_TO_PROCESS` plus the sensor code, because AIDL v4 has no "already enrolled" error. Settings' generic enrol-error text ("try again or use a different finger", from AOSP; not checked in this build) is all the user sees, and the reason is in logcat. After any other refused enrol it deletes the template's own name if a refused commit stored it anyway. After a cancelled enrol it now settles, as identify already did: it reads past the abandoned command's late packets and switches the power-button shield off, so the next operation starts on an idle sensor. Open failures are now logged (`sensor open failed: N`; boot-10's HW_UNAVAILABLE left no line). `main` detects tmpfs `/data` with `statfs` (on failure it assumes persistent, which only ever keeps more templates), parses `--no-gbos-install` and logs both. `usb/hook.sh` adds `--no-gbos-install` only when both stage0's `$SYS` and a fresh `findfs LABEL=GBOSSYS` find no install. That AND is deliberately stricter than the WIP plan (findfs only): a missed install would lose its fingerprint, a missed cleanup only means an error message. The WIP `fp_name_parse` read 5 bytes with `memcmp` past the end of names shorter than "GBOS-" (ASan; harmless on the sensor's 57-byte uid buffer, but undefined): now `strncmp`. **Tests:** new `fingerprint/goodix_sim_test.c` runs against a simulated sensor. The simulator keeps a template database, answers each command with an acknowledgement and then a response, matches check-duplicate against every stored template whoever stored it, and can hold packets back to deliver them late after a cancel. It covers the name format, the 32-byte length limit and 13 rejected look-alikes, and the policy table (live and no install, live and install, persistent, untrusted state). It also covers the cleanup on live and install databases (Windows, other-user and enrolled templates are kept), the boot-10 failure (a3 status 2 with the old template named, then the cleanup lets the first attempt succeed), duplicates that must be kept, a refused commit, cancels in the b0:02 acknowledgement wait, in the response wait and in capture (settle then a working open or enrol), and the 5-stale-packet limit. Two mutants fail it: stale-packet skipping off, and old-style orphan deletion off. `tools/test_gbos_fp.sh` adds the hook flag in three cases: no install, an install seen by stage0, and one seen only by findfs. All pass under ASan/UBSan. It is in `fingerprint/build.sh`, so every build runs it. **Built** with `fingerprint/build_image.sh lapis/parts` (local docker images, no download): `usb/gbos_fp` SHA-256 `066f4734a471afdd8fa3624d62a05a25f33afeeb211f7c41ef5eb9674a90fffc` (was `f63b095a…`). **Not verified:** which of a3 or a4 returned 2 on boot-10 (the new per-step logs will say), the libfprint semantics (recalled: any nonzero a3 status is a duplicate and a4 fails only at >= 0x80; this HAL still treats any nonzero a4 status as a failure, and the sources were not downloaded), and the firmware's reply to a7:04 delete. **Next boot:** deploy `usb/gbos_fp` and `usb/hook.sh`, boot the stick, choose 1 (Update), enrol a finger, lock and unlock with it. Look for `starting Goodix HAL for 27c6:6384 (no gbOS86 install found)` in the hook log and, in logcat, `data tmpfs (live boot)`, `deleted sensor template GBOS-…` and `enrolled`.

- 2026-11-17 **Correction: the Latitude front OV5675 is RGB-IR after all; the grid came from a 3x3 demosaic window.** The diagnostic bridge's `cfa4x4` log (boot-5, 20:1x, stable across 6 samples) measured per-phase means above black of `G 169 | IR 21 | G 174 | IR 21` / `R 123 | G 172 | B 100 | G 173` / `G | IR | G | IR` / `B 100 | G | R 123 | G`: a 4x4 RGB-IR mosaic exactly matching the originally assumed map (IR at odd x/even y; R at (0,1),(2,3); B at (2,1),(0,3)), even though the driver reports `SGRBG10`. The earlier entry blaming the RGB-IR map was wrong. The real cause of the cyan/yellow grid: R and B are only 2 of 16 sites, so the 3x3 averaging window frequently held no R or no B site and read that channel as zero; the 4.05-pixel sampling step made those patches drift into a grid. The Bayer decode then read the IR sites as red (WB `mean R=21 G=172`, red gain clamped at 4.0), giving the cyan cast. The bridge now uses the measured RGB-IR map (refusing any bus code but GRBG10, since a flip would move the sites), demosaics over a 4x4 window that contains every site once (shifted inward at frame edges), subtracts the window's IR level from R/G/B, and balances white on IR-corrected means (AE still meters raw green). Tests: uniform-colour frames give chroma within ±2 of neutral at every output pixel for RGB-IR and Bayer, a negative control with the 3x3 window fails that test, IR added equally to all sites leaves a grey patch grey and unchanged in brightness; ASan/UBSan and suites pass. Cost 11.6 ms/frame single-threaded (i7-1180G7). Rebuilt `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` SHA-256 `01e91b0e431ab93f577825bdbccc6d4d474cce640660e3ec4845cce223d8ba54`. Not yet tested on hardware; grain at ~50x gain remains.

Rebuilt `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` SHA-256 `a2fe40efc634e4f21cf3f7ea75073b41712ac10f2acf66f3a11ab05da1baa935` (includes the RGB-IR 4x4 fix). (P2) stage0 skips the screen reader when no PCI class 0x0403 HDA controller exists (SOF-only machines never get a card that early; ~5 s per boot on the Latitude 7320). (S4) `gbos_hide_unimplemented_hals` bind-mounts filtered copies of `/system/etc/vintf/manifest/com.google.android.snapshot.xml` (keeps `IAqueduct/default`) and `com.android.desktop.video_chat.xml`, removing the `ISnapshot/default` and `IVideoChatManager/default` declarations nothing implements; it acts only when the file still declares that exact fqname, `HIDE_UNIMPLEMENTED_HALS=0` disables it. (P3) `gbos_coldplug` skips the 1 s settle after a pass that loaded nothing. S1 (stick unmount) not done: no late-shutdown hook and no fsck.fat on the stick (download would need approval). `test_ui_defaults.sh` already failed at `5380e2b`; two suites need a privileged container.
- 2026-11-17 **Latitude front camera after the RGB-IR fix: neutral but muted and grainy; saturation, chroma and temporal denoise added.** The user's 21:02 photo shows correct, cast-free but desaturated colours and heavy speckle (AE ~50x). The bridge now (1) applies a grey-preserving saturation of 1.6x in linear light (channel distance from Rec. 709 luminance, no calibrated CCM available), (2) box-filters U/V over 5x5 output pixels (R/B are 2 of 16 sites, so chroma noise dominates), and (3) runs a recursive temporal luma filter: changes up to 14 levels move a third of the way, larger changes pass through (no motion ghosting); it resets at start and whenever the bridge resumes from idle. Tests: warm/cool halves gain chroma with saturation, chroma variance of a noisy frame drops more than 4x, a small luma step is damped to a third and a large one passes unchanged; ASan/UBSan pass. Cost 16.1 ms/frame on the i7-1180G7 (only while a consumer streams). Rebuilt `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` SHA-256 `c3341ca18c327369a17e1c568620b4df25643756a89c5ec9c59d75a5e83b627c`. Tuning values (`saturation_q12`, `chroma_radius`, `TNR_THRESHOLD`) are untested on hardware.
- 2026-11-17 **Front camera is an OV5678; colour calibration taken from its Windows tuning file.** This Windows/WSL host is the Latitude 7320 Detachable itself (Win32_ComputerSystem `Dell Inc. / Latitude 7320 Detachable`). Windows PnP names the front sensor `Camera Sensor OV5678` (ACPI\OVTI5678, module `0BF501T3`), so Linux binds an RGB-IR OV5678 with the `ov5675` driver and its register tables; that may contribute to the ~50x gain need (unverified). The driver package `ov5678.inf_amd64_*` contains `OV5678_0BF501T3_TGL.aiqb` (identical to the YHUE variant) and `graph_settings_*.xml` (pipeline routing, confirms `rgb_ir` stages). The aiqb holds a colour-matrix table of 920-byte records: u32 illuminant id, raw white point R/G and B/G, CIE x/y, then 25 hue-sector 3x3 float matrices. Seven illuminants (ids 1 A, 6 D65, 4, 10, 7, 19, 12 horizon). Cross-check: boot-5's IR-corrected white point R/G 0.675, B/G 0.523 matches illuminant 10 (0.6735/0.506), so our IR subtraction agrees with Intel's. `tools/extract_aiqb_ccm.py` writes the per-illuminant mean matrices; the output is proprietary-derived and lives only on the stick at `gbos/camera/ov5678_ccm.txt` (gitignored, along with `*.aiqb`). The bridge takes it as an optional third argument (the launcher passes it when present), blends the two calibrated illuminants nearest the measured white point (inverse distance), applies the matrix to white-balanced linear RGB instead of the generic 1.6x saturation, and logs `ccm: illuminant A (w%) + B`. For boot-5's light it picks illuminant 10 at 67% + 19. Host tests use synthetic tables only. Rebuilt `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` SHA-256 `57fdffcae0da1c1c01c8d17d23f35bf00e351aef3cf9ca6cacf900b27eb119a1`; bridge, launcher and table copied to the stick and verified. Not yet seen on hardware. The aiqb likely also holds lens shading, black level and tone data (not extracted).
- 2026-11-17 **More of the OV5678 tuning file decoded and applied: lens shading; black level and sensor mode confirmed.** The `.aiqb` is a `CPFF` container (sections `LCMC`, `DFLT`, `AIQB`) of records `u32 size, u16 format (100/101/102/200), u16 record id`. Record 2 (general): 2592x1944, 10-bit. Record 3 (black level, by exposure and analogue gain up to 15.5x): 63.9–64.3 at 10 bits, so the bridge's fixed 64 is right. Record 25: the colour matrices already used. Record 28 (207,456 bytes): lens shading, header = Intel's own 4x4 CFA map `0 1 0 1 / 2 3 4 3 / 0 1 0 1 / 4 3 2 3` (G, IR, R, G, B: exactly the measured RGB-IR layout), 7 illuminants (ids 1, 4, 6, 7, 10, 19, 20 with their own raw white points), 5 channels, 63x47 u16 grids; corners need ~3.4–3.9x (G), ~4.3x (R), ~4.1x (B) relative to the centre; the IR grid is clamped at its maximum (unused here). `graph_settings_*.xml` shows Windows always streams the full 2592X1944 sensor mode (no binning) and halves resolution in its RGB-IR stage, so a binned mode is not an available noise fix. `tools/extract_aiqb_ccm.py --lsc` writes the grids normalised to 1.0 at the centre (green = mean of both green phases) to `gbos/camera/ov5678_lsc.txt` (stick only, gitignored). The bridge now takes the calibration directory (`/postinstall/gbos/camera`; a single matrix file still works), blends the two grids nearest the measured white point, eases correction strength from 100% at <=8x total sensor gain to 60% at 64x (to limit corner noise; our choice, not from the file), precomputes a per-output-pixel R/G/B gain map (rebuilt only when pick, weight or strength move by >0.05; 27 ms), and applies it to the window means before IR subtraction (IR sites take the green gain). Logs `lsc: illuminant A (w%) + B, strength S at sensor gain Gx`. For boot-5's light at ~50x: illuminants 10/19, strength 0.65, corner gain ~2.1. Tests use synthetic grids (map values, strength roll-off, a vignetted flat field converting flat; without correction the same field reads 207 centre vs 112 corner). 17.0 ms/frame. Bridge SHA-256 `a2f1115dd379285ae8593b18c9880040d023da163eb5444bf6cc4519d73f289e`; bridge, launcher and both tables on the stick, verified. Not yet seen on hardware.
- 2026-11-17 **Front camera with lens shading and calibrated colour: good overall; lamps turned black with a magenta rim, fixed.** The user's photo (21:4x) shows even illumination, much lower grain and natural colour, but a ceiling light rendered as a black blob with a magenta fringe. Cause: at the lamp every site clips, including the IR sites, so "clipped colour minus clipped IR" is zero (black); where only green clips first, the colour matrix turns the imbalance magenta. The bridge now tracks the peak raw level in each 4x4 window and, above 85% of full scale, fades the final linear colour towards white (pure white when a site is at full scale). Tests: a fully clipped RGB-IR area converts to Y>=250 neutral (it failed without the fade: negative control), green-only clipping comes out near-white without a strong tint, and mid grey is untouched. Bridge SHA-256 `c5064561e9c22bad29872cbf0b9fcbb3209ccb02a5fdf6daa05e73d7c2b454ca`, on the stick and verified. Not yet seen on hardware.
- 2026-11-17 **Windows-side survey of the Latitude 7320 (this WSL host) for build improvements.** Findings and what was done:
  - *Camera white balance:* grey-world is now constrained to the module's calibrated illuminant locus (the 7 white points from the OV5678 tuning file, ordered warm to cool): the measured point is projected onto the polyline and may stray at most 0.03 in (R/G, B/G), so one dominant colour in the scene no longer becomes a cast; the constrained point also drives the colour-matrix and lens-shading choice. Without a table grey-world is unchanged. The bridge logs grey-world, the used point and the locus distance (`ccm:` line). Tests use a synthetic locus. Bridge SHA-256 `15b0223dd00d3b592150ea8c320b98b50a3183f1ab5ba8404b6a02f2cca81053`, on the stick. Not yet seen on hardware.
  - *Thermal:* Android's Pixel thermal HAL (`vendor.thermal-hal`) uses `/vendor/etc/thermal/lapis/thermal_info_config.json` (selected by `thermal_profile_setter`), whose sensors are zone types `TSR0`–`TSR4` and `TCPU`; Windows Intel DTT lists this laptop's ACPI participants as `TCPU` (processor), `TSKN` (skin), `TMEM`, `CHRG`, `AMBF`, `NGFF`, and Linux loads `int3400_thermal`/`int3403_thermal`/`processor_thermal_device`. Only `TCPU` can match, which explains `ThermalHal cannot read any sensor data`. A Latitude config must map skin thresholds to `TSKN` (mapping them to `TCPU` would throttle at 60 C); `hwdetect.txt` now reports every thermal zone (type, temperature, policy, trip points) and cooling device so the config can be written from the next boot's data. Not yet done.
  - *Sensors:* Linux exposes nine HID IIO sensors (two `als`, `accel_3d`, `gyro_3d`, `magn_3d`, `incli_3d`, `dev_rotation`, `geomagnetic_orientation`, `relative_orientation`; Windows: six HID Sensor Collection V2 nodes on ISH `8087:0AC2/0AC3`), but Android registers only the accelerometer (sub-HAL `sensors/trogdor` accepts only the profiled accel). Adding the light sensor (adaptive brightness) and gyroscope needs a sub-HAL rebuild; no Windows values are required. Not done.
  - *Rear camera:* module `0BA801T3` (`HKLM\...\ACPI\OVTI8856\1\Device Parameters\DosDeviceName`); `tools/extract_aiqb_ccm.py` extracts its colour matrices from `OV8856_0BA801T3_TGL.aiqb`; its lens-shading record has a different (4-channel Bayer) layout the extractor does not yet handle. The laptop also has a DW5821e LTE/GNSS module and Broadcom NFC (Windows PnP), unused by the build.

- 2026-11-17 **Rear camera (OV8856, module 0BA801T3) calibration extracted; extractor generalised.** The OV8856 `.aiqb` has the same `CPFF` record container. Its lens-shading record (id 28) is Bayer: 4x4 map `0 1 / 2 3` with unused entries `0xff`, 4 channels, 7 illuminants, 63x47 grids, 6 bytes of record padding. The u16 after each illuminant's 8 zero bytes is *not* the channel count (it reads 5 in both files; the earlier front-camera parser only worked because that sensor has 5 channels); the record header's count is used now. Channel colours: the general record's colour-order field is 0 for the OV8856 (260 for the RGB-IR OV5678) and Linux reports `SGRBG10`, so the map is Gr R / B Gb; confirmed by the data (channels 0 and 3 agree to ~1-2%, channel 1 shows the strongest corner fall-off, 6.25x under illuminant A, the usual red colour shading). Grids are stored with 2048 = 1.0. Its colour-matrix table (record 25: u16 6 illuminants, u16 24, sector angles) includes an illuminant with id **0** (x,y 0.464/0.409, ~2600 K) that the extractor used to reject, truncating the table at 4; ids >= 0 are accepted now (6 illuminants: 19, 4, 13, 7, 0, 1). `tools/extract_aiqb_ccm.py --lsc` handles RGB-IR (5-channel) and Bayer GRBG (4-channel), refuses unknown colour orders and green grids that disagree; the front camera's outputs are byte-identical to before. New `tools/test_extract_aiqb.py` (synthetic bytes only) covers both layouts, padding, the refusals and id 0. `gbos/camera/ov8856_ccm.txt` and `ov8856_lsc.txt` are on the stick (gitignored) for a future rear-camera bridge; nothing uses them yet.
