DEAD_OBJECT`) at 08:43:31, during the user's shutdown after SurfaceFlinger was terminated: benign. *Thermal:* zones on Linux are `INT3400 Thermal`, `TMEM` (passive 70.05 C, hot 77.05), `TSKN` (45.05 / 83.05), `AMBF` (50.05 / 79.05), `NGFF` (55.05 / 85.05), `CHRG` (57.05 / 87.05), `TCPU` (active 109.05, critical 125.05), `iwlwifi_1`; eight `Processor` cooling devices. Google's `init.lapis.rc` selects `thermal/lapis/thermal_info_config.json` (zones `TSR0`–`TSR4`, a PID loop driving `package-0` PL1 between 13 and 25 W, a fan) and, in `on init` on every device, writes PL1 25 W / PL2 55 W to `intel-rapl:0` and `intel-rapl-mmio:0` and a TCC offset; `intel_rapl_msr`/`processor_thermal_rapl` load in stage0 (27–31 s), so on this 7–15 W i7-1180G7 those writes succeeded (only the TCC write failed). The hook now bind-mounts a copy of `init.lapis.rc` without the powercap/TCC writes on every machine (`KEEP_LAPIS_POWER_LIMITS=1` keeps them), and on DMI `Latitude 7320 Detachable` bind-mounts `usb/thermal/latitude7320_thermal_info_config.json` over both HAL config paths: monitoring only (VIRTUAL-SKIN on TSKN LIGHT 45 / EMERGENCY 80, CPU on TCPU 90 / 100, DDR on TMEM 70 / 75, CHARGE on CHRG 57 / 84; no PID, cooling devices or SHUTDOWN level). PowerHAL's budget manager disables itself (no `thermal_powerbudgets.json`). `hwdetect.txt` now also records the RAPL constraints before Android, so the next boot shows Dell's firmware limits and confirms they stay. All untested on hardware.
- 2026-11-18 **Rear camera (OV8856) exposed to Android as the back camera (deployed to the stick, not yet seen on hardware).** *How the USB camera HAL decides facing* (strings of `/vendor/bin/hw/android.hardware.camera.provider-usb-service.android-desktop`, Google's Rust `hal_usb`, plus its config): built-in cameras are looked up by USB `VID:PID` in `/vendor/etc/camera/internal_usb_camera_info.txtpb` (lens info, sensor size, `facing`); every entry there is `FACING_FRONT`. The front works as Front because its fake identity `0408:4052` (Quanta YHVA-5) is one of them. The proto has `FACING_BACK`, and the HAL rejects two cameras with the same facing (`... already exists, multiple camera with the same facing?`). *Changes:* (1) `kernel/ipu6-loopback.c` takes `--sensor ov5675|ov8856` (sensor profile: media entity, RGB-IR vs Bayer, calibration prefix `ov5678_`/`ov8856_`) and `--rotate 0|180`; without options it is the front exactly as before (old and new source give byte-identical output for RGB-IR and Bayer test frames). The rear takes its 2x2 Bayer order from the bus code (GRBG10; a flip would report BGGR). AE: the OV8856's digital gain is 0..4095 with default 1024 = 1x, which the old range check refused; a digital gain whose minimum is 0 now uses its default as unity (AE range 1x..4x; exposure 6..2482 lines and analogue 128..2047 as reported). The OV8856 black level is 64, as for the front (tuning record 3: 16406/256). (2) `ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` loads v4l2loopback once with `video_nr=64,65` (labels `GBOS_IPU6_Camera`, `GBOS_IPU6_Rear_Camera`; the 640x480 bounds are module-wide), starts the front as before, and only once the front streams starts `ipu6-loopback --sensor ov8856` on `/dev/video65` (log `camera-bridge-rear-runtime.txt`). Only after the rear streams does it give video65 a fake UVC identity `1209:0001` (pid.codes test id; new `usb/surface/latitude_rear_fake_uvc_desc.bin` = the front descriptor with only idVendor/idProduct changed, `tools/make_latitude_uvc_desc.py --usb-id`). It then bind-mounts a copy of the HAL's camera table with one added `FACING_BACK` entry for that id: focal length 2.83 mm and 1.12 µm pixels from the OV8856 tuning file's record 13 (the OV5678's record reads 2.22 mm, the same layout); the aperture of 2.0 is assumed. Then it restarts the HAL and cameraserver once. If the rear fails, it logs a warning and the front runs alone; `CAMERA_REAR=0` in gbos.cfg disables the rear, and `CAMERA_REAR_ROTATION=180` turns its picture (the ov8856 driver exposes no rotation control). *Concurrency:* front (CSI2 6) and rear (CSI2 1) use separate CSI2 receivers and ISYS capture nodes, and both stream until reboot. Two sensors streaming at once on this IPU6 has **never been tested**: the earlier one-sensor-per-boot rule was about the capture probe's cleanup, not a measured conflict. Watch for a front failure after the rear starts. The HAL may now number the cameras by facing (back 0, front 1). Not done: autofocus (the DW9714 stays at its probe position; the entry claims 0.30 m minimum focus); output stays 640x480 for both. Rear conversion with the real stick calibration tables (6 matrices, 7 shading grids, 63x47) gives a neutral grey at the calibrated white point, about 20 ms/frame (host). Bridge SHA-256 `332d96aa8643310f082cb74f6de158868f079fa6e7759c711c8ec34d7dc87857`. The bridge, launcher and rear descriptor are on the stick, hash-verified. New tests cover profiles, CFA selection, argument parsing, OV8856 AE ranges, rotation and the calibration prefix (ASan/UBSan clean). The launcher test runs the launcher's own uevent, facing and config functions against a synthetic table, and a mutation check showed it catches a changed front id, a wrong facing and a rear failure that exits. Aside: `install.sh`'s Update `DIRS` has no `camera`, so an NVMe install gets no calibration tables (front or rear); camera tests so far are Live boots.
- 2026-11-18 **Front camera OV5678 (Windows) vs ov5675 (Linux): same sensor control; bridge AE now follows the Windows exposure program.** Full comparison in `docs/ov5678-windows-vs-linux.md` (reproduce with `tools/compare_ov5678_windows.py`; Windows files stay local). Disassembling `ov5678.sys` shows the chip-ID check accepts `0x5675` or `0x5678` (Linux's `0x5675` check passes), and exposure (`0x3500` = lines x 8; VTS stretched to lines + 4), analogue gain (`0x3508`, raw) and digital gain (MWB `0x5019/1b/1d`, group hold) use the same registers and units as Linux: the ~50x gain is not a unit mismatch. The four Windows mode tables equal Intel's OV5675 table except: an equivalent PLL (`0x0300-0x030d`, both 900 Mbit/s/lane, 30 fps), VTS 2000 vs 2020, `0x5000` = 0x71 vs 0x77 (bits 1-2 cleared; function not verified) and extra `0x3d8c/0x3d8d` = 0x71/0xe7. The real difference is the exposure program: the `.aiqb` black-level record is calibrated up to 100 ms and 15.5x, and the AE exposure-program record 261 caps analogue gain at 15.5x and lets the automatic mode reach 66.7 ms, while Linux sat at 33.6 ms and 16x x 3.3x digital (boot-8). The bridge now caps analogue gain at 15.5x (1984) and plans exposure to 30 fps max, analogue to 4x (our choice, matches our reading of the record), then stretches the frame with `V4L2_CID_VBLANK` up to 2x (4036 lines, 15 fps), then analogue to 15.5x, then digital; VBLANK is written before a longer / after a shorter exposure (the driver bounds exposure by the frame), restored on exit, and skipped when absent or inconsistent. Lens-shading strength uses analogue x digital gain. New `ae: vblank` start line and `vblank=`/frame-time/gain on the periodic `ae:` line. Host tests (plan order, cap, return to 30 fps, write order, fallbacks), ASan/UBSan and the docker suite pass. Bridge SHA-256 `d99ac0f97d3a98a0c4c284f709b3b04de87e4dd94a5b280242293d63c4532bb4`. Not changed: `0x5000`/`0x3d8c-d` (would need a rebuilt `ov5675.ko` and an A/B test; clearing defect correction could expose hot pixels, the bridge has no DPC), AE target. Unverified on hardware: 15 fps through IPU6 and the Android USB HAL, actual noise gain, flicker at 66.7 ms.
- 2026-11-18 **OV5678 Windows register values as an A/B switch; merged with the rear-camera bridge.** `camera_7320.py` adds the `ov5675` parameter `gbos_windows_isp` (bool, 0644, default off). At stream start, and only on DMI Latitude 7320 Detachable `i2c-OVTI5678:00`, it writes the Windows driver's `0x5000=0x71`, `0x3d8c=0x71`, `0x3d8d=0xe7` after the mode table. Every stream start logs `gbos: Windows ISP values on|off: 0x5000=.. 0x3d8c=.. 0x3d8d=..` with the values read back. `ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` sets the parameter from `OV5678_WINDOWS_ISP=1` in `gbos.cfg` (default 0) and logs `OV5678 Windows ISP values: on/off` in `camera-bridge-setup.txt`. Only `ov5675.ko` was rebuilt in `gbos-ksrc`, whose bzImage is byte-identical to the stick's `BOOTX64.EFI` (#61); vmlinux and `.version` are unchanged. The stray 139-byte `/build/src/.config` (a SoundWire config fragment from 2026-11-04) blocks out-of-tree builds; it was moved aside for the build and put back. The module was stripped and signed with the tree key (`sig_key 2F:5D:...`, the same as the deployed modules); its `__versions` CRCs equal the deployed module's, plus `param_ops_bool` (matches vmlinux). New module SHA-256 `40d9cbda0806460bb138bff3c76ec366e2ae84065d1f1bd52a235282f05d6664`; the previous stick module (`4b258aa5...`) is kept in ignored `build/ov5675-windows-isp/`. The bzImage did not change, so no QEMU ISA run was needed. The stick already carried the rear-camera launcher from `claude/cool-almeida-1c503b` (per-sensor bridge processes, `--sensor ov8856`). The earlier AE-only bridge binary would not have run its rear instance, so that branch is merged here. Its control-query rework gains the VBLANK default. Its OV8856 AE test now sets frame limits and expects the 15.5x cap, which the OV8856 Windows tuning also uses (record 261: analogue 15.5x, 66.7 ms, breakpoint 4). Merged bridge SHA-256 `9d16fa8de0ba42727ae9aba2e2e9d7c845d5ecb8189b7a3376f8f5d0ec1374db`. Host tests, ASan/UBSan and both docker suites (`test_ipu6_loopback.sh`, `test_ipu6_camera_bridge.sh`) pass. Not tested on hardware.
- 2026-11-18 **Boots 9 and 10 (Latitude 7320): both camera bridges died on the first bad IPU6 frame; fixed in the bridge and launcher (not yet deployed).** Logs are in `build/boot-logs/boot-9`, `boot-10`, reported by the "Boot log errors" session.
  - *Boot 9* (Windows ISP values off, `0x5000=0x77`): the front looked good. AE ran at 4–6.6x gain with a stretched 46–67 ms frame, so the frame-stretch plan worked on hardware. Both bridges then logged `invalid or error-marked IPU6 frame` and exited. The kernel shows `Transfer FIFO overflow` and `Inter-frame long/short packet discarded` on csi2-6 (front) and csi2-1 (rear) at the same moment (182.9 s), about 49 s after both sensors started streaming at full resolution. Nothing else happened just before it. Boot-8 (front only) streamed 9 minutes without such errors, so the overflow is most likely from two concurrent full-rate streams (front 2592x1944, rear 3280x2464; neither can be stopped).
  - *Boot 10* (`OV5678_WINDOWS_ISP=1`, the driver confirms `0x5000=0x71 0x3d8c=0x71 0x3d8d=0xe7`): the front got `Frame sync error`, `SOT sync error` and `HSIDLE detected` on csi2-6 0.6 s after its stream start, and 14 ms after the rear bridge opened its device, i.e. while the rear sensor powered up on the shared TPS68470. It exited on that frame. The launcher then exited and init's group kill took the rear down (its `stream stop/close time out` comes from that teardown). So it is not established that the Windows values break the front; the timing equally fits the rear power-up. The switch stays default-off; the stick still has `OV5678_WINDOWS_ISP=1` until the next deploy.
  - *Bridge fixes:*
    - Error-marked or short frames are requeued and skipped, and the last converted picture is rewritten for an active consumer. Logs: `ipu6: <kind> frame ... requeued` for the first 3 of a run and every 100th, and `ipu6: recovered after N bad frame(s)`.
    - The bridge exits (status 3) only after 10 s without a good frame, counting poll timeouts too. An invalid buffer index stays fatal.
    - While a bridge has no consumer, its sensor runs at an 8x frame (VBLANK; front 14216 = about 3.75 fps; rear 8x its default, capped at the control's maximum). This cuts the time both sensors transfer at once, and so the chance of an overflow, to about an eighth. AE keeps running at that rate, and the planned frame returns as soon as a consumer streams. Logs: `ae: idle frame/active frame: vblank N`.
  - *Launcher fixes:* `ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` supervises both bridges. A bridge that exits is restarted after 5 s, up to 3 times per sensor, appending to its runtime log; the loopback nodes and UVC identities stay. One bridge exiting no longer ends the script or kills the other. Whether a restart can stream again after the IPU6 stop timeouts is unverified.
  - *Tests:* host tests (idle frame order and cap; bad-frame give-up timing, including timeouts and recovery), ASan/UBSan, `test_ipu6_loopback.sh` and `test_ipu6_camera_bridge.sh` pass. The latter has new supervision checks, with a negative control (an `exit` in the loop fails it).
  - Bridge SHA-256 `3041aa98905f624d1a02c652834a495b11563d6dbc7c7b99cec6586c7da507d5`, launcher `c97d828461bbcd677fd4933f2e83fbf193c1c27e67d8a5ddd6dd1d000c0e7e87`.
  - *Unverified:* whether overflows still occur with an idle partner at 3.75 fps; whether a stream continues normally after an overflow or sync error (now observable through the `recovered` lines); restarts after IPU6 stop timeouts.
- 2026-11-18 **The gbos FAT volume is remounted read-only at shutdown; boot reports the previous shutdown and runs a read-only fsck (S1 of `docs/os-diagnostic-2026-11-17.md`; untested on the laptop).** Every boot logged `FAT-fs (sda2): Volume was not properly unmounted`. Boot-8's `kmsg.log` shows why. Android init's `DoReboot` stops every service, logs `init: sync() before umount...` (557.278 s) and `Ready to unmount apexes`, unmounts the APEXes, lists the `Remaining userspace processes except init` (stage0's two loop subshells, the kmsg copier `busybox cat` and the sync loop's `sleep`), and sends `sysrq: Terminate All Tasks` (557.594 s, 316 ms later) before `reboot()`. It never touches the stick: it only unmounts `/dev/block/*` mounts, and super.img is loop-mounted from the volume, so `umount` is impossible anyway. Two kernel facts (6.18 `fs/fat/inode.c`, confirmed in a privileged container on a loop vfat): (1) a superblock remount read-only (`busybox mount -t vfat -o remount,ro DEV /gbos`, no MS_BIND) clears the dirty flag and also makes the `/postinstall` bind read-only. It works from a root with no `/proc` and no `/dev` (busybox doesn't look the source up on a remount; vfat ignores remount options) and returns EBUSY while any file is open for writing. (2) A volume that was already dirty when mounted is never cleaned by Linux (`sbi->dirty`), so the fix alone would never show; a 1-byte clear through the block device while mounted is coherent and persists.
  - *Shutdown:* `hook.sh` sources the new `usb/stick_fat.sh` and starts `gbos_fat_start_shutdown_watch`: a background child of stage0, like the copier, so init's service shutdown leaves it alone. It opens `/dev/kmsg` (read and write fds) and stderr→`/dev/null` before switch_root (afterwards only `/gbos` and `/gbos/busybox` are reachable; a `2>/dev/null` there would make ash skip the command). It reads kmsg through `busybox cat | awk` until a record `;init: sync() before umount` or `;init: Ready to unmount apexes` (anchored at the record's `;`), then SIGSTOPs the copier's subshell, kills its `cat` (found at hook time by cmdline `/gbos/busybox cat`; the subshell is its parent), appends `gbos-fat: shutdown: ... (end of this log)` to `kmsg.log`, syncs and remounts read-only (5 tries, 50 ms apart). On failure the reason goes to the end of `kmsg.log`, which is still writable then. An `on shutdown` rc action was rejected: it runs before init stops the writers, and it would lose the 4 s of shutdown log. `install.sh` (Update/installer) did `poweroff -f`/`reboot -f` with the stick mounted rw too; all six now go through `fat_off`, which does the same release first, and `install.sh` runs the boot check below at its start (hook.sh does not run in install mode). gbos.cfg `FAT_SHUTDOWN_RO=0` turns the watcher off.
  - *Boot:* `gbos_fat_boot_check` (start of `hook.sh`, before any binding/writing) takes the previous shutdown's state from the kernel's mount message in dmesg (stage0's earlier read-only mount prints nothing). When it was not clean (`FAT_FSCK=auto`; `1` every boot, `0` never) it pauses the copier, runs `df` (the driver recounts free clusters, so a stale FSInfo hint is rewritten), syncs, and runs `fsck.fat -n` (read-only) on the mounted volume. If the output has nothing but the dirty bit (plus its FAT32 backup-sector difference at that byte, and the FSInfo hint), it clears bit 0 of the state byte (offset 65 FAT32 / 37 FAT12/16) and reads it back. Otherwise it changes nothing and says to repair on another computer. Output: `logs/boot-N/fat-check.txt`, and `hook: FAT-CHECK /dev/sda2: previous shutdown ...; ...` in `stage0-dmesg.txt`. vold still never sees the stick.
  - *fsck.fat:* with the user's approval, `usb/installer/bin/fsck.fat` comes from Alpine 3.24 `dosfstools` 4.2 (71,576 bytes, SHA-256 `43d53b9514a76255b27d0053ffb737094d5b5e6443488373691383049144acfc`; the `alpine:3.24` docker image, ~4 MB, was pulled for it). It is musl 1.2.6 like the stick's loader and runs under it. `tools/fetch_installer_tools.sh` now fetches it too. dosfstools 4.2 `-n` exits 1 for a dirty bit alone; the accepted lines are in `gbos_fsck_only_dirty`.
  - *Tests:* `tools/test_stick_fat.sh` covers real FAT32/16 images, the real fsck.fat (dirty-only, lost cluster, FSInfo hint, truncated output), a fake `/proc` copier, a FIFO as `/dev/kmsg` (lookalike lines, EOF restart, no wait for a further record), EBUSY retries and `install.sh`'s `fat_off`. `BUSYBOX=<stick busybox> FAT_KERNEL_TEST=1` repeats it with busybox 1.37 applets plus the privileged-docker kernel test. All pass, as do `test_boot_tuning.sh` and the other tests that read `install.sh`/`hook.sh`.
  - *To check on the laptop:* the first boot after deploying (live or Update) still says `not properly unmounted` (boot-8 ended dirty) and should log `FAT-CHECK ... previous shutdown NOT clean; fsck.fat -n: no errors besides the dirty flag; dirty flag cleared`. Then shut down from Android's power menu. On the next boot there must be no `Volume was not properly unmounted` in `stage0-dmesg.txt`, `FAT-CHECK ... previous shutdown clean; fsck not needed`, and the previous boot's `kmsg.log` should end with `gbos-fat: shutdown: init reached its unmount step; ... (end of this log)` (a `remount ... FAILED:` line after it means a writer was still open). A forced power-off (holding the button) still leaves it dirty; the next boot's fsck handles that. Not covered: the dual-boot NVMe install's GBOSSYS holds `metadata.img` on loop12 read-write, so its remount gets EBUSY (logged); `KVM_TRACE=1`'s `dd` writer does the same while it runs.
- 2026-11-18 **Boots 9 and 10 (Latitude 7320): the shutdown read-only remount did not clean the stick. Three causes, all fixed in `usb/stick_fat.sh` and `usb/hook.sh` (untested on the laptop).** Both `fat-check.txt` said `previous shutdown NOT clean`, and fsck reported `Dirty bit is set`, `65:01/00` and `Reclaimed 1 unused cluster (4096 bytes)`. Both `kmsg.log` end with the watcher's `gbos-fat: shutdown: init reached its unmount step; ... (end of this log)` right after init's `sync() before umount...`, so the watcher did run at the right moment. `pstore-prev/` is empty: the only pstore backend is `efi_pstore`, which records panics, not shutdowns. Findings, each reproduced on the real vfat driver in a privileged container:
  1. *The remount always failed with EBUSY.* `hook.sh` looped `gbos/bootdisk.img` (the boot HAL's fake boot disk) read-write on loop6 with `losetup -P`. The kernel holds that backing file open for writing until the loop is detached, and `sb_prepare_remount_readonly` refuses while any mount of the volume has a writer. Container test: a busybox rw loop on a file of the volume gives EBUSY, and detaching it makes the remount work.
  2. *Why no `FAILED` line was written.* The old path appended its start line (a close sleeps 100 ms with `flush`: `fat_file_release`), then made 5 tries 50 ms apart, and only then wrote the reason. That took ~450 ms in the container. Init sends sysrq "terminate all tasks" 316 ms after the marker (boot-8), and the watcher did not ignore SIGTERM, so it died first. A missing FAILED line is not evidence of success.
  3. *The boot check never cleared the old dirty flag, so even a working remount could not clean the stick.* The lost cluster was fsck's own output file. `fat-check.txt.tmp` was on the volume being checked: the driver puts the new file's first cluster in the FAT at once, but writes the directory entry's start cluster only at inode writeback, so fsck sees an allocated cluster no file owns. Reproduced on a freshly made image (no damage at all): `Reclaimed 1 unused cluster` with the output on the volume, nothing with it in /tmp. `gbos_fsck_only_dirty` counted that as a real problem and kept the flag. Linux never clears a flag that was set at mount (`sbi->dirty`, `fat_set_state`), so the stick could not become clean. It is not old damage: the file was deleted after each check, and the cluster with it.
  - *Fixes:* the fake boot disk runs on a RAM copy (`/tmp/bootdisk.img`, 8 MB; the boot HAL's writes are no longer kept, as on a freshly written stick; `BOOTDISK_RAM=0` restores the old loop). The boot check writes fsck's output and its report to RAM, and writes `fat-check.txt` once afterwards. Lost clusters (FATs agreeing) count as harmless, since left allocated they only waste space. The dirty flag is no longer cleared at boot while the volume is mounted rw (that would hide a crash in this boot): `GBOS_FAT_CLEAR=1` tells the watcher to clear it right after the read-only remount, through a block-device node. The watcher ignores SIGTERM/HUP. Before switch_root, `hook.sh` sets up a 1 MB tmpfs at `/.gbos-fat`, which switch_root leaves alone, with proc, sysfs, a node `blk` for the volume and `null`. At the marker the watcher writes its line to kmsg (the copier copies it to `kmsg.log` within 50 ms) and stops the copier. It then detaches read-write loops backed by the volume (`losetup -d`) and remounts (30 tries, 50 ms apart). It checks the superblock state in mountinfo and clears the flag if asked. On the success path nothing is written to the volume. If the remount keeps failing, `fat-shutdown.txt` gets the error, every process fd open for writing on the volume (same `st_dev`, fdinfo flags) and the rw loops. The watcher then tries sysrq `s`+`u` (emergency remount: `SB_FORCE` skips the writer check) and waits up to 3 s for `ro`. It skips this while a rw loop is still attached, such as the dual-boot install's `metadata.img` under `/metadata`; `FAT_SHUTDOWN_SYSRQ=0` turns it off. `install.sh`'s `fat_off` uses the same path.
  - *Evidence across the reboot:* at boot, `logs/boot-N/fat-shutdown.txt` gets an `armed at boot: ...; clear the dirty flag then: yes|no (kmsg copier ...; read-write loops on it now: ...)` line. Failures are appended at shutdown while the volume is still writable. The next boot's `fat-check.txt` quotes the previous boot's `fat-shutdown.txt` and the watcher line at the end of its `kmsg.log`, then concludes with one of: `=> it left the volume read-only and clean` / `failed (lines above)` / `started but did not finish: killed or cut off by reboot()` / `already mounted it dirty and ... did not allow clearing` / `never started`. `install.sh`'s `save_logs` copies whole boot directories, so `nvme-logs/` includes both files.
  - *Tests:* `tools/test_stick_fat.sh` covers lost clusters in one or both FATs, the boot check writing nothing to the volume, the previous-shutdown summary, the watcher surviving SIGTERM, loop detach before the remount, the flag cleared after it, and writers/loops/sysrq on EBUSY. A delayed "kworker" covers the emergency remount, plus `FAT_SHUTDOWN_SYSRQ=0`. With `FAT_KERNEL_TEST=1` it also runs the kernel cases (rw-loop EBUSY, fsck's self-made lost cluster) and an end-to-end case. That case starts from a dirty image mounted rw with a rw loop on it and runs the boot check and watcher in a chroot. It then emulates switch_root (`/bin`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev` gone), sends SIGTERM to the watcher and writes init's marker. It checks that the loop is detached, the volume read-only, the flag cleared, `fsck.fat -n` rc 0, and later writes kept. All pass with the stick's busybox 1.37. `test_boot_tuning.sh` and the other hook/install tests pass. `test_ui_defaults.sh` already failed at bf5a6ea (`disable_window_blurs`), unrelated.
  - *To check on the laptop* (deploy `usb/stick_fat.sh`, `usb/hook.sh`, `usb/install.sh`, `usb/gbos.cfg`; no kernel rebuild, `stage0_init.sh` is unchanged). First boot: `fat-check.txt` should say `NOT clean`, show no `Reclaimed` line, and end with `the flag is cleared at shutdown, after the read-only remount`. `stage0-dmesg.txt` should have `bootdisk on loop6 (/tmp/bootdisk.img)` and the watcher line without `missing in /.gbos-fat`. `fat-shutdown.txt` should say `armed ... clear the dirty flag then: yes` with no read-write loops. Shut down from Android's menu. Next boot: no `Volume was not properly unmounted` in `stage0-dmesg.txt`, `FAT-CHECK ... previous shutdown clean`, and `fat-check.txt` should show `=> it left the volume read-only and clean`. Any other conclusion there, or lines after `armed` in the previous `fat-shutdown.txt`, says what is still wrong.

- 2026-11-18 **Goodix delete after boot-11: verified deletes, delete with the listed record, restart after a duplicate (built, not deployed):**
  - *Why it looked deleted:* `goodix_delete()` returned success both when the template was not in the sensor's list (`GOODIX_NORECORD`) and when the sensor answered "no such record" (0x9c), and nothing checked the list afterwards. So "deleted it" only meant "no error reply".
  - *Why the session-start cleanup logged nothing:* boot-11's logcat starts at 21:13:50, but `gbos_fp` started at boot+36.7 s, earlier, so those lines rotated away. The enrol path ran no second cleanup, so `ss->reconciled` was true: the session-start cleanup had counted its delete of `GBOS-1c2ac773-u10-5cfb789b` as done, the same false success.
  - *What the sensor answered* is still unknown: success but kept, 0x9c, or a list without the template.
  - *Delete:* `goodix_delete_template()` sends a7:04 with the finger id record exactly as the sensor listed it (new `goodix_template.raw`), then the re-encoded record if that differs. After each attempt it lists again and succeeds only when the name is gone; after a success reply it waits 100 ms and lists once more.
  - *Delete format and libfprint:* the re-encoded record matches libfprint's recalled `encode_finger_id`: 67, type 1, finger 1, 0, 32-byte account id, 32-byte tid, uid length, uid, 0. The firmware may keep its own bytes in a stored record, in which case only an exact copy would match. Our 2-byte length prefix is the same one our commit uses, and that commit was accepted on 2026-11-17. libfprint's sources were not downloaded to confirm any of this.
  - *Logs:* each attempt logs `delete <uid> (listed|re-encoded record, N bytes, type T, finger F): status 0xSS` and, if it stays, `still listed after status 0xSS`. The cleanup logs every stored template with its verdict, and a duplicate the list lacks is reported as such.
  - *Restart:* `fp_enroll()` (`gbos_fp_policy.h`) restarts the enrolment after a verified delete of a deletable duplicate, at most twice (`enrollment restarts: touch the sensor again`). Settings' progress starts over.
  - *`fingerprint.txt`:* `usb/hook.sh` adds `gbos_fp_log`, a logcat of tag `gbos_fp` only, to `logs/boot-N/fingerprint.txt`, started with the HAL, so startup and cleanup lines survive logcat rotation.
  - *Tests:* the simulator models firmware that keeps its own account id and deletes only on an exact record (the listed record works, the re-encoded one gets 0x9c), firmware that acknowledges a delete and keeps the template, and firmware that answers 0x9c. It covers the boot-11 restart, a check-duplicate naming a template the list lacks, and two restarts then giving up. A mutant that trusts the delete reply fails them. The old `goodix_test.c` passed a record length one byte past its buffer (now caught by ASan, because the parser copies the raw record): fixed. All suites pass under ASan/UBSan.
  - *Built:* `usb/gbos_fp` SHA-256 `0abf97395a734fbd550ffe0516ab0e102ce5ba57d8038042caf7a5d8900fe00e`. Not deployed.
  - *Next boot:* deploy `usb/gbos_fp` and `usb/hook.sh`, Update, enrol, unlock. Read `fingerprint.txt`. If the delete still leaves the template listed with status 0x00 in both forms, the firmware ignores this delete and the libfprint sources are needed. A full clear (a7:01) is not an option: it would erase Windows' templates.

- 2026-11-18 **Boot-11 camera follow-up: front green cast and noise; rear focus.** Logs are in `build/boot-logs/boot-11`; the user's photos were reported by the "Boot log errors" session.
  - *No code regression in the front colour path.* A host simulation fed a 2592x1944 RGB-IR frame with boot-11's measured per-site levels (frame 900: G ~178, R 122, B 105, IR 19 above black) and the stick's calibration tables (local only) through the bridge from 4f76a65 (the 2026-11-17 21:4x "good" build) and from febba32. Both produce byte-identical output, and a flat grey comes out neutral at the centre (U −0.3, V 0.0); CCM 10+19 and lens shading are active as on hardware. The one colour-path difference, the white-point locus constraint (bf89f85), did not engage (distance 0.015 < 0.03 band).
  - *The green comes from noise.* The bridge averaged chroma after the sRGB gamma curve. Gamma is concave, and R and B are much noisier than G (2 of 16 sites each, IR subtracted, then the colour matrix: B row 2.32, −0.80 G), so noisy R and B lose more than G and greys turn green. Simulated at σ_G ≈ 40 DN, the centre grey moved to U −3.6, V −2.4, growing in darker areas. The same mechanism existed in the "good" build, so boot-11's light (dim room at night, 14–19x gain, 66.7 ms frames) is the likely difference; the earlier photo was in another light.
  - *Fix:* colour is now built in linear light. Each output pixel keeps its own linear luminance but takes the R:G:B ratios of a linear-light neighbourhood average. The neighbourhood grows with total sensor gain: 5x5 up to 8x, 7x7 up to 16x, 9x9 above. Above 8x, colour is also faded towards grey across strong luma edges (up to 70% removed), which targets the purple/green speckle where the 4x4 window straddles an edge. The temporal luma threshold grows with √(gain/8), up to 2x (14 → 28).
  - *Simulated effect at 17.5x and σ ≈ 40 DN:* chroma bias U −3.6/V −2.4 → −0.5/+0.1; chroma noise sdU 3.6 → 1.2, sdV 3.0 → 1.1; luma sd 9.7 → 7.1. Converter 20 → 27 ms/frame on this i7-1180G7 (budget 67 ms at high gain, 33 ms at ≤4x where it is ~23 ms).
  - *Tests:*
    - a noisy RGB-IR grey stays within ±1 U/V, while the per-pixel/gamma-domain path (negative control) shows U < −2;
    - a neutral dark/bright edge keeps |U|,|V| ≤ 2 against ≥ 5 per pixel;
    - the gain steps.
  - *Rear:* `ov8856 ... Instantiated dw9714 VCM` and `camera.txt` show the `dw9714` driver bound, with a `dw9714 2-000c` lens entity in the media graph. The ueventd `Unable to find a module for i2c:dw9714` is only Android's vendor modprobe looking for a module stage0 already loaded, so no module build is needed. The lens subdev was runtime-`suspended`: nothing opened it, so the VCM was unpowered and the lens sat at its rest stop, blurring everything.
    - The bridge now finds the lens through the sensor's ancillary media link and keeps its subdev open (powered). It starts at DAC 286, a thin-lens estimate for ~1.5 m.
    - Whenever a consumer starts streaming, it runs contrast-detect AF: coarse 230..470 in steps of 20, then ±15 around the best in steps of 5, settling 2 frames per step, with AE held. The sharpness score is squared differences of same-colour sites two pixels apart in the central ninth, divided by level². It logs `af: lens ... range ..., start N` and `af: focus at N (coarse M, sharpness S)`.
    - Our reading of the OV8856 tuning's optomechanics record 13 (VCM present, focal length 2.83 mm, f/2.5, 1.12 µm, then 278/400/1023, read as infinity/macro/max DAC) set the scan range; that reading is not confirmed.
    - Test: a synthetic lens peaking at 252/337/461 is found within ±5 in ≤ 63 frames; no lens means no AF and no AE hold.
  - Host tests, ASan/UBSan and both docker suites pass. Bridge SHA-256 `d0baa664acdc6a718dfb95971422a53be69b01c5228835d41a95255bb7b3fd6d`. Not deployed.
  - *Unverified on hardware:* the residual tint in boot-11's light, the edge desaturation on real detail, the AF scan's visible defocus (~2 s) and its result, whether the VCM range is right, and the front's 30 fps converter cost.
- 2026-11-19 **Installer (menu 2: install and Update) skipped `gbos/camera/` and `gbos/thermal/` (found by the OV8856 thread; checked before the user's first permanent install).**
  - `usb/install.sh` DIRS listed `firmware patched audio installer kmod modules surface ksu` (+ `sensors` if present). The camera bridge reads its calibration from `/postinstall/gbos/camera` (`ov5678_*`, `ov8856_*` CCM and lens shading; stick only, never in git), and `hook.sh` binds `$G/thermal/latitude7320_thermal_info_config.json` over Google's thermal config. So an installed system booted without camera calibration and with Lapis' thermal limits, logging only `Latitude thermal config missing`. Live boots were fine.
  - Fix: `camera` and `thermal` are added when present, like `sensors`. `tools/test_install_dirs.sh` evaluates install.sh's DIRS lines on a fake stick and checks every `$G/<dir>/` or `/postinstall/gbos/<dir>/` the boot scripts read is copied or listed as stick-only (`a11y`: stage0 screen reader for stick boots; `logs`). It fails on the old install.sh.
  - Also checked: every top-level file the boot uses is in FILES. `usb/optimize.sh` is referenced by hook.sh's `gbos_optimize` service but no tool puts it on the stick or the disk (only the old `tools/install_dualboot.sh`), so it is missing on live and installed boots alike; left as is (it forces the `performance` CPU governor, bad for battery).
  - Not tested on hardware or in the QEMU installer runs.
- 2026-11-19 **Goodix templates on the installed system (checked before the user's first permanent install; built, not deployed).**
  - *The trap:* the sensor still holds `GBOS-1c2ac773-u10-5cfb789b`, an old-style name from a live boot before 2026-11-18. After the install, live boots find `LABEL=GBOSSYS` and no longer pass `--no-gbos-install`, and the install's persistent /data classified another instance's old-style template as `FP_KEEP_OTHER_SYSTEM`. Nothing would ever delete it again, and enrolling that finger on the install would fail every time ("kept, so not enrolled here"). Other fingers would work.
  - *Why deleting it on the install is safe:* stage0 mounts an ext4 /data only when it boots the NVMe install (`FROM=nvme`); live boots always get tmpfs. So the install's /data is the only persistent one on the machine, and another persistent instance belongs to a /data that is gone (a pre-2026-11-18 live boot, or a wiped or reinstalled install).
  - *Fix:* `hook.sh` passes `--installed` when `FROM=nvme`. `gbos_fp` sets `installed` only when that flag is present, statfs confirms a non-tmpfs /data, and the instance id was read from its file or newly created and saved. New verdict `FP_DELETE_REPLACED`: another instance's old-style template, seen from the install. It is still kept when its user and id are among the enrollments Android lists, or when that user's state is unreadable, so a lost instance file cannot cost a working finger.
  - *Instance id:* `load_instance()` used to overwrite an unreadable or damaged `instance` file with a new random id. It now writes a new id only when the file does not exist. Otherwise it uses a temporary id for that boot and logs the error, which also turns off `installed`.
  - *Stable per install:* `DATA_DIR/instance` (`/data/vendor/crosfp/gbos_fp/instance`) lives on GBOS_DATA. It survives reboots and Updates, and changes only when /data is wiped (reinstall or factory reset). The install's own templates (`GBOS-<its id>-u<user>-<id>`) are kept while Android lists them.
  - *Tests:* new policy cases, a reconcile on the install, and `test_install_stale_template` (with `installed` the boot-11 template is deleted and the enrolment restarts and succeeds; without it, the finger stays blocked). Hook tests cover `--installed` on an NVMe boot and neither flag on a live boot with an install. A mutant with the rule off fails the tests.
  - *Built:* `usb/gbos_fp` SHA-256 `6374c55fe55a4ec3d0244d60b1c95f778e497c1f4a93aeb7fd6fc0de32650fe5`. Not deployed. For the install to get it, it must be on the stick before menu 2 runs, or arrive later with an Update (menu 1).

Handed to a camera agent: the locus clamp against the single-coefficient IR subtraction, plus highlight clipping.

- 2026-11-19 **Goodix delete: the wrong sub-command (a7:04); libfprint deletes with a7:00 (built, not deployed).**
  - *Source checked:* with the user's approval, libfprint v1.94.9 `drivers/goodixmoc/goodix.c`, `goodix_proto.c` and `goodix_proto.h` were downloaded (76 KB, gitlab.freedesktop.org) and read; they were not committed. `gx_fp_template_delete()` sends `MOC_CMD0_DELETETEMPLATE` (a7) with `MOC_CMD1_DEFAULT` (0x00). `MOC_CMD1_DELETE_TEMPLATE` (0x04) is defined but never sent. `MOC_CMD1_DELETE_ALL` (0x01) is `clear_storage`. Boot-11 and boot-12 sent a7:04, which firmware 01010264 acknowledges with 0x00 while keeping the template.
  - *Payload:* `encode_finger_id()` builds a 2-byte length (70 + uid length) plus the 67/1/1/0/account/tid/uid record, the same as ours, but libfprint passes that length as the payload size, so it sends the buffer without its last 2 bytes (the uid's last byte and the 0). The commit (a4) is sent the same way; our commit sends the whole record, and the firmware stores it.
  - *Fix:* `GOODIX_CMD_DELETE_ONE` is now 0x00. a7:01 is not defined anywhere, and a `_Static_assert` in `goodix_delete_payload()` rejects any other value. `goodix_delete_template()` sends up to three a7:00 payloads and checks the list after each one: libfprint's exact bytes, then the record as listed, then the full re-encoded record if it differs. The log line now reads `delete <uid> (a7:00, libfprint|listed|re-encoded record, N bytes, ...): status 0xSS`.
  - *Tests:* every simulator model answers a7:04 with 0x00 and keeps the template (the boot-12 negative control), and a7:01 aborts the test. New models: a7:00 matching on tid and uid (libfprint's short payload gets 0x9c, the listed record deletes), and matching on tid alone (libfprint's payload deletes, and nothing else is touched). Mutants built with 0x04 and 0x01 both fail the tests, and 0x01 fails to compile.
  - *Not done:* the other session suggested adopting an undeletable stale template as the new enrolment. That is not built, and is held back until a7:00 is tried on the laptop.
  - *Next boot:* in `fingerprint.txt`, the session-start cleanup should log `deleted sensor template GBOS-1c2ac773-u10-5cfb789b (no longer listed)`, then enrolling that finger should work. If all three a7:00 forms still log `still listed`, build the adoption fallback.
  - *Built:* `usb/gbos_fp` SHA-256 `8602ca7b4b12daaf07c683e019c176eb2154e35b50be0f2a757752cfe0d8d462`. Not deployed.
- 2026-11-19 **Goodix delete guard: other templates must survive (requested by the "Boot log errors" session; built, not deployed).**
  - *Risk:* the "listed" and "re-encoded" a7:00 payloads are ones libfprint never sends. If a firmware read an odd payload as a wildcard, a delete could silently erase other templates, including Windows Hello's.
  - *Guard:* `goodix_delete_template()` lists every template before the first attempt and compares by uid and tid after each attempt (`goodix_delete_check()`). If any other template is gone, it logs `delete <uid> also removed <other> from the sensor`, and then `... removed N other template(s): no more deletes until gbos_fp restarts`. It sets `goodix.delete_blocked`, which only `goodix_init()` clears (once per HAL process, i.e. per boot), and returns GOODIX_FAILED. Later deletes log `not sent: an earlier delete removed other templates`.
  - *Only libfprint's payload* is sent while the sensor holds any template not named `GBOS-` (logged as `holds templates not named GBOS-, so only libfprint's payload is sent`).
  - *Simulator:* the default a7:00 model now matches on the tid, so libfprint's payload works, as fprintd's delete does. New `DELETE_WILDCARD`: libfprint's payload answers 0x00 and deletes nothing, while any whole-record payload deletes every template. Its tests check three cases. With two GBOS templates, the loss is detected and deletes stay blocked across a close and reopen. With a Windows print stored, only one a7:00 goes out and the print survives. With a working firmware and a Windows print stored, the target goes and the Windows print stays. Mutants without the block, or without the non-GBOS rule, fail.

- 2026-11-19 **Boot-12 (Latitude 7320, 11:04–11:30, live, kernel #62, bridge `d0baa664…`): front camera green in daylight, pink sky; s2idle blocked by the camera. Fixed in the bridge and launcher (host-tested, not deployed).** Logs are in `build/boot-logs/boot-12`.
  - *Evidence, front camera:* all frames at 1.0x gain, by a window.
    - `cfa4x4`: IR sites read 50.7–68.9 against G 177–203 (IR/G 0.29–0.35; boot-11's LED room: 0.10). Raw R equals raw B within 2% in every sample.
    - Grey-world R/G is 0.487–0.521 and B/G 0.440–0.510, which puts it 0.072–0.149 from the locus. The 0.03 band clamped it every time.
    - Frame 6300 (the 11:30 selfie) used 0.545/0.544: R under-gained by ~11% and B by ~19%, and the matrix roughly doubles that.
    - Boot-8's morning frames show the same pattern (IR/G 0.24–0.27, distance 0.07–0.13).
    - The rear OV8856 has no IR sites. In the same room at the same time it sat 0.02–0.04 from its own locus, near illuminants 13+4 (~6000 K), so the light was ordinary daylight. Rear AF worked (`af: focus at 395`).
  - *Cause of the green (1), infrared:*
    - The bridge subtracted the IR site from R, G and B with one coefficient. That is right for the IR site's visible leakage, about 0.10 of green in NIR-free light (boot-11: 0.103–0.106), and the calibrated white points assume it.
    - Daylight adds real NIR, and the blue sites respond to it less than the IR site does. Blue was over-subtracted, grey-world fell off the locus on the low-blue side, and the locus clamp then turned every grey green.
    - New model: NIR `n = max(0, IR·1.1 − 0.1·G)`, colour `= C − IR + (1 − k_c)·n`, with k_R = k_G = 1 and **k_B = 0.60**.
    - k_B is fitted to the 25 daylight samples of boot-8 and boot-12, lens-shading-corrected. The median locus distance goes from 0.135 to 0.04 (boot-12: 0.001–0.022, boot-8: 0.03–0.08). Boot-11's LED frames stay at 0.005–0.017.
    - This is our fit under the grey-world assumption. It is **not** taken from the tuning file. With n = 0 (LED, lamps) nothing changes.
    - The locus band now widens with NIR (0.03 + 0.10·n/G, at most 0.08), because the calibration lights carry none.
  - *Tuning file:*
    - The OV5678 `.aiqb` has an LCMC record 27 that only RGB-IR sensors have (the OV8856 file lacks it). It holds the 4x4 CFA map (G, IR excluded, R, B). Then, for each calibration light (ids 1, 6, 4, 7, 19, 20, each with R/G, B/G, x, y), it holds three 15x9 u16 grids (G, R, B; radial, higher at the edges) and one float.
    - The floats are the IR/G ratios of those lights: ~0.79 for illuminant A (tungsten) and 0.096–0.105 for the others. The 0.10 leakage comes from there.
    - The grids look like per-channel IR weights, but their scale is not confirmed. Under the most plausible reading (64 = 1.0) the three channels differ by under 10% in daylight-like lights, which cannot explain boot-12, so the bridge doesn't use them.
    - LCMC record 15 holds the AWB locus: 8 white points in three rows (nominal and two bounds).
    - LAIQ has DFLT and IRVI variants, which suggests Intel switches AWB tuning when IR is present. Not decoded.
  - *Cause of the green (2), shading:*
    - Grey-world was measured on the raw, vignetted frame. On the front, blue falls off less than green towards the corners, so the frame's B/G read ~5% above the picture's. The centre of a grey wall came out yellow-green (U ≈ −4), in LED light too.
    - Front white-balance tiles now take the lens-shading gains of their position.
    - The rear keeps the old statistics: its grey-world sat on its locus and the user reports good colour. The same simulation suggests its centre greys are slightly warm (its red falls off most). Left for a rear-specific test.
  - *Cause of the pink sky:*
    - Green clips first. White balance (R and B ×~1.85) and the matrix then turn a clipped area magenta.
    - The fade to white was keyed to raw full scale, so if the sensor saturates below code 1023 it stops short. After the matrix, channels past the top were also clipped one by one.
    - Simulated sky with the old bridge: a sensor clip of 900 gives U +2.9, V +3.8 (pink). At 1023 it is white.
    - Fixes:
      - The white-balance sampling detects a flat top in the green histogram (≥ 0.1% of samples within 2 codes of the maximum, above code 768). The fade runs from 80% to 97% of that level.
      - Colours past the top are desaturated towards their own luminance (Rec. 709) instead of clipped per channel.
      - Tiles with a site above 95% of the clip level are left out of grey-world.
    - Whether the OV5678 really saturates below 1023 is not measured. The new `ir:` line logs `clip at raw N` (1023 when there is no flat top).
  - *Simulated:* 2592x1944 frames built from the logged per-site levels, with the real vignetting and the stick's tables (local only). Centre grey U/V, old → new:
    - boot-12 idle (frame 900): −12.8/−3.0 → +0.4/−0.2;
    - boot-12 selfie (frame 6300): −19.0/−6.1 → −1.6/−2.2 (the scene's own grey-world is still 0.08 off and clamped to the wider band);
    - boot-8 at 50x: −12.0/−5.6 → −1.7/−1.1;
    - boot-11 at 17.5x, σ 40 DN: −4.2/−0.2 → −0.1/+1.2.
    - The blue NIR term does most of the work: the wider band alone leaves boot-12 idle at −6.0/−3.2. Rear scenes come out byte-identical.
    - Converter cost on this i7-1180G7: 24.4 → 26.0 ms/frame at 1x, 27.2 → 28.8 ms at 17.5x.
  - *Suspend:*
    - kmsg shows 24 of 25 s2idle attempts at 459–490 s failing with `isys_suspend [intel_ipu6_isys] returns -16`.
    - `isys_suspend` returns -EBUSY while `isys->stream_opened` is non-zero, and both bridges kept streaming with no consumer.
    - In the #62 source, `close_streaming_firmware()` drops `stream_opened` even when the firmware's close times out, so STREAMOFF is enough.
    - When the last stream stops, `ipu6_isys_fw_close()` closes the firmware. ISYS can then runtime-suspend, which clears `need_reset`, and the next start re-initialises the firmware.
  - *Idle stop:*
    - **When:** after **15 s** without a consumer. 15 s is longer than an app's camera switch or mode change (well under 2 s), so those don't cycle the sensor. It is also Android's shortest screen timeout, so the sensors are off by about when the system first tries to sleep.
    - **Stop:** the bridge stops its stream (STREAMOFF, buffers released) and closes the lens subdev, so the sensor and VCM power down.
    - **What stays open:** the capture node, the loopback output and the sensor controls. Android still lists the camera, and exposure and gain are kept: the drivers cache control writes while powered off and apply them at stream start.
    - **Restart:** when a consumer appears, the bridge
      - writes one black frame at once;
      - restores the planned frame, not the 8x idle frame;
      - streams on the still-open node (no reopen, so no `isys power cycle required`);
      - re-opens the lens. The rear runs its AF scan, as on every consumer start.
    - **Wake-up:** v4l2loopback's client-usage event, polled at most 1 s apart (250 ms without the event), plus `readers_active`. That control comes from the capture stream token, which the consumer takes at STREAMON and releases at STREAMOFF, REQBUFS(0) or close (a crash closes it too), so it is reliable. With a module that lacks the control (readers −1) the bridge never stops.
    - **Settings:** `--idle-stop N`, or `CAMERA_IDLE_STOP=N` in gbos.cfg (the launcher passes it as `IPU6_IDLE_STOP`). 0 streams forever, as before.
    - With fewer streams running at once, the boot-9 FIFO overflow is also less likely.
  - *Supervisor:*
    - An idle stop never exits the bridge, so it never counts as a failure.
    - A restart that fails exits with status 3, as does 10 s without a good frame (as before); the launcher then restarts the process.
    - A bridge that ran for 300 s or more before exiting gets its 3 restarts back, so occasional failures over days of stop/start cycles can't use up the budget.
  - *Tests:*
    - `test_ipu6_loopback.c`:
      - a daylight RGB-IR grey (boot-12 levels, synthetic locus) comes out neutral within 2. Negative controls: the old full subtraction with the fixed band gives green, and the wider band alone is still green;
      - LED low light (boot-11, 17.5x, noisy) is neutral within 1.5, and within 0.5 of the old subtraction;
      - a clipped sky (green saturating at 920) comes out white, with the grey below neutral. With clip detection off it is pink (U +5, V +4);
      - the soft clip keeps luminance and hue order;
      - the idle state machine stops at 15 s, does not stop on a 3 s gap, starts on a consumer, and never stops with readers −1 or with timeout 0 (the old behaviour);
      - a restart restores the planned frame in E-V-A-D order and keeps the exposure;
      - `--idle-stop` and `IPU6_IDLE_STOP` parsing.
    - `test_ipu6_camera_bridge.sh`: the idle-stop config and the restart-budget refill (mutations are caught).
    - These suites pass: host, ASan/UBSan in `gbos-kbuild`, `test_ipu6_loopback.sh`, `test_ipu6_camera_bridge.sh`, `test_hook_camera_service.sh`, camera capture, media-graph, fw-trace, kernel-config, module-load, and payload-guard (with the gitignored firmware copied in for the run).
    - Not run: `test_camera_fw_trace_kernel.sh` needs a privileged container, and `test_camera_7320_patch.py` needs a kernel source argument. Neither touches the bridge.
  - *Built:* `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` SHA-256 `f42fc06d363b128b584a022185b20687747d7e29dee8788dfdf7938fde894278`, launcher `29f86135118ada126c9b908ff6fe46f523e699f93c2647fee55c0899b85cee0a`. Not deployed: the stick needs `gbos/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` and `gbos/installer/bin/ipu6-camera-bridge.sh`.
  - *Unverified on hardware:*
    - **Above all: whether an IPU6 stream restarts cleanly after a stop.** S2 (2026-11-17) saw flush/close firmware timeouts on every stop, and nobody has tried a restart in the same process. If it fails, the bridge exits and the launcher relaunches it; if that fails too, set `CAMERA_IDLE_STOP=0`.
    - k_B = 0.60 in other daylight and other scenes. It is one coefficient for every NIR spectrum, and tungsten, with far more NIR than daylight, was never logged with the RGB-IR bridge.
    - Whether the OV5678 saturates below code 1023.
    - The first-frame latency after a restart, and whether Android's USB HAL tolerates it.
  - *Next boot:* deploy both files, run Update, then:
    1. Point the front camera at a white wall, by the window and indoors. The `ccm:` lines should show a locus distance under the band; the `ir:` line should show NIR ~0.3–0.45 in daylight and ~0.03 under LED light.
    2. The sky should be white or pale blue. Check `clip at raw N`.
    3. Close the camera app and wait more than 15 s. Both runtime logs should show `bridge: no consumer for 15 s; stopping the sensor stream` and `sensor stream stopped in X s`. Then open the camera: expect `restarting the sensor stream (start N)`, `first sensor frame X s after the consumer appeared`, a live picture, and `af: focus at` on the rear. Switch between front and rear a few times.
    4. Let the screen sleep. kmsg should show `PM: suspend entry (s2idle)` without `isys_suspend ... returns -16`.
    - Also check kmsg for `stream stop time out`, `stream close time out`, `failed to close fw isys`, `clearing old context` and `isys power cycle required`, and `camera-bridge-setup.txt` for bridge exits.

The HAL restarts identify while the finger is still down and logs no per-attempt result. Also `sensor open failed: -125` after each lockout clear, and `discarding stale packet d0:00`. - 2026-11-19 **Goodix unlock after boot-13: identify from a verify-mode capture, one touch = one attempt (built, not deployed).**
  - *Boot-13 (from the "Boot log errors" session):* the a7:00 delete removed `GBOS-1c2ac773-u10-5cfb789b`, and enrolment worked (`enrolled 961518466`, later `1092421691`). On the lock screen, one touch gave five `onAuthenticationFailed` about 290 ms apart, then `30 s lockout after 5 failures`. After each password unlock came `sensor open failed: -125`, then `createSession while user 10's session is open` and `discarding stale packet d0:00`.
  - *Cause 1, never a match:* libfprint (`fp_verify_sm_run_state`) captures for verification with a2 `{1, config[10], config[11]}` = `{1, 0x00, 0x23}` and accepts quality > 0 and coverage ≥ 35 (`fp_verify_capture_cb`). `goodix_identify()` used the enrol capture `{0, config[4], config[5]}` with enrol thresholds. `goodix_capture()` now takes `verify`. This is the most likely cause, not yet proven: the HAL logged no a5 status.
  - *Cause 2, five failures from one touch:* after a no-match the HAL captured again at once, with the finger still down. `goodix_identify()` now waits with b0:02 until the finger is lifted after a no-match or an unusable image, so each failure that `count_failure()` counts is a separate touch.
  - *Cause 3, -125:* keyguard cancelled the authentication while the sensor was opening. `goodix_command()` returned ECANCELED after the acknowledgement even for commands that can't be cancelled, leaving d0:00's response in the pipe. Now only cancellable commands stop there; others read their response, and the cancel takes effect at the next command. `open_failed()` reports a cancelled open as ERR_CANCELED, not ERR_HW_UNAVAILABLE.
  - *Logs per touch:* `identify: capture quality Q, coverage C` (or `capture status 0xSS`), then `identify: status 0xSS: no match`, or `identify: status 0x00, score N, study S, template <uid>` (with `(not one of this user's)` when the template isn't this user's).
  - *Simulator:* a touch model, where each touch puts a finger down until b0:02 lifts it and a capture with no touch left never answers. a5 matches only after a verify-mode capture. a5's reply follows libfprint's layout. `test_identify_touches` checks:
    - the right finger matches and the score is logged;
    - a wrong finger then the right one gives one failure, then a match;
    - one wrong touch counts once, and the next attempt waits for a new touch;
    - a quality-0 image is retried without an identify;
    - another user's template is a no-match.

    `test_cancel_during_open` covers the boot-13 cancel, with nothing left in the pipe. Mutants with enrol-mode capture, without the finger-up wait, or with the old post-acknowledgement cancel fail the tests.
  - *Built:* `usb/gbos_fp` SHA-256 `ddc471629060a8736c897192660e2f940e994d60f21ac551580d77281b391f32`. Not deployed.
  - *Next boot:* in `fingerprint.txt`, an unlock touch should show `capture quality/coverage` and `identify: status 0x00, score ..., template GBOS-L-...`. If the status is still nonzero with good quality and coverage, the template itself is suspect (enrol capture or commit), and the logged status is the lead.
- 2026-11-19 **Boot-13 idle-stop hang: cause and fix (bridge `3c248339…`, launcher `6b76b7ff…`; host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Where it blocked:* after STREAMOFF and REQBUFS(0), the idle stop called `loopback_drain_events()`, a loop of `VIDIOC_DQEVENT` on the loopback node. The node is opened without `O_NONBLOCK`, and there DQEVENT sleeps until the next event (`v4l2_event_dequeue(..., f_flags & O_NONBLOCK)`). So each bridge slept before printing "sensor stream stopped". Every app open or close woke it for one event (the 0x5659 audits at 151.4 and 154.2 s) and it went back to sleep, so the restart check never ran. Selinux audits are rate-limited samples (`audit_rate_limit=5`), not a trace, which is why the first DQEVENT at ~142 s doesn't appear. The ISYS stop/close timeouts are real but didn't cause the hang: STREAMOFF returned.
  - *Fix:*
    - The drain only dequeues while `poll(POLLPRI, 0)` reports an event.
    - The idle stop is STREAMOFF only: the ISYS `stream_opened` count, which blocks suspend, drops at stream stop. The buffers stay mapped, and the restart is QBUF + STREAMON, with a fallback to REQBUFS from scratch.
    - Every stop/restart step is logged (`bridge: idle step: ...`, `idle stop: STREAMOFF done ... in X s`, `idle restart: stream on in X s`). A SIGALRM watchdog (12 s per step) ends a blocked step with exit status 4. A failed restart, or no good frame 5 s after the consumer appeared, also exits 4.
    - On status 4 the launcher restarts that bridge at once with `--idle-stop 0`, so that camera streams for the rest of the boot without using up a restart.
  - The launcher default stays off (`CAMERA_IDLE_STOP` unset = 0). To test the restart, set `CAMERA_IDLE_STOP=15` and look for those lines and for `first sensor frame ... after the consumer appeared`.
  - Tests:
    - fake ioctl/poll for the drain (no DQEVENT with nothing pending), pause/resume ordering and failures;
    - a forked child whose STREAMOFF/STREAMON blocks forever, which must exit 4 in about 1 s;
    - the restart deadline;
    - the real supervisor loop against a fake bridge that exits 4.

- 2026-11-20 **Boot-14 front green tint under evening room light: cause and fix (bridge `f0cbd504…`; host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Cause:* the light, not the IR model. The white wall's grey-world (lens-shading-corrected, IR-free) was R/G 0.57–0.62, B/G 0.40–0.42, which is 0.10–0.14 off the module's locus on its green side (both ratios lower than the calibrated light of the same colour temperature, as fluorescent and many LED lights are). NIR was 0.00–0.02.
    - The rear, in the same room, sat 0.04–0.05 off on the same side, so the light really is greenish. The RGB-IR filters just see it as more so.
    - Even with nothing subtracted from blue, B/G would have been about 0.50 against a locus value of about 0.57, so the IR subtraction can't explain it.
    - The 0.03 band pulled the white point back to the locus: R gain about 8% and B gain about 15–20% short, so the wall came out green/cyan.
  - *Fix (front only; the rear is unchanged):*
    - White-balance statistics are also gathered per region (8×6). The white point is the mean of the regions whose colour a light could have: up to 2× the band on the magenta side and up to 0.18 on the green side. That mean may stay up to 0.18 off on the green side.
    - If fewer than a quarter of the regions qualify (for example a frame of foliage), it falls back to grey-world with the plain band, as before.
    - Saturated colours are left out of the measurement, so a grey under a green top third now stays neutral. Before, it was tinted U −3, V +5.
    - Log: `ccm: ... (locus distance D green side|magenta side, band B, N of M regions lit like a light, green side allowed|; grey-world)`.
  - Simulated with the module's real tables (stick only, never committed): boot-14 frames 1800/2700 go from U −6.7/V −3.6 to −0.0/−0.5. Boot-11 LED at 17.5x, boot-12 daylight and boot-14's dark start-up frame change by ≤0.1.
  - Tests (synthetic locus):
    - `test_green_side_light`: boot-14 levels come out neutral; negative control with the zone off is green.
    - `test_green_scene_not_light`: foliage regions are left out; the zone off tints the grey; foliage alone never opens the green side.
    - The daylight test's old-bridge controls now also switch the zone off.
  - Unverified on hardware: other lights, and pale green walls, which any grey-world AWB will neutralise.
- 2026-11-19 **Security: the `gbos_sync` run.sh root backchannel is now off by default (requested by the "Boot log errors" session; built, not deployed).**
  `gbos_sync` (added in `9ab314c`) ran `/postinstall/gbos/run.sh` as root (`u:r:shell:s0`) every 5 s and deleted it. The gbos FAT is mounted `umask=000`
  and SELinux is permissive, so any app with storage access, or any process, could drop run.sh and get root within 5 s. Nothing in the repo, the docs or
  `tools/` writes run.sh (HANDOFF's other `run.sh` is `usb/localai/run.sh`, which is a different file), so no workflow depended on it.
  - *Now:* `hook.sh` builds the loop body at hook time. It includes the run.sh step only when gbos.cfg has exactly `RUN_SH_BACKCHANNEL=1`, read by stage0 before
    Android starts, and logs `hook: RUN_SH_BACKCHANNEL=1: ...` to stage0-dmesg. Otherwise the service is
    `while true; do sleep 5; sync; done`. The periodic `sync` stays because the logs need it (the laptop is powered off by hand). A leftover
    `gbos/run.sh` with the flag off is logged as `gbos/run.sh present but ignored`. The generated .rc line has no `$` and keeps its seclabel.
    `install.sh` copies the stick's gbos.cfg, so an install made while the flag is on inherits it. Remove the line from both copies when done.
  - *Test:* `tools/test_run_sh_backchannel.sh` cuts the gate and the service line out of hook.sh and generates the rc line for unset, empty, 0, yes, true,
    2 and " 1" (all off) and for 1 (on). It runs the loop body once against a fake /postinstall and checks that nothing outside the gate mentions
    gbos/run.sh. The pre-change hook.sh fails it.
  - *Still open (not fixed here):* the FAT is world-writable, and Android runs root code from it: `gbos_fp`, `soft_gk`, the diag scripts started 60 to 70 s
    after boot, and `hook.sh`/`gbos.cfg` on the next boot. So an app can still get root, more slowly. The real fix is to mount it without
    `umask=000`, e.g. `uid=0,gid=0,fmask=0133,dmask=0022`, or read-only for apps.
- 2026-11-19 **Security: Android sees the gbos FAT read-only, and only root can reach this boot's log directory (hook.sh only, no kernel rebuild; host- and container-tested, not deployed, not booted).**
  stage0_init.sh mounts the volume `rw,flush,umask=000`, so every file and directory on it is 0777. hook.sh bound it to `/postinstall`, SELinux is permissive, and Android runs root code from it: `gbos_fp`, `soft_gk`, `apex_patch.sh`, the diag scripts (all `u:r:shell:s0`, root), and `hook.sh` and `gbos.cfg` on the next boot. So any app could get root. The run.sh gate (7055154) was not on `latitude-7320-integration`; it is cherry-picked here.
  - *Why not new mount options:* vfat permissions belong to the superblock, a vfat remount ignores `uid`/`gid`/`umask` (2026-11-18 entry), and a second mount of the same device reuses the superblock. So `uid=0,gid=0,fmask=0133,dmask=0022` can only come from stage0_init.sh, and that means rebuilding the kernel (initramfs). Done per mount in hook.sh instead (`gbos_postinstall_lockdown`, called right after the `/postinstall` bind):
    - `/postinstall`: bind remount `ro,nosuid,nodev`. Stage0's `/gbos` stays writable: the kmsg copier, the sync loop and stick_fat.sh's shutdown watcher use it. exec stays, because `gbos_fp` and `soft_gk` (both `user system`) and the camera tools run from the volume.
    - `/postinstall/gbos/logs`: a 64 KB tmpfs, mode 0700 root. Nothing without `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` (apps, system_server, `gbos_fp`) can look inside, so older boots' logs are not readable from Android either.
    - `.../logs/boot-N`: a bind of `$L`, read-write, `nosuid,nodev,noexec`.
    - Fallbacks, logged as `POSTINSTALL-RW` in stage0-dmesg: if the ro remount fails, it is logged and the volume stays as before. If the tmpfs gateway fails, `logs/boot-N` is bound directly, so apps can write log files but nothing else. `POSTINSTALL_RW=1` in gbos.cfg (debug only) keeps the old world-writable `/postinstall`.
  - *Who writes in Android (checked; `user` lines in hook.sh, setuid in usb/ and the C sources):* logcat and crash logs, the audio/sensor/camera/display/AI/zygote/ksu/iptsd/tablet-mode/camera-bridge diagnostics, and the touchpad IDC that init copies from `logs/boot-N`. All of them are root services writing into `logs/boot-N`. `soft_gk` (`user system`) opens no files. The `gbos_aidns_*` services (app uids) write to `/dev`. The camera bridge never drops root.
  - *`gbos_fp` (`user system`) is the only non-root writer.* Since 4142874 it appends `fingerprint.txt` itself (`--log FILE`, one O_APPEND write per line), so it can't reach the root-only directory. vfat has no per-file owner, so `gbos_system_log` gives it that one file through `/dev/gbos-system-log/fingerprint.txt`. The directory is in stage0's `/dev`, which `mount --move` takes into Android with its mounts. It is mode 0500 and owned by system, and the file is a bind of `$L/fingerprint.txt`. The HAL gets `--log /dev/gbos-system-log/fingerprint.txt`; the binary is unchanged. system can append to that file but cannot create or remove anything there, or write anywhere else on the volume. Apps can't reach it. The lines still land in `logs/boot-N/fingerprint.txt`. When the logs aren't root-only (`POSTINSTALL_RW=1` or the fallback), the HAL keeps the old `/postinstall/gbos/logs/boot-N/fingerprint.txt`. If the bind fails, that is logged and the HAL logs to logcat only. The sleep guard and the camera fake sysfs use `/data/local/tmp`. `gbos_sync` only calls `sync`. Updates (`install.sh`, Update/Install) and stick_fat.sh run in stage0 on `/gbos` and are not affected. The only exception is `audio/audio-profile.sh`, a manual root tool that writes `surface/speaker_profile` and the `dsp.ini` template. It now re-runs itself under `unshare -m` with `/postinstall` remounted rw in that namespace only. That is also how root gets full write access by hand: `unshare -m sh -c 'mount -o remount,bind,rw /postinstall && ...'`. No app-facing workflow wrote the stick.
  - *run.sh backchannel* (`RUN_SH_BACKCHANNEL=1`): stage0 moves `gbos/run.sh` into `logs/boot-N/`, and gbos_sync runs it from there (output in `logs/boot-N/run.log`). It runs only while the logs are root-only, so not with `POSTINSTALL_RW=1` or the fallback.
  - *Tests:* `tools/test_postinstall_ro.sh` (75 checks; one opens the file with gbos_fp.c's exact flags, `O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_APPEND|O_CLOEXEC|O_NOFOLLOW`, as uid/gid 1000). `test_gbos_fp.sh` covers the `/dev/gbos-system-log` path and the case where its bind fails.
    - Host: the function cut out of hook.sh, with a fake mount: the call sequence, `POSTINSTALL_RW`, each fallback, and the call site. It also checks the run.sh gate, that every `/postinstall` write and log path in hook.sh is under `logs/boot-$N`, that the services writing there run as root, the log paths in the Android-side scripts, and the audio-profile wrapper.
    - Kernel (privileged `gbos-kbuild` container, 6.18 WSL kernel, stage0's busybox 1.37): a loop vfat mounted exactly as stage0 does, with `/` rshared as Android does. Uids 10123 and 1000 cannot write, create, delete or list anything, but can still exec `gbos_fp`. A namespace-private rw remount works and does not leak. stick_fat.sh's superblock `remount,ro` still works.

Not `fmask=0133`: vfat has no per-file modes, so every file would be 0644, and `gbos_fp`, `soft_gk`, `installer/bin/*` and stage0's own `/gbos/busybox` could not run. Then the volume is safe even where the bind is missing.

- 2026-11-20 **Boot-15 front colour in strong NIR, and 1280×960 output for both cameras (host-tested only, not deployed).** Bridge `dc9c4595…`, launcher `03bf261c…`, `usb/kmod/v4l2loopback.ko` `8a2cb95e…`, descriptors front `b974b2d0…` / rear `6dc902c0…`.
  - *Colour, cause:* the frame's white balance was close (white ceiling near neutral), but things that reflect much NIR were too blue: a dark green T-shirt came out teal, a dark teal wall navy, skin pinkish.
    - Dark dyes and paints reflect little visible light but a lot of NIR, so in those windows NIR is several times the visible green, and any error in the blue NIR term is multiplied.
    - k_B = 0.60 was fitted assuming daylight sits on the locus. Boot-14 showed real lights 0.10–0.14 off it on the green side.
    - Host simulation: a sensor with k_B 0.75 under the 0.60 model gives exactly the teal shirt.
    - Refit: with k_B = 0.75, boot-12's daylight frames sit on the locus (0.00–0.03; at 0.60 they were 0.04 on the magenta side), and boot-8's sit 0.07 on the green side, inside the green-side zone.
    - k_R stays 1: boot-15's frames that wanted less were mostly the green shirt.
  - *Colour, fix (front only):*
    - k_B = 0.75.
    - A colour guard per 4x4 window fades colour where NIR is more than 1.5 above the frame's own ratio (half kept at +3). That is a safety net: a correctly measured NIR-rich green keeps about 75%.
    - The same guard fades colour where the window's 8 greens disagree strongly (contrast > 0.25, down to 15% at 0.75): stripes and sharp edges, where the 2 R and 2 B sites see other parts of the pattern. These were the blinds' green and magenta fringes. Stripes 6 px apart: mean chroma 25 → 7.
  - *Resolution:*
    - The bridge's 640×480 output came from one 4x4 window per pixel (about every 4 sensor pixels), so real detail was about 650×490 whatever the output size, and fine stripes aliased.
    - The output is now 1280×960 (4:3). Colour still comes from the same windows on a 640×480 grid. Each output pixel takes that grid's Y′U′V′ (bilinear) and scales Y′ by (its own green / the window's green)^(1/2.4), its green being the 2 green sites of its 2x2 block. Green is a checkerboard on both sensors.
    - Detail is full up to 8x gain, falling to 0.4 at 32x (2 sites are noisier than 8).
    - Colour vs the 640×480 converter, 2x2-averaged, on six scenes front and rear: bias ≤ 0.1 (≤ 0.4 on stripes). Stripes 4 px apart are lost without detail (luma sd 0) and resolved with it (sd 25).
  - *Cost (this machine is the laptop, i7-1180G7):*
    - The old converter at 640×480 took 27 ms per frame on one core.
    - The new one at 1280×960 takes 27 ms (front) / 24 ms (rear) with 2 row-band threads per bridge. Both converting at once: 31 ms, inside the 33 ms of 30 fps. Normally only one camera has a consumer; the other runs statistics only.
    - Changes: raw→linear lookup table, masked register sums per window, colour grid / colour mix / output pass in 2 bands, detail + temporal filter + YUYV packing fused into one pass.
  - *HAL/size plumbing:*
    - The USB HAL picks the largest size v4l2loopback enumerates, so the launcher pins min = max.
    - The module's fixed list had no 4:3 size above 640×480, so `{1280, 960}` is appended (the generic six keep their order).
    - v4l2loopback.ko was rebuilt in `gbos-ksrc` (bzImage = the stick's #62), stripped and signed with key 2F:5D…; vermagic and all symbol CRCs match the deployed #61/#62 module.
    - Launcher bounds are now 1280×960. The front/rear fake UVC descriptors now hold one 1280×960 YUY2 frame (`tools/make_latitude_uvc_desc.py` resizes the 640×480 frame and recomputes bit rates).
    - **Deploy bridge, launcher, module and both descriptors together:** with any old piece the HAL finds no matching size.
  - Tests: detail resolves 4 px stripes (control without detail: none); stripe fringes (control without guard); colour unchanged by detail on 12 patches; NIR-rich dark green stays green with k_B 0.75 (control 0.60: teal); frame-size list with Latitude bounds; launcher bounds = bridge size. Host, ASan/UBSan, TSan, docker suites pass.
  - Unverified on hardware:
    - that the HAL takes 1280×960 YUYV and what extra CPU it spends;
    - the real per-object NIR response;
    - fringes on the real blinds;
    - frame rate with both cameras;
    - the higher memory (bridge planes ~16 MB, loopback 2.4 MB per buffer).

Five files on the stick, all verified by sha256: `installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` dc9c4595…, `installer/bin/ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` 03bf261c…, `kmod/v4l2loopback.ko` 8a2cb95e… (vermagic `6.18.21-gbos-whl SMP preempt mod_unload modversions` and the appended signature, same as the old module), `surface/latitude_fake_uvc_desc.bin` b974b2d0…, `surface/latitude_rear_fake_uvc_desc.bin` 6dc902c0…. They only work together. *Next boot:* the camera opens at all (if not: `configure V4L2 loopback output` in camera-bridge-runtime.txt, and the HAL's chosen size in logcat); sharpness of both cameras; the sunny-window selfie (shirt green, wall teal, no fringes on the blinds); plus the lockdown check list above.
- 2026-11-20 **Camera converter on the GPU (Vulkan compute; host-tested on lavapipe, not yet on the laptop's GPU, not deployed).**
  - *Why:* the CPU converter needs about 27 ms of CPU per 1280×960 frame (2 threads), which caps resolution and costs battery. The image's graphics stack is Mesa's Vulkan driver for this GPU (logcat: `ANGLE ... Vulkan 1.4.348 (Intel(R) Iris(R) Xe Graphics (TGL GT2))`); GLES runs on ANGLE over it.
  - *How:*
    - `kernel/ipu6-gpu.c` (included by `ipu6-loopback.c` with `-DIPU6_GPU`) runs the four per-pixel stages as compute shaders (`kernel/ipu6-gpu/*.comp`): colour grid, chroma box average, colour mix to Y′U′V′, and the output pass (detail, temporal filter, YUYV, rotation). They use the CPU code's integer arithmetic (every intermediate fits 32 bits).
    - The CPU keeps the sparse work: white-balance/AE statistics, AF, choosing the colour matrix and lens shading.
    - SPIR-V is compiled by `tools/build_ipu6_gpu_shaders.sh` (NDK glslc) into the committed `kernel/ipu6-gpu/spirv.h`.
    - libvulkan is dlopen'ed: without it, or on any Vulkan error, the bridge logs `gpu: ... converting on the CPU` and carries on.
    - Every 300 frames it logs `gpu: N ms per frame on <device>`.
  - *Build:* `tools/build_ipu6_loopback_gpu.sh` (Android NDK r27d, installed in `~/Android/Sdk/ndk/27.3.13750724` on the build machine, downloaded with the user's OK). Output: a bionic PIE `usb/installer/bin/vulkan/ipu6-loopback`, which needs only libc/libm/libdl. The static CPU build `usb/installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` stays and is the fallback.
  - *Launcher:*
    - Probes `vulkan/ipu6-loopback --probe-gpu` (10 s limit) and uses it if that works. `CAMERA_GPU=0` in gbos.cfg forces the CPU build.
    - If the GPU bridge doesn't reach streaming, the CPU build is started instead. If a GPU bridge exits later, its restarts use the CPU build.
    - Log: `camera converter: GPU (gpu: Vulkan compute on ...)` or `CPU (...)` in camera-bridge-setup.txt.
  - *Tests:*
    - `tools/test_ipu6_gpu.sh` runs 8 cases (front/rear, lens shading, stripes, NIR, highlights at 20x, 40x rotated) through both paths on lavapipe. Outputs are identical except ≤ 27 of 2.46 M bytes per frame, which differ by 1 (the two float spots).
    - Negative control: the GPU path without the fringe guard is detected. It also passes under ASan/UBSan.
    - Launcher: the converter choice runs with fake GPU builds (ok, failing probe, missing, `CAMERA_GPU=0`), and the supervisor switches to the CPU build after a GPU bridge exits.
  - *Unverified on hardware:*
    - that the bionic binary runs from the stick (linker namespace, SELinux) and finds the Iris Xe Vulkan device;
    - the real GPU time per frame. The CPU's remaining share is about 1 ms of statistics plus copying 10 MB in and 2.4 MB out per frame; a dma-buf import of the IPU6 buffer could remove the 10 MB copy later.
- 2026-11-20 **Also deployed for the same test boot:** the GPU camera converter (camera thread 2b897f4, merged as 977d050; the user approved it in the camera thread). `installer/bin/ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` cdd7ecc3…, `installer/bin/ipu6-loopback` 8d4b4f8b… (static CPU build, now the fallback) and the new `installer/bin/vulkan/ipu6-loopback` e9cb7d8f… (bionic PIE, NDK r27d, dlopens libvulkan), all verified on the stick. The lockdown remounts /postinstall `ro,nosuid,nodev` without noexec; only the logs are noexec, so the binaries still run. *Next boot:* `camera-bridge-setup.txt` says `camera converter: GPU (...)` or `CPU (GPU build unusable: ...)`; the runtime logs have `gpu: converting on ...` and `gpu: N ms per frame`, and no `gpu: ... failed; converting on the CPU`. `CAMERA_GPU=0` forces the CPU build.
- 2026-11-20 **Full camera support, step 1: a test-camera plugin for Google's libcamera camera HAL (host-checked only, not deployed).**
  - *What the image has:*
    - `/vendor/lib64/hw/camera.libcamera_hal_base.so` is libcamera 0.2.5 (AOSP `external/libcamera`, a ChromeOS fork) with its Android HAL layer: all stream sizes, JPEG + EXIF, minigbm buffers, metadata.
    - It runs on every boot as `vendor.camera-provider-2-7-legacy-wrapper` (AIDL `ICameraProvider/legacy/0`; boot-15 kmsg: started, pid 3916) and finds no camera here.
    - It builds no pipeline handlers in. It `dlopen`s every `.so` in `/vendor/lib64/libcamera/pipeline` (`CameraManager::Private::loadPipelineHandlerFromLibrary`); Google ships only Intel's IPU7 handler there (`camera.libipu7_hal_fatcat.so`, Panther Lake).
    - `/vendor/etc/camera/libcamera/camera_hal.yaml` already maps libcamera camera ids `camera0` → front and `camera1` → back. A camera reporting `Location = External` needs no entry.
  - *Plan:* our own pipeline-handler plugin for the IPU6 sensors (raw capture + the bridge's ISP, then the GPU path), so Android gets real cameras: several sizes, full-resolution JPEG, video, controls, and sensors powered only while an app uses them. The USB loopback path stays as fallback until then.
  - *Step 1 (this commit):* `kernel/libcamera-gbos/gbos_test.cpp`, an external "gbOS test camera" with moving colour bars, NV12 at 640×480–2560×1920, modelled on libcamera's virtual handler.
    - Built by `tools/build_libcamera_gbos.sh`, with the NDK and the AOSP libcamera headers (android17-release, downloaded with the user's OK to `build/src/libcamera`; generated headers via its `utils/codegen` scripts).
    - The platform libc++ ABI (`std::__1`, via an overriding `__config_site`) is used instead of the NDK's `std::__ndk1`.
    - It links with `--no-undefined` against the vendor `libc++.so` and `camera.libcamera_hal_base.so`, copied locally from `lapis/parts`, never committed.
    - Output: `usb/installer/lib/libcamera-pipeline/gbos_test.so`.
  - *ABI evidence:*
    - every symbol resolves in Google's binaries;
    - vtable sizes match the vendor's base classes: `PipelineHandler` 120, `Camera::Private` 32, `CameraConfiguration` 40 (the same as Google's IPU7 plugin);
    - checked by `tools/test_libcamera_gbos.sh`, which also checks the committed `.so` against a rebuild.
  - *Launcher (`add_libcamera_plugins`):*
    - Copies `/vendor/lib64/libcamera/pipeline` (Google's plugin included) plus our plugins to `/data/local/tmp/gbos-libcamera-pipeline`, with the original SELinux labels.
    - Bind-mounts it over the vendor directory and restarts the legacy-wrapper service before the HAL/cameraserver restart. `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=0` in gbos.cfg skips it.
