  - *Cause: libcamera test plugin crash loop.* After the launcher restarted `vendor.camera-provider-2-7-legacy-wrapper` with gbos_test.so, the wrapper crashed 23 times in 2 minutes. Each time: `HandleCameraDeviceStatusChange: camera_id: 1000, new_status: PRESENT` during Initialize, then SIGSEGV (null pointer, fault addr 0xd0) in `AidlCameraProvider::HandleCameraDeviceStatusChange` ← `CameraHalManager::setCallbacks` ← `AidlCameraProvider::Create`. The EXTERNAL test camera is reported while the provider object isn't set up yet. init: `process with updatable components ... exited 4 times in 4 minutes` (x20). Neither USB bridge ever saw a consumer, so cameraserver/the app never got to the loopback cameras. Also `libcamera: DmaHeap ... Failed to open /dev/dma_heap/system: Permission denied`.
  - *Workaround (this commit, deployed):* `ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` adds the libcamera plugins only with `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1`; the default is off. The fix itself went back to the camera thread.
  - *GPU converter:* `camera converter: CPU (GPU build unusable: gpu: vkEnumeratePhysicalDevices failed (VkResult -3) ...)`, so the CPU fallback ran as designed (VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED: the vendor Vulkan driver didn't initialise for the bridge process). 1280x960 configured for both bridges ("bridge active: ... to 1280x960 YUYV"), but it couldn't be seen because of the crash loop.
  - *Lockdown check list passes:* `hook: POSTINSTALL: /postinstall read-only for Android; logs/boot-16 writable by root only` and `... fingerprint.txt writable by system through /dev/gbos-system-log/fingerprint.txt`, no POSTINSTALL-RW and no bind FAILED; all logs present (fingerprint.txt 2836 bytes with 4 identify lines); no "cannot open log"; soft_gk enrol/verify OK. The shutdown check is pending (read on the next boot). crash.txt is 114 KB from the crash loop. ai-*.txt have been missing since boot-14, before the lockdown, so they're unrelated; not investigated.
  - *Lockdown confirmed on hardware* (cross-checked by the FAT-permissions session): both POSTINSTALL lines at 33.3 s and 33.6 s in kmsg, no POSTINSTALL-RW, 28 gbos_fp lines in fingerprint.txt. init's early-init `restorecon /postinstall` (init.rc:32) now fails with "Read-only file system". On boot-14 and boot-15 it failed with "Operation not supported", because vfat has no xattrs, so only the errno changed: harmless. The `/cache` chown/chmod EROFS lines come from the erofs system image and predate the lockdown.
- 2026-11-20 **Boot-16 follow-up: libcamera test camera made internal, crash guard, GPU driver fallback (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Crash cause, confirmed in the source:*
    - `CameraHalManager::setCallbacks()` (src/android/camera_hal_manager.cpp) reports every **external** camera `CAMERA_DEVICE_STATUS_PRESENT` immediately.
    - Google's adapter calls `setCallbacks` inside `AidlCameraProvider::Create`, before the provider object exists, so `HandleCameraDeviceStatusChange` dereferences null. Every external camera crashes it; Google's IPU7 setup only has internal ones.
  - *Fix:*
    - `gbos_test.so` now registers an **internal** camera, id `camera1`, Location Back, Rotation 0. The image's `camera_hal.yaml` maps `camera1` to the back camera, and internal cameras get no status callback. Rule for every gbOS plugin: no external cameras, no cameras added after start.
    - The DMA heap is opened only when the pipeline exports buffers (boot-16: `Failed to open /dev/dma_heap/system: Permission denied` at load; the HAL normally passes its own gralloc buffers).
  - *Guard (launcher):* after restarting `vendor.camera-provider-2-7-legacy-wrapper` with the plugins, it looks at the process after 3 s and again 8 s later. If the pid is missing or changed (crash loop), it removes the bind mount and restarts the service as shipped, logging `WARNING: ... crashed or restarted with the plugins; removing them for this boot`. Plugins stay off unless `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` (ffedd4b).
  - *GPU on boot-16:* `vkEnumeratePhysicalDevices failed (VkResult -3)`. Android's loader found no device for the bridge started from the stick; the only bridge denials were on its video nodes, in permissive mode. The driver is `/vendor/lib64/hw/vulkan.intel.so` (`ro.hardware.vulkan=intel`), a hardware module.
    - When the loader yields nothing, the Android build now opens that module itself (`HMI` → open `vk0` → the driver's `GetInstanceProcAddr`, layouts per AOSP hardware/hwvulkan.h) and uses the driver directly.
    - Each failing step prints its reason (`dlerror`, VkResult, device count), and the launcher logs every probe line as `  probe: ...` in camera-bridge-setup.txt.
    - The host GPU test (lavapipe, through the loader) still passes; the fallback path only exists in the Android build and is unverified.
  - Builds: the plugin and the bionic bridge build from relative paths with prefix maps, and refuse outputs that embed a local path.
- 2026-11-20 **Boot-17 (19:24, kernel #62, integration `e8a3103`, live boot, `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` on the stick): camera still failing (user).** Logs in `build/boot-logs/boot-17`.

  - *But camera IDs clash:* CameraService takes the legacy provider's camera as ID 1, so the USB rear (`device@1.0/usb/1`) is rejected: `CameraProviderManager: addDevice: Device device@1.0/usb/1: ID 1 is already in use` and `Unable to enumerate camera device 'device@1.0/usb/1': Invalid argument (-22)`. The camera app (com.google.android.desktop.camera) loads `CameraIdList [CameraId-0, CameraId-1]` (levels LIMITED and FULL) and then never calls `CameraService::connect`: no open attempt in two launches (19:25:37, 19:25:46). Neither bridge saw a consumer. On boot-15 the same app called connect for camera 0 within a millisecond of loading the list.
  - So 1280x960 and the GPU picture are still unseen. *Stick change:* `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=0` in the stick's gbos.cfg, so the next boot tests the USB cameras at 1280x960 with the GPU converter without the test camera. Handed to the camera thread (ID clash, the app's stall, and whether 1280x960 alone opens).
- 2026-11-20 **Boot-17 analysis: libcamera camera ID clash and the camera app stall; CAMERA_SIZE fallback (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *ID clash:* Google's legacy adapter publishes the libcamera camera as `device@1.0/legacy/1`, so it takes public ID 1, and CameraService then refused the USB rear (`device@1.0/usb/1: ID 1 is already in use`). We can't change the adapter's numbering. With `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` the launcher now leaves the USB rear off: test = USB front + libcamera back. When the real plugin takes over, the USB cameras go away anyway.
  - *Stall:*
    - The app (com.google.android.desktop.camera) loaded IDs 0 (USB front, LIMITED) and 1 (libcamera, FULL), logged `Handling first camera set`, then never opened a camera; cameraserver and the HAL logged nothing either. On boot-15 the same app bound camera 0 55 ms after that line.
    - Boot-17 differed in two ways, which the logs can't separate: the libcamera camera, and a front that offers only 1280×960.
    - Found: Google's fork forces FULL level for every libcamera camera (`camera_capabilities.cpp`: "force switch to full mode", hence MANUAL_SENSOR, BURST, …). Our test camera had no `ExposureTime` control, so the HAL left out `SENSOR_INFO_EXPOSURE_TIME_RANGE`, which a FULL/manual-sensor camera must have.
    - `gbos_test.so` now reports `ExposureTime` 100..66666 µs. `AnalogueGain` stays out, so the HAL's default 32..2400 sensitivity range applies. The real plugin must report consistent manual controls.
  - *Fallback:* `CAMERA_SIZE=640x480` in gbos.cfg selects the previous output size: bridge builds in `installer/bin/640x480/` (`-DOUT_WIDTH=640 -DOUT_HEIGHT=480`, colour grid 320×240, luma detail at 640×480), loopback bounds 640×480 and the `-640x480` descriptors (byte-identical to the pre-1280 ones). The default (1280×960) binaries are unchanged. The host, docker and GPU suites also run the 640×480 build.

On a tap it crashes: `java.lang.IllegalStateException: Required value was null` (twice, 19:41:30 and 19:41:44, from `dcg.dispatchTouchEvent`). The GPU path can't be the cause, since no frame was ever requested. So with 1280x960 as the only size, the app finds no stream configuration it accepts (perhaps needing a size ≤ some limit, a 16:9 size matching the 1920x1080 active array, or several sizes).

- 2026-11-20 **Boot-18: why Google's camera app rejects 1280×960, and 1920×1080 by default (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Cause, from the app itself* (AL_Camera.apk from lapis/parts, inspected locally with the SDK's dexdump; nothing kept):
    - The crash `IllegalStateException: Required value was null` at `ezv.k`, line 169, is in `com/google/android/desktop/camera/main/MainViewModel`: the state's `Size` (field `fas.b`) is null.
    - That size comes from CameraX's video `Quality` mapping (class `aws`: SD 720×480/640×480, HD 1280×720, FHD 1920×1080, QHD 2560×1440, UHD 3840×2160).
    - The vendor image has no encoder profiles for these cameras (only `media_profiles_camera_fl225f-1b.xml`), so CameraX matches qualities to the camera's sizes. 1280×960 matches none; 640×480 (boot-15) is SD.
    - So boot-17's stall was the same cause, not the libcamera camera.
  - *Change:*
    - Output 1920×1080 by default. That is also the active array Google's camera table declares for the front's `0408:4052` identity.
    - The 4:3 sensors are cropped to a centred 16:9 band (`sensor_crop`: front 2592×1458 from row 243, rear 3280×1845 from row 309), with no stretching.
    - The crop drives the colour grid, the detail pass, the lens-shading map and the WB/AE statistics, on the CPU and in the GPU shaders. 4:3 outputs crop (almost) nothing.
    - `CAMERA_SIZE=1280x720` or `640x480` select builds in `installer/bin/<size>/` (`-DOUT_WIDTH/-DOUT_HEIGHT`), that size's descriptors and loopback bounds. `1280x960` and anything else fall back to 1920×1080 with a warning.
    - No module change: v4l2loopback's list already has 1920×1080 and 1280×720.
  - *Cost:* the CPU converter at 1920×1080 is 35 ms/frame (2 threads, ~28 fps); the GPU path (working since boot-17) is the default.
  - *Tests:* `test_sensor_crop` (aspect, centring, 4:3 untouched); the scene and lens-shading tests are now crop-aware. The host, docker and GPU suites run 1920×1080, 1280×720 and 640×480. The launcher size block is tested for '', 1920x1080, 1280x720, 640x480, 1280x960 and invalid values.
  - *Unverified:* that the app accepts the 16:9 FHD size, and the USB HAL's extra CPU at 1920×1080. `CAMERA_SIZE=640x480` (on the stick now) should work as on boot-15: the same builds as deployed, crop = full sensor.

In the stick's gbos.cfg, `CAMERA_SIZE=640x480` is commented out; uncommenting it is the fallback, with no redeploy. *Next boot:* the camera opens at 1920x1080 (camera-bridge-setup.txt `camera output: 1920x1080`, bridges logging `consumer streaming`), the GPU converter, sharpness, colour.

- 2026-11-20 **Boot-19 front green at 1920×1080: diagnostics and the speckle fix (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *What the logs show:* the front's only colour lines were logged while idle (frame 900: grey-world 0.573/0.395, green side 0.141, 45 of 48 regions; frame 1800: 0.612/0.588, magenta side 0.010). The front logged nothing about colour while the app used it.
  - *Checked, not the cause:*
    - The crop keeps the CFA phase: sites are looked up in absolute sensor coordinates, and `crop_y` is even (242 for the front).
    - CPU and GPU agree at 1920×1080 on every synthetic case.

    - `tools/ipu6_raw_render.c` (`-DOUT_WIDTH/-DOUT_HEIGHT` = the dump's size; `-DRAW_RENDER_GPU --gpu` for lavapipe) renders a dump with the same converter, writes YUYV, and compares U/V with the laptop's picture. `tools/yuyv_to_png.py` turns either into a PNG. Round trip tested in `tools/test_ipu6_raw_render.sh`.

    - If the dump looks right, the green is added after the bridge (HAL/app).

Boot-19's green is not reproduced. The chroma-radius scaling is the only colour change since then, so the scene/light or the idle-time logging may have misled us on boot-19.
  - *Hiccup = the dump write:* the rear's first app session failed at 21:55:48. The USB HAL logged `v4l2 dequeue timeout, stop retrying`, then `Camera 1: notifyError: Camera HAL reported serious device error`, and the app closed camera 1; the next session worked. The rear bridge had just written `raw-ov8856-1` (16 MB raw + 4 MB YUYV) to the FAT stick from its frame loop. *Stick:* `#CAMERA_RAW_DUMP=2` (off). The dump should be written off the frame loop.

G=345` against a target of 173; dumps at 11.6x then 7.2x, the wall blown out). AE needs a faster correction when far off target at session start.

- 2026-11-20 **Boot-20 follow-up: the start-up hiccup and the overexposed start (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Hiccup = the raw dump.* The rear's first dump (16 MB raw + 4 MB YUYV onto the FAT stick) was written from the frame loop, which stalled it long enough for the USB HAL to report `v4l2 dequeue timeout`. Dumps are now copied and written by a detached thread (`raw_dump_start`); the `.txt` goes last, so a dump is complete once it exists. One dump at a time: one asked for while another is being written is skipped.
  - *Overexposed start.* The rear's first active frames were about 2x the idle-metered level (wb G 345 vs 174): the idle frame is metered at 8x exposure time and AE then moved at most 1.6x every 3 frames. Now, when a consumer starts, AE runs a fast phase for 12 frames: every 2 frames, up to 4x per step, and while more than 5% of the green samples are within 5% of clipping it halves the exposure at least. The front gets the same. In the synthetic test it settles in 4 frames instead of 8 or more (`test_ae_fast_start`).
  - *GPU = CPU on real frames.* The boot-20 dumps rendered on the PC with the CPU path match the laptop's GPU pictures. Rear: mean |dU| 0.03–0.07, |dV| up to 0.13. Front, with the module's tables: |dU| 0.05, |dV| 0.07, worst 2 steps. The GPU path is not the cause of colour problems.
  - *Boot-19's front green stays unexplained.* `chroma_radius` explains the speckle, not a global green cast. The boot-20 front, with the same tables and light, is natural. If it comes back, the in-session colour lines and a `CAMERA_RAW_DUMP` dump will show whether it comes from the bridge.
  - *Not done: a second listed size.* v4l2loopback is single-format and the HAL may pick either listed size, which would not match the bridge's output (the 2026-11-17 incident). The 320x240 overlay stream it rejects is harmless.
- 2026-11-20 **libcamera route, step 1: the real rear camera through Google's libcamera HAL (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *Design:* the sensor, AE/AWB/AF and the raw→YUV conversion stay in the `ipu6-loopback` bridge, a separate process. With the output `channel:camera1` instead of `/dev/video65`, it hands each converted frame to the new plugin `kernel/libcamera-gbos/gbos_ipu6.cpp` (`usb/installer/lib/libcamera-pipeline/gbos_ipu6.so`) over a frame channel (`kernel/ipu6-channel.h`).
    - The channel is the abstract unix socket `@gbos-camera:camera1` (SOCK_SEQPACKET, no file, so no permissions or labels), plus a memfd with 4 YUYV slots. Each frame message carries the slot, a sequence number, the capture timestamp, the exposure time and the gains.
    - One client at a time; a new connection replaces the old one. A connected client counts as the loopback reader did, so idle, conversion, the AE fast start and the idle stop work unchanged. A frame message the client can't take at once is dropped; the bridge never waits.
    - A bridge crash can't take the camera service down: the plugin keeps the app's requests and reconnects every 500 ms.
  - *Plugin:* INTERNAL `camera1` (Location Back; `camera_hal.yaml` maps it to the back camera). It registers only when `/proc/net/unix` shows the bridge's channel.
    - NV12 streams at any even size up to 1920×1440, up to 3 at once. Listed: 640×480, 1280×720, 1280×960, 1440×1080, 1920×1080, 1920×1440; the HAL adds 320×240 and 640×360. Each stream is a centred crop of the picture at the stream's aspect, scaled bilinearly (`gbos_convert.h`); 1920×1080 from 1920×1440 is a plain copy.
    - JPEG, EXIF and thumbnails come from Google's HAL layer (`PostProcessorJpeg`, `EncoderLibJpeg` in `camera.libcamera_hal_base.so`).
    - Metadata per request: SensorTimestamp, ExposureTime, AnalogueGain, FrameDuration. Controls as the test camera: FrameDurationLimits 33.3 ms, ExposureTime 100–66666 µs (needed for the FULL level Google forces); manual exposure isn't applied yet.
    - Buffers are written through `MappedFrameBuffer` with `DMA_BUF_IOCTL_SYNC` around the CPU writes.
  - *Bridge:* new 4:3 builds `installer/bin/1920x1440/{,vulkan/}ipu6-loopback`, used for the libcamera rear whatever `CAMERA_SIZE` says.
  - *Launcher:*
    - `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` now runs the rear bridge with `channel:camera1` (no `/dev/video65`, UVC identity or facing entry) and adds only `gbos_ipu6.so`. `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=test` adds only `gbos_test.so`, as before.
    - The rear's GPU/CPU choice follows the front's, and it falls back to CPU on its own.
    - If the crash guard removes the plugins, the rear is unavailable for that boot; the front is unaffected.
  - *Tests:*
    - `test_frame_channel`: connect/hello/memfd, slots and sequence numbers, metadata, replacement, disconnect, no blocking with a client that doesn't read, a second bridge refused, at every build size.
    - `tools/test_libcamera_gbos_convert.c`: exact copy, uniform colours exact at all sizes, centred crops, colours in place, padding untouched.
    - The launcher tests cover the rear mode, the rear build choice, the rear GPU→CPU restart with the channel output, and the choice of plugin. vtable sizes are checked against the vendor library for the new classes.
  - *Unverified on hardware:*
    - that the app opens the libcamera camera at all (boot-17's stall was the front's 1280×960 size, but the libcamera camera itself was never seen in the app);
    - the stride of the HAL's gralloc NV12 buffers (taken from the plane length, as the test camera did);
    - the GPU time at 1920×1440 (expected ~26–31 ms from boot-19's 20–23 ms at 1080p, close to the 33 ms frame);
    - SELinux denials (permissive) for the provider connecting to the bridge.
  - *To test:* `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` in the stick's gbos.cfg. Look for:
    - in `camera-bridge-setup.txt`: `rear camera: libcamera` and `Rear bridge ready (libcamera frame channel channel:camera1)`;
    - in the rear runtime log: `frame channel: client connected`;
    - in logcat: `GbosIpu6 ... camera1: registered`, `frame channel connected`, `30 frames delivered`.
- 2026-11-20 **Deployed (files only): libcamera step 1, b60e6b1** (the real rear camera through Google's libcamera HAL: the rear bridge outputs 1920x1440 NV12 over the frame channel `@gbos-camera:camera1` (abstract socket + memfd slots) to the new `installer/lib/libcamera-pipeline/gbos_ipu6.so`, an INTERNAL back camera "camera1"; with it there is no USB rear and no /dev/video65). Ten files on the stick, all verified by sha256: the four size builds of `ipu6-loopback` (static and vulkan, new `1920x1440/`), `ipu6-camera-bridge.sh` 1810c07e…, `gbos_ipu6.so` 200e89a5…; gbos_test.so unchanged. It is only active with `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` (the stick still has 0, so the next boot runs the USB cameras as on boot-20, plus the fast-AE fix). Checks for the boot with it on: `rear camera: libcamera (gbos_ipu6.so)`, `Rear bridge ready (libcamera frame channel channel:camera1)`, `libcamera plugins: ... gbos_ipu6.so ... stable (pid N)`; `frame channel: client connected`; logcat `camera1: registered`, `stream N NV12 WxH`, `30 frames delivered`; no legacy-wrapper SIGSEGV; avc for socket or /proc/net/unix. Risks: the app may not open the libcamera camera, or a skewed or striped picture (gralloc stride guessed).
- 2026-11-21 **Boot-21 (11:01, kernel #62, integration `cacfed0`, live boot, `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1`): the rear (libcamera) failed; the front is green in daylight (user).** Logs in `build/boot-logs/boot-21`.
  - *libcamera rear loaded fine:* `dlopen ... gbos_ipu6.so successfully`, `GbosIpu6 gbos_ipu6.cpp:518 camera1: registered (OV8856 (gbOS IPU6))`, the wrapper `stable (pid 7729)`, 320x240–1920x1440 NV12 supported. The rear bridge ran `to 1920x1440 YUYV via the frame channel @gbos-camera:camera1` but never got a client.
  - *Why it failed:* the app connects camera ID 1 and asks for three streams: `1440x1080 NV12 (0x22)`, `1920x1440 MJPEG (BLOB 0x21)`, `1440x1080 NV12 (0x23)`. libcamera answers `'camera1': Camera configuration adjusted` (to 1440x1080, 1440x1080, 1920x1440 NV12), and the framework rejects it: `Camera 1: configureStreamsLocked: Set of requested inputs/outputs not supported by HAL`. This happened on every try. gbos_ipu6.so's validate() must accept the requested sizes as they are (scale each stream from the 1920x1440 source) instead of adjusting.
  - Also check: camera_hal_config lists `'camera0' (1)[0]` and `'camera1' (0)[0]`. In Android, facing 0 is FRONT, so camera1 may be registered as front-facing, not back.
  - *Front green in daylight:* NIR 0.4–0.8 of green. Grey-world R/G is 0.33–0.44, but every streaming line uses R/G ≈ 0.476–0.517 with `gain R=2.10` (a cap at 1/0.476), so red is under-corrected and the picture comes out green/cyan. With k_R = 1, high NIR also likely over-subtracts red. Boot-20 (indoor, NIR 0.03) was fine.
  - *Stick:* `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=0` again (USB rear). Handed to the camera thread.
- 2026-11-21 **Boot-21 follow-up: libcamera rear stream setup, front green in daylight, both-cameras mode (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *(1) Rear via libcamera: why every open failed.*
    - Google's HAL asks for its JPEG source as an **MJPEG** StillCapture stream (`buildStreamConfigsNoMap`), and treats any `Adjusted` result from `validate()` as a failure (`configureStreams` → "Set of requested inputs/outputs not supported by HAL").
    - Its JPEG encoder and thumbnailer read NV12, in YCbCr_420_888 buffers it allocates itself. `gbos_ipu6.cpp` changed MJPEG to NV12 and said `Adjusted`; it now turns MJPEG into NV12 without reporting an adjustment.
    - The app's set was 1440×1080 PRIVATE, 1440×1080 YUV_420_888 and a 1920×1440 JPEG; the HAL keeps the two 1440×1080 streams separate. Up to 4 streams are allowed now.
    - Row stride: for the HAL's buffers (`HALFrameBuffer`, recognised by type name) it now comes from `crosLibcamera::CameraBufferManager::GetPlaneStride`, which `camera.libcamera_hal_base.so` exports; the length-based guess remains only for our own DmaHeap buffers. Writes are checked against the plane sizes.
    - Facing is right: `camera_hal.yaml` is camera0 front / camera1 back, rotation 0; the yaml log's "(0)" is HAL3 `CAMERA_FACING_BACK`, and the app logged camera 1 as "Back (Physical, Full)".
  - *(2) Front green in daylight: green keeps more NIR than the model removes.*
    - Boot-21 (sunlit room, NIR 0.40–0.82 of green) and boot-15 (window light): grey-world R/G and B/G are **both** low, 0.10–0.26 off the module's locus on the green side. The band could only pull the white point 0.08 off (red gain capped at ~2.10), so the picture stayed green.
    - One extra green term, G = G_site − IR − e·n, with e = 0.39 (boot-21) or 0.40 (boot-15), puts every frame of both boots within 0.005–0.07 of the locus, between D65 and D50, and a fit on either boot predicts the other. Red-only or red+blue fits are worse or ambiguous.
    - But boot-12's window light (NIR 0.43, 1×) already sat on the locus, and a fixed e = 0.39 would push it 0.12–0.19 towards magenta.
    - So e is estimated per scene: the value in 0–0.45 that brings the frame's grey-world closest to the locus. It fades in from NIR 0.20 (lamps and LEDs are never touched) and from 0.04 off the locus (boot-12 stays as it was), and is smoothed over frames. `nir_keep_q12[1] = −e`, so the whole picture is corrected on CPU and GPU. Logged in the `ir:` line as "green NIR excess".
    - *Intel's values checked first* (user's question): the OV5678 `.aiqb` record 27 holds, per calibration light, three 15×9 IR weight grids (centre ~61–63 for D50/D65, ~24–29 for D75) and the lights' IR/G ratios (A 0.79, others ~0.10). Taken as subtraction weights (64 = 1.0, every channel order), no light gives a self-consistent white, and D50/D65 make boot-21 worse (0.25–0.34 off). Their formula and scale are Intel's closed algorithm's. The AIQ tuning has a DFLT and an IRVI (IR) variant that differ in 8 of 11 records, but those records are fixed-point and undocumented.
    - Tests: `test_daylight_green_nir`: boot-21's grey comes out neutral with e in range; negative control without the estimate gives U, V ≈ −9 (the laptop's green); no NIR gives e = 0; sunlit foliage over a third of the frame keeps the grey neutral and the foliage green. `test_daylight_ir_grey` checks that boot-12's daylight gets no correction. All at 4 sizes, plus CPU = GPU.
  - *(3) `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=both` (the goal: both cameras through libcamera):*
    - The front bridge also runs from the 1920×1440 builds with `channel:camera0`, and `gbos_ipu6.so` registers camera0 (front) as well as camera1. No v4l2loopback module and no UVC identity are made, so the USB HAL has no cameras.
    - If the plugin can't be used (crash guard, missing file, bind mount failure), the launcher stops both bridges and runs itself again with `GBOS_CAMERA_USB_FALLBACK=1` (CAMERA_LIBCAMERA reads as 0): the cameras come back on the USB route that boot.
    - `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=1` stays rear-only, `=0` the USB route.
  - *Unverified on hardware:* the app's whole libcamera session (stream start, frames, JPEG); the public IDs with two libcamera cameras (the HAL numbers them 0, 1 in registration order; the adapter published the lone back camera as 1); the fallback re-exec; e on real frames.
- 2026-11-21 **Deployed (files): the boot-21 fixes, 3d6283f / db67f12.** libcamera: MJPEG StillCapture is mapped to NV12 without reporting Adjusted, up to 4 streams, the real HAL buffer stride via CameraBufferManager::GetPlaneStride. Front daylight: a per-scene "green NIR excess" (0–0.45). New `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=both` (both cameras via gbos_ipu6.so, falling back to USB if the plugin can't run). Ten files on the stick, verified by sha256 (launcher ad548a24…, gbos_ipu6.so bcb9df1b…, the eight size builds). The stick's settings stayed at `CAMERA_LIBCAMERA=0` and no raw dump until the user confirms.
- 2026-11-21 **Calibration shot (boot-23 vs Windows): front white point and red fixed against ground truth; the per-scene green correction removed (host-tested only, not deployed).**
  - *The shot:* the user photographed a Portuguese flag (white shield, red, green, yellow, blue) with the front camera in three lights: natural light in a teal room, a bathroom lamp, direct sun on a balcony. First on Windows (Camera Roll, EXIF OV5678: Intel's pipeline as the reference), then on gbOS with `CAMERA_RAW_DUMP=3` (boot-23 `raw-ov5678-{1,2,3}`; boot-22's dumps were taken before the camera pointed at the flag). The photos stay local.
  - *What the raw frames say:*
    - Direct sun: the white shield sits 0.05 from the module's locus with the infrared model as it was (no green term). So the boot-21 green term had the wrong cause, and it is **removed**.
    - Natural light in the teal room: the shield is 0.17 off on the green side. The light itself is green, bounced off the walls; Windows still renders the shield white.
    - Lamp: the shield is 0.06 off, the white tiles 0.14 (green side).
    - Red flag fabric reflects 2–6x more NIR than its green (1.4x its red).
  - *What the bridge did wrong (rendered with the bridge's CPU path from the raw frames and compared patch by patch with Windows):*
    - Sun: the large red flag dragged the white point warm (R/G 0.63 for a white at 0.46), so the shield came out light blue (U +30, V −26).
    - Natural light: the colour guard measured the red's NIR against its tiny green, so it read as "NIR-dominated" and was faded to brown (V +8 vs Windows +54).
  - *Fixes:*
    - The white point now comes from the **brightest** regions a light could have lit: weight (G/G_max)^3 over 16x12 regions (were 8x6, each weighing its brightness once).

    - Sun white 37 → 10, lamp tiles 10 → 2, natural red 48 → 18.

  - *Still off:*
    - Natural light: the shield stays cyan (~25). A gilded picture frame in that frame is brighter than the shield, and a uniformity weight didn't help because the shield's regions contain the crests.
    - Saturated colours in sun (red, yellow) lean orange and over-saturated vs Windows. Not tuned further on three photos.

Region-count checks are now relative to 16x12. All suites at 4 sizes, CPU = GPU.

- 2026-11-19 **SoundWire link 2 "Bus clash" / "Clock stop failed -110" (diagnostic item S3): cause found; link 2 is now left powered off on the Latitude 7320 Detachable (built and deployed to the stick, NOT yet booted).**
  - *What the logs show (boots 8-11, kernel #61):* exactly 10 `Bus clash for control word` + 1 `Bus clash for data word` on `soundwire_intel.link.2` about 10 ms after the SOF firmware boots. That is the `dev_err_ratelimited` burst, so there were probably more. Then, 3.5 s later (`INTEL_MASTER_SUSPEND_DELAY_MS` = 3000 plus idle), `Clock stop failed -110` / `intel_stop_bus: cannot stop clock: -110`. After that, nothing for the rest of the boot. The logs are identical whether SOF starts at 28.8 s (boot-8) or 17.5 s (boot-11), and only link 2 is affected. So it is not module order, coldplug timing or the TPS68470 (I2C camera PMIC, unrelated to the PCH SoundWire links).
  - *Cause:* nothing is fitted on link 2. `hwdetect.txt` lists `sdw:0:2:025d:1308:00` (an RT1308 that the ACPI declares) with no driver and no modalias. It never attached. Windows on this laptop (SKU 0A45, Intel SST SoundWire driver 10.29.0.12635) enumerates only `SNDW\...PART_0711...CI_00`, `PART_1316...CI_01` and `PART_0714...CI_03`: no device on link 2, present or not. The DSDT (read from `HKLM\HARDWARE\ACPI\DSDT`) takes each link's `intel-quirk-mask` from NVS (`SWQ0..SWQ3`), and SWQ2 does not set BUS_DISABLE. Linux therefore powers up and starts an empty link. With nothing driving it, the master sees clashes. At runtime suspend the clock-stop handshake never completes, and the link is powered down anyway (`intel_stop_bus` continues after the error). No function was lost: the selected topology `sof-tgl-rt711-l0-rt1316-l1-mono-rt714-l3.tplg` never uses link 2.
  - *Why not `snd_intel_sdw_acpi.sdw_link_mask=0xB`:* (built-in, so it would go on the cmdline). The match entry `tgl_3_in_1_sdca_mono` in `soc-acpi-intel-tgl-match.c` requires `link_mask = 0xF` (upstream comment: "link_mask should be 0xB, but all links are enabled by BIOS"). With 0xB the subset check skips it and `tgl_rvp_headset_only` (`sof-tgl-rt711.tplg`) is selected: no speakers, no internal microphone. `sdw_clock_stop_quirks` applies to all links and would keep the DSP awake. `soundwire_cadence.cnds_mcp_int_mask` would only hide clashes on every link.
  - *Fix:* `kernel/isa_emu_patch/sdw_link_skip.py` (applied by `build_kernel.sh`) adds a `soundwire_intel` parameter, `sdw_link_skip_mask` (one bit per link, default 0 = upstream behaviour). It sets `prop->hw_disabled` like the BIOS BUS_DISABLE quirk does at the driver level. The link stays in the controller's link mask, so the same machine entry and topology are selected. The ACPI peripheral and the auxiliary device still exist. `intel_link_startup()` returns before powering the link up, and every PM callback already skips hw_disabled links. This is the state link 2 reaches today after 3.5 s, minus the failed start and stop. On DMI `Dell Inc.` / `Latitude 7320 Detachable` only, `usb/hwdetect.sh` writes `options soundwire_intel sdw_link_skip_mask=4` before MODOPTS. `MODOPTS="soundwire_intel.sdw_link_skip_mask=0"` restores the upstream behaviour. A module without the parameter ignores it ("unknown parameter"). The hwdetect report's `== soundwire` section now also shows each peripheral's `status` and the parameter value.
  - *Build:* only `soundwire-intel.ko` was rebuilt in `gbos-ksrc` (incremental `make modules`; the stray `/build/src/.config` was moved aside and put back). The bzImage is still byte-identical to `usb/out/BOOTX64.EFI` (#61, `55521e63...`). The module was stripped and signed with the tree key (`sig_key 2F:5D:4A:...`, same as deployed). vermagic is unchanged and all 127 `__versions` CRCs equal the deployed module's. New module SHA-256 `4859dd3f594f6cbf7720ccee229790a516fee0e18d500060575d00640d66f88c`; the previous one (`fa1a20f0...`) is kept in ignored `build/sdw-link-skip/`. `gbos-build-id` was left alone, as for `ov5675.ko` on 2026-11-18. A single-target `make drivers/soundwire/soundwire-intel.ko` fails in modpost here; use `make modules`.
  - *Tests:* `tools/test_sdw_link_skip.sh` covers the DMI gate, the MODOPTS override (also with a dash), the ordering before MODOPTS, the report line, and the patch on the 6.18 anchors (applies, idempotent, aborts unchanged on a changed source). `test_audio_config`, `test_detect_hardware`, `test_tablet_mode`, `test_boot_tuning`, `test_module_build_id` and `sh -n` pass.
  - *Check on the next boot (`stage0-dmesg.txt`, `hwdetect.txt`):*
    - `hwdetect: soundwire_intel sdw_link_skip_mask=4`;
    - `soundwire_intel.link.2: gbos: link 2 left powered off` and `SoundWire master 2 is disabled`;
    - no `Bus clash` and no `Clock stop failed` lines;
    - the same topology `sof-tgl-rt711-l0-rt1316-l1-mono-rt714-l3.tplg`;
    - `soundwire_intel sdw_link_skip_mask=4` and RT711/RT1316/RT714 `status=Attached` in `hwdetect.txt`;
    - speakers, headphones, the internal mic and the headset mic all still work, also after a suspend/resume.

- 2026-11-20 **System updates without the stick (built and tested on the PC, not on hardware; not deployed).** `docs/system-update.md`.
  - *What:* a signed bundle (`gbos86-update-<seq>-<id>/`: manifest, manifest.sig, changed files) copied into `gbos/update.inbox/` on GBOSSYS
    (from Windows, `sysupdate.sh stage` as root, or the new gbOS86 Updater app) is checked and switched in by stage0 at the next start, from
    `early_patch.sh` (works on today's kernel; `stage0_init.sh` has a 4-line fallback that needs a kernel build). Rollback after 3 starts that
    do not reach `sys.boot_completed`; held instead for a new Google build that Android already ran (`/data` may be upgraded).
  - *Signature:* RSA-3072 PKCS#1 v1.5 / SHA-256 checked in busybox `bc` (0.37 s here), whole encoded block compared. `usb/sysupdate.pub` is a
    placeholder: laptops refuse every bundle until the project's public key is committed there (`tools/make_system_update.sh keygen`, offline).
  - *Safety details:* the kernel is replaced by a rename over `BOOTX64.EFI` (never missing) and backed up on GBOSSYS, not the EFI partition;
    a journal undoes a power cut at any step (tested at every step, kernel switch included); the code that undoes it is the copy that wrote it
    (`update/engine.sh`); only `EFI/gbos86` or `EFI/BOOT` on a `GBOS_EFI` partition is written, never a shared ESP's fallback loader.
  - *Menu 1:* shows both sets, refuses an older Google build unless `DOWNGRADE` is typed, trusts `.stamps` only for the same
    `system.manifest`, never skips `super.img` when `super.layout`/`libkm_compat.so`/`system_ext_manifest.xml`/`patched/MANIFEST` changed.
  - *App:* `updater-app/` (no permissions; a root init service does the copy, from the app's private request file only). Built with JDK 17,
    platform 35, build-tools 35.0.0 (downloaded with the user's approval into `~/Android/Sdk`): 16.8 KB, test key, not committed.
  - *Stick check:* a read-only listing of the stick's firmware/modules/... (5,724 entries) has only names the manifest rules accept.
  - *Tests:* `tools/test_system_update.sh` (96 checks, `SYSTEM_UPDATE_TEST_PASS`). Untested on hardware: the erofs loop-mount build check,
    the EFI partition lookup with `sfdisk`, the restart after a kernel change, the tty1 messages, the app and its init service.
  - *First hardware test:* commit the real public key, deploy, menu 1 once (puts `sysupdate.sh` and the key on the laptop), boot the
    laptop, copy a small delta bundle into `update.inbox` from Windows, restart. Read `gbos/update/status.txt` and `kmsg.log`'s
    `gbos-sysupdate:` lines (menu 1's `nvme-logs`). Then try one that changes the kernel.
- 2026-11-20 **Updates from GitHub in the gbOS86 Updater app, no second PC (built and tested on the PC, not on hardware; not deployed).**
  - *Publish:* `tools/publish_system_update.sh --key KEY --tree <stick's gbos/>` builds the set from a pushed commit, makes a full and a
    delta bundle **without `super.img`** (GitHub's 2 GiB limit; Google's image is not ours to redistribute), signs a feed and, after asking,
    creates release `sysupdate-<seq>` and replaces `gbos86-update.feed` on release `sysupdate-feed` with `gh`. Nothing was uploaded.
  - *App (v2):* checks after each start (the boot service broadcasts `BOOT_CHECK`) and daily, notifies, downloads (resume, sha256 from the
    signed feed), stages from its own storage (`where app`), falls back from the delta to the full bundle on `NEEDS-FILES`. Settings tabs
    General / Experimental features. New permissions: internet, network state, notifications, boot, foreground service.
  - *Modification check:* `sysupdate.sh audit` (files vs `system.manifest`; added files in the set's folders; `gbos.cfg`/logs ignored;
    big files by size unless deep) + the set's commit (`-dirty`; clean ones looked up on GitHub). A modified system gets a warning and a
    second confirmation before installing. `make_system_update.sh manifest` now records `-dirty` when the tree differs from HEAD.
  - *Experimental, off by default:* rebuild `super.img` from Google's `recovery.zip` (range-read zip directory, one inflate stream, GPT +
    liblp, Java port of `make_super.py`; resumes a broken connection with the same inflater). `tools/super_parts.py` proves a published
    `super.img` is make_super.py's image of Google's partitions: true for 16693588 with `lapis/parts` (`google_super 3688890368 e94590cf...`).
  - *Tests:* `test_system_update.sh` 116 checks; `test_updater_core.sh` (feed, RSA vs openssl, resume, tar, Java vs make_super.py, Google-like
    zip64 with split/out-of-order/zero extents and a dropped connection). By hand: the real 16693588 partitions in a 3.2 GB zip64 test zip
    -> the Java code rebuilt the identical 3.7 GB `super.img` in 29 s. Never run: GitHub redirects, Google's real zip, the app on Android.
- 2026-11-20 **Updater app: the "Modified system" warning now also covers unknown systems (PC only, not on hardware).** Before, the app
  asked "Install anyway" only for changed files or a `-dirty` build. A laptop installed from a stick deployed with local changes has no
  `system.manifest` (deploy_stick.sh deletes it), and a commit not on GitHub only got a line of text, so an update could replace local
  work without asking. Now any system that is not exactly a published set needs the confirmation (codes in `Updater.warningCodes`), and the
  accepted reasons go to `gbos/update/history.txt` through the request's new `accepted_warning` line.

  (Nov 6) are R4, and make_super.py of them gives exactly the stick's `super.layout` and size. `lapis/BUILD` and `usb/patched/MANIFEST`
  say R5 since the 2026-11-13 port, but no R5 `super.img` was ever put on the stick. The patches still bind (the originals' hashes are the
  same in R4 and R5). `make_system_update.sh` refuses such a set (super.img's fingerprint differs from the MANIFEST's), which is right; and
  `publish_system_update.sh` used to carry on after that refusal (the build's exit status was lost in a pipe): it now stops and removes
  the half-made release (test added).

- 2026-11-22 **Updater: staging failed on the persistent stick (read-only update.inbox); new way to write GBOSSYS from Android
  (PC and container only, not on hardware).** The app's "Download and install" on lab update 202611212051 failed: `mkdir:
  can't create directory '/postinstall/gbos/update.inbox': Read-only file system`. The namespace re-run (3558f76) works with
  the stick's own busybox on a real kernel (privileged container, the same mounts the lockdown makes), so the hardware cause is
  **not proven**. The boot's logs were lost to FAT damage. Two candidates: (1) the kernel made the FAT itself read-only after a FAT
  error that boot (`errors=remount-ro`; the same session damaged the logs), which no remount of a bind can undo; (2) something
  Android-specific broke the namespace re-run. Now stage0 makes `/dev/gbos-rw/stick` (hook.sh `gbos_root_rw`: a rw, nosuid, nodev,
  noexec bind of `/gbos` inside a 0700 root directory on /dev) and sysupdate.sh writes through it, with no namespace. It keeps the
  namespace re-run only for older hooks. It probes with a real mkdir (busybox `test -w` is always true for root, so the old check
  could never fail). It refuses with the reason ("FAT is read-only for everyone" / "stayed read-only in its own namespace" / "no
  /dev/gbos-rw"). It logs mount lines and its choice to `logs/boot-N/sysupdate.txt` on every stage/cancel/boot-ok, and to
  `updater-app.txt` every boot. `request` no longer re-runs itself: its stage and cancel children choose for themselves, so their
  errors reach the app. `tools/test_postinstall_ro.sh` (kernel part) runs `sysupdate.sh cancel`/`boot-ok` as init does (empty
  environment, `/postinstall/busybox sh`): through /dev/gbos-rw, then through the namespace fallback, then with the FAT superblock
  read-only. It also checks that uid 10123 and 1000 cannot reach `/dev/gbos-rw/stick`. To try it, the stick needs the new `hook.sh`
  and `sysupdate.sh`; staging through the old copy cannot work.

VcClient::get_manager: connected to ...IVideoChatManager/default`, `DesktopVideoChatManager: registerListener/getSettings`. - 2026-11-22 **FAT dirty after boot-4 (persistent stick): cause found and fixed in `usb/stick_fat.sh` (real vfat driver in a container; not yet on hardware).**
  - *Cause (high confidence, reproduced):* boot-4's own boot check made the volume read-only. boot-4 mounted the volume dirty (`Volume was not properly unmounted`, 9.6 s). At 25.766 s, 8 ms after hook.sh started, `T1860` logged `FAT-fs (sda2): error, fat_bmap_cluster: request beyond EOF (i_pos 143899267)` and `Filesystem has been set read-only`, then 24 more of the same error. That was `gbos_fat_prev_shutdown`'s `tail -c 8192` of boot-3's `kmsg.log`. Boot-3's dirty end had left that file's directory entry claiming more bytes than its cluster chain holds, which is why that boot's fsck said `PROBLEMS FOUND` at 27.08 s. Reading past the chain is a FAT error, and stage0's mount has the default `errors=remount-ro`, so the kernel made the superblock read-only for the rest of the boot. *Real driver (alpine container, loop vfat with stage0's options):* a file with its size raised 64 KB past its chain, read with `tail -c 8192`, logs the same error, and mountinfo shows the superblock `ro`. Creating a new file then fails with EROFS. Files already open keep growing and reach the disk (2001 of 2001 lines), and `mount -o remount,ro` returns 0 even with an open writer. That explains everything boot-4 shows. Its folder holds only `hw.txt`, `hwdetect.txt` and `kmsg.log` (all created before 25.7 s). There is no `fat-check.txt`, no `fat-shutdown.txt` (the "armed" line could not be created at 27.09 s) and no logcat, while `kmsg.log` (open since 14.7 s) ran on to 446.66 s. At power-off the watcher did run (446.613 s), the remount had nothing to do, and the flag was not to be cleared (`clear the flag at shutdown: 0`, since fsck had found problems). The volume had been dirty at mount (`sbi->dirty`), so it stayed dirty. That part is correct behaviour for a damaged volume. Not explained: boot-5's fsck finds boot-3's damage gone (only the dirty bit). Nothing in boot-4 could have repaired it, so something between the boots (Windows?) changed the stick.
  - *Why fat-check said "never started":* the watcher's line is followed by 72 apexd records and one EXT4 record (13 KB) that the copier copied in its last 50 ms, so the 8 KB tail never saw it. With no fat-shutdown.txt, it fell through to "never started".
  - *Not our mounts:* `Service vold has 'reboot_on_failure' option and failed, shutting down system` appears because init SIGKILLs vold itself after `vdc volume shutdown` ("Stopping 4 services by sending SIGKILL"). It is in boot-8, -15 and -18 of the old stick too, and boot-18 ended clean. The `/dev/gbos-rw/stick` bind shares the superblock, so the superblock remount covers it. Nothing in boot-4 points at it.
  - *Fix (`usb/stick_fat.sh` only; hook.sh, install.sh and stage0 unchanged):* (1) The boot check now runs fsck (block-device reads, which cannot trip the FAT driver) **before** it opens any file of the previous boot. It reads them only when the volume is known intact: clean at mount, or fsck found nothing but the dirty flag and lost clusters. Otherwise fat-check.txt says `its files were not read: fsck found problems (in <paths fsck names>)` or `... fsck did not run (...)` and concludes `unknown`. (2) The watcher line is looked for in the last 256 KB, with a count of the records after it. When this mount found the volume dirty, the whole previous kmsg.log is scanned for the kernel's own `Volume was not properly unmounted`, the first `FAT-fs ... error` and `Filesystem has been set read-only`. The conclusion now follows the watcher's line: started at X s; allowed to clear the flag or not (its line now says `and clearing the dirty flag` or `, leaving the dirty flag as it is`); dirty at mount (from kmsg or fat-check.txt). A missing fat-shutdown.txt is reported as "could not be created, read-only from Y s" when that happened. For boot-4 it would now say: `that boot already mounted it dirty and was not allowed to clear the flag ...: it stays dirty whatever the remount did (its shutdown step started at 446.613 s)`, plus the 25.766 s read-only event. (3) Safety net: after the check, if mountinfo shows the volume read-only, `FAT-CHECK` adds `/gbos is READ-ONLY now (FAT error: ...)`. fat-check.txt goes to kmsg as `hook: fat-check.txt (not writable): ...` lines when it cannot be created, and the armed line as `hook: fat: fat-shutdown.txt NOT writable (...)`. The already-open kmsg.log records both. (4) The watcher's shutdown line says `/gbos is ALREADY read-only (a FAT error earlier this boot?)` when it is. Not done: no automatic repair (still "repair on another computer"), and no remount rw after a FAT error (it would keep writing to a volume the kernel found inconsistent).
  - *Tests (`tools/test_stick_fat.sh`):* a boot-4 replica kmsg.log (dirty mount, the error and read-only lines, the watcher line followed by 73 records, more than 8 KB) with three watcher wordings, plus negative controls: no watcher line (still `never started`, with the read-only event), a log with no FAT events (none invented), and a clean mount (no whole-log scan). The boot check skips the previous files after fsck problems and with `FAT_FSCK=0`, and reads them when the volume is dirty-only or clean. It parses fsck-named paths. On a read-only volume (fake mountinfo, read-only log dir), the report and the armed line go to kmsg; a read-write volume gives no such claim. The watcher's new line (clear / no clear / already read-only) is parsed back by the next boot's check. Kernel (`FAT_KERNEL_TEST=1`): a real image whose previous kmsg.log is 64 KB short of its size. The new check leaves the superblock rw with 0 FAT errors and writes fat-check.txt naming `/gbos/logs/boot-3/kmsg.log` and fat-shutdown.txt. The old check (from `23208d6`, the negative control) makes it `ro` with 25 FAT errors (boot-4 had 25), and neither file exists. That is boot-4 exactly. Nine mutants of the new code (8 KB tail, reading anyway, no read-only check, no kmsg fallback, no armed-line log, no read-only event, no "already read-only", old conclusion order) all fail the test. `BUSYBOX=kernel/out/busybox FAT_KERNEL_TEST=1` passes, and so do `sh -n` on the five scripts and the other `tools/test_*.sh` (results in the commit message).
  - *Also exposed, not fixed (updater thread):* `sysupdate.sh su_booted_ok` greps tried boots' whole `kmsg.log` in stage0. A damaged one with no `boot_completed` line before the damage would trip the same read-only FAT.
  - *Deploy:* only `usb/stick_fat.sh` → `gbos/stick_fat.sh`. On the persistent lab stick that file belongs to the installed set, so a hand copy makes `sysupdate.sh audit` report it modified. Use a lab update, or accept that.
  - *Next hardware boot:* `stage0-dmesg.txt` must have no `FAT-fs (sda2): error`, no `Filesystem has been set read-only`, no `READ-ONLY now`, no `(not writable)` / `NOT writable`. If boot-5's shutdown (old script, armed with `clear ... yes`) worked, the next `fat-check.txt` says `previous shutdown clean` and `=> it left the volume read-only and clean`. Otherwise it should name the step's start time and why. After that boot's power-off, its `kmsg.log` should end with `gbos-fat: shutdown: ... remounting /gbos read-only and clearing the dirty flag (end of this log)` or `, leaving the dirty flag as it is (end of this log)`, never with `ALREADY read-only`.

- 2026-11-23 **Boot-5 research fixes (host-tested, not on hardware, not in a lab update yet).**
  - *stage0 stick wait:* once GBOSSYS is found, `stage0_init.sh` stops as soon as USB has settled (no new USB device for `STICK_SETTLE_S`=2 polls, and every usb-storage/uas interface has its disk), still at most 6 s. It used to wait a fixed 6 s, ~5.7 s on every NVMe and persistent-stick boot (boot-5: 3.9 s -> 9.6 s). Expect `USB settled (N devices, no GBOS stick) after 3 s with GBOSSYS`.
  - *busybox copy:* `cp /bin/busybox /gbos/busybox` now happens only when the file differs (`busybox copied to the volume (it changed)`). Boot-5 had 5 s between the mount (9.6 s) and the first log line (14.7 s). The copy is the suspect (2 MB on a `flush` mount), not proven: compare the timestamps on the next boot.
  - *coldplug settle:* `hwdetect.sh gbos_settle_devices` replaces the fixed 1 s after each pass that loaded modules: 0.2 s polls until the modalias count holds twice, at most 1 s (boot-5: 4 passes, ~3 s of sleeps).
  - *power HAL:* vendor `set_power_symlinks` links `/dev/power/irq-by-name/xe` only for the xe driver, so libperfmgr failed 1,374 writes (`XeIrqAffinity`, PROFILE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK) on i915. The hook resolves i915's IRQ in stage0 and adds `on property:init.svc.vendor.set_power_symlinks=stopped` / `symlink /proc/irq/N /dev/power/irq-by-name/xe` (no `$` in the rc). `GPU_IRQ_LINK=0` turns it off. Expect `hook: power HAL: ... -> /proc/irq/N` and no `Failed to write to node: /dev/power/irq-by-name/xe`.
  - *touchpad-inhibitor:* it keeps running (it is what inhibits the detachable keyboard's touchpad); only its log level is set to ERROR in vendor build.prop. 2,142 retry lines in boot-5. Not verified that its logger honours `log.tag.*`.
  - *SELinux labels:* `on early-init` now runs `restorecon_recursive` on /dev/input, /dev/dri and /dev/snd (stage0's mdev left them `tmpfs`; denials for touchpad-inhibitor, the composer, surfaceflinger and system_server, hidden by permissive mode).
  - *updater:* `sysupdate.sh su_booted_ok` no longer greps the tried boots' kmsg.log when this boot's kernel logged `FAT-fs (<dev>): Volume was not properly unmounted` (the boot-4 read-only trap). It relies on the boot-ok file, which is written on every good boot.
  - *deploy_stick.sh:* on a stick with `gbos/system.manifest` it lists what differs and exits 2; `--over-set` keeps the old behaviour. New `tools/test_deploy_stick.sh` (fails on the old script).
  - *Found, not changed:* `usb/optimize.sh` (service `gbos_optimize`) is in no shipped-file list, so it never runs: boot-4 `exited with status 127` after 5 ms. It would turn off zram swap and disable Chrome, TalkBack and other apps, so shipping it needs a decision. The health HAL's 37 `Failed to initialize EC device` errors are expected without a ChromeOS EC (left alone). Boot-3's FAT damage that boot-5's fsck no longer saw is still unexplained.

- 2026-11-23 **Boots 5–7 ended by power-off, not a freeze** (the user: "I powered off"). None of them has the `gbos-fat: shutdown` line, so it was a forced power-off (held button), not Settings > Power off. Every such end leaves the open log files (kmsg.log, logcat.txt) longer in their directory entry than their cluster chain; stage0 then mounts dirty and fsck -n keeps reporting it until it is repaired. Candidate fix: let stick_fat.sh repair this one class automatically (fsck.fat -a when the only problems are "File size is X bytes, cluster chain length is Y" truncations plus the dirty bit), instead of waiting for chkdsk.
- 2026-11-23 **FAT auto-repair of power-off damage (1ba0ea9, host and real-vfat tested, not on hardware, not in a lab update).** `stick_fat.sh gbos_fat_boot_check`: when `fsck.fat -n` finds nothing but "File size is X bytes, cluster chain length is Y bytes." for files matching `/gbos/logs/boot-N/<name>` (size > chain) plus what `gbos_fsck_only_dirty` accepts, `gbos_fat_repair_logs` truncates each one to Y through the mounted volume (truncate(2) does not read past the chain), fsck runs once more, and the boot carries on as dirty-only (previous boot's files read, flag cleared at shutdown). fat-check.txt keeps the fsck output from before the repair; the FAT-CHECK line starts with `repaired N of M log file(s) a forced power-off left longer than their data: ...`. Any other problem, a damaged file elsewhere, or `FAT_REPAIR_LOGS=0`: only reported, as before. Tests: parser on the stick's real boot-8 fsck output plus 5 refusals; real vfat (k3): repaired → `sb=rw errors=0 clear=1` and fsck afterwards has no "File size" line; FAT_REPAIR_LOGS=0 → unchanged and still damaged; the 23208d6 script → read-only (negative control).

- 2026-11-24 **Boot 11: the FAT dirty flag was clear at mount although boot 10's kmsg.log/logcat.txt are unreadable in Windows** (no `Volume was not properly unmounted` in boot 11's stage0 dmesg; fat-check "previous shutdown clean, fsck not needed"; Windows: GBOSSYS "Full Repair Needed", ERROR 1392 on boot-10 and boot-11 kmsg.log and logcat*). A clean flag means boot 10's shutdown step did remount read-only (the remount line goes only to kmsg.log, so fat-shutdown.txt holding just the "armed" line is normal and not proof of a forced power-off). So either the damage happens during a normal shutdown, or Windows objects to something Linux reads without error (boot 11 grepped boot 10's kmsg.log with no FAT-fs error). (3) `chkdsk D:` without /f works without admin ("Access is denied" only for the lock; it still scans) and now reports no problems. *Next:* after the next gbOS86 session (Settings > Power off), run `chkdsk D:` read-only BEFORE opening any log, to get Windows' own reason (size vs chain, cross-link, ...), then compare with Linux's view.

Not seen as a code fault; an NVMe install should not show them (not compared yet). Fix options: a faster UAS stick/SSD for the persistent image, or fewer cold starts after boot.

Boot 21 (102 s) did one idle stop per camera; the rear's took 2.0 s with one `intel_ipu6.isys.40: stream stop time out` in kmsg (the 2 s firmware timeout), and the stop still completed. CameraService only logged normal client disconnects. *Sleep is NOT tested yet:* neither boot has a single `PM: suspend entry`, and boot 20's logcat (21:42–21:51, nearly the whole session) has no screen-off; the screen never went off long enough. *Otherwise clean:* crash.txt empty, no FATAL EXCEPTION, native crash, LMK kill, or kernel I/O/FAT/USB/GPU error; both shutdowns reached the unmount step, and the next mount found the volume clean. ANRs are the known startup ones (`googlequicksearchbox:interactor`, Chrome, Photos "failed to complete startup" in the first seconds after boot_completed) plus one Play Store input timeout (boot 20, 21:43:28). Boot 21's `legacy/0-1 has died` is the bridge's planned restart of the camera provider. *Next:* leave the screen off for a few minutes (or close the lid) and look for `PM: suspend entry` without `isys_suspend ... returns -16`.
- 2026-11-25 **Less disk load around updates and at startup (from the boots 16–21 check; not yet on hardware).** *Staging* (`sysupdate.sh stage`): big files are copied in 32 MB chunks with `dd conv=fsync` (each flushed before the next), clean page cache dropped every 8 chunks and after each big file's hash check, against boot 18's lmkd kills during R5 staging. *Startup check:* stage writes `staged.checked` in the bundle folder (`sha size sha-of-last-MB files/path`, files over 1 MB); the next stage0 skips hashing a file whose size and last MB still match and whose recorded hash is the signed manifest's, only when the volume was cleanly unmounted (otherwise everything is hashed, as before). Aimed at the 70 s R5 check; log line `N big file(s) hashed when staged ... not hashed again`. su_walk accepts the file. *Logs* (DEBUG_LOGS=1): logcat files filter `pixel-thermal:W keystore2:F` (LOGCAT_FULL=1 keeps them); new `gbos_logcat_boot` writes the first 600 s to `logcat-boot.txt` (1 MB × 41, `timeout 600`, oneshot). *Updater app:* `install-app` runs `cmd deviceidle whitelist +org.gbos86.updater` every start (out of the NEVER standby bucket); MainActivity no longer runs the modification audit on open, only on "Check for changes" or right before an install/copy (badge "Not checked" until then). The release APK in `usb/` is NOT rebuilt (needs the maintainer's key). *Freezes (Chrome, launcher):* look again after these are on hardware, once first-boot disk load is lower. *Expected next boots:* `logcat-boot.txt*` in the log dir with no pixel-thermal INFO lines; updater-app.txt `Added: org.gbos86.updater`; after a staged update, stage0 kmsg `hashed when staged` and a much shorter check.

- 2026-11-25 **Updater APK in a main release must point at main's feed (host-tested).** Every APK built so far (lab updates 5–14, `build/updater-app-m3-lab/*`, `build/lab-tree-r5/gbos/`) checks `Stark869/gbos86-lab`, and `tools/release.sh` shipped whatever `gbos86-updater.apk` was in the tree without looking: a main release could have moved every laptop to the lab feed. Now `updater-app/build.sh` defaults the source to `REPO` in `tools/release.conf` (linuxkid473/gbos-x86, the repo release.sh publishes `main` to), not this clone's `origin`; lab builds still pass `REPO=Stark869/gbos86-lab`. Each APK carries `BuildInfo.BUILD_STAMP` = `gbos86-build repo=… tree=<git tree of updater-app/ at HEAD>[-dirty] commit=…`; `tools/apk_stamp.py APK` reads it from the dex. release.sh step **3b** refuses a tree whose APK is missing, has no stamp, checks another repo, or was built from other or uncommitted `updater-app/` sources, and prints the build command. `tools/test_release_app_check.sh` (9 cases with fake APKs; part of release.sh step 4) passes; a real `--test-key` build carries the stamp; the real lab-tree-r5 APK is refused. *Not done:* there is no release keystore on this computer (`~/.config/gbos86/updater-app.p12`), so no main APK is built yet, and the signing cert is not checked (laptops running a lab-key app can't `pm install -r` a release-key app without uninstalling it first).
- 2026-11-26 **AMD built-in microphones (ACP DMIC), issue 39 (Lenovo Yoga Pro 7 14AKP10, Ryzen AI 7 350): kernel config + audio config, host-tested only, NOT on hardware.** The reporter's `hwdetect.txt` (kernel #46) lists the ACP `1022:15e2` under "pci devices without a driver": Google's config had no AMD ASoC driver at all (`SND_AMD_ACP_CONFIG`, `SND_SOC_AMD_ACP_COMMON` unset), so no DMIC card existed; the ALC287 card only has the jack mic. `configure_whl.py` now builds the ACP drivers as modules for Renoir (`snd_rn_pci_acp3x` + `snd_acp3x_rn`, card `acp`), Rembrandt/Phoenix (`snd_pci_acp6x` + `snd_acp6x_mach`, card `acp6x`), ACP 6.3 and ACP 7.x with BIOS flag 0 (`snd_pci_ps` + `snd_ps_mach`, card `acp63`), and ACP 7.x without the `acp-audio-config-flag` ACPI property (`snd_acp_pci` + `snd_acp_legacy_mach`, card `acp-pdm-mach` = id `acppdmmach`); `snd_acp_config` picks one per platform. SoundWire AMD stays off. olddefconfig resolves all of them to `=m` (`--check` OK). `gen_config.sh`: when the main (HDA) card has no DMIC PCM, InternalMic uses the first DMIC capture PCM on a card without HDA codecs (`hw:acppdmmach,0`), the jack mic stays on the HDA card; `test_audio_config.sh` case 3c. *Unknown:* whether dras opens an InternalMic PCM on another card than `SoundCardName` (look for `acppdmmach`/`acp63` in logcat `dras` lines and `audio.txt`), which ACP driver binds on the 14AKP10 (stage0-dmesg `snd_acp_pci`/`snd_pci_ps`), and whether Lenovo needs a DMI quirk for the DMIC (`acp6x-mach` has a table for Rembrandt/Phoenix). Needs a kernel + modules release.
